# Open Tensions

Each tension is an unresolved state. The system works on these. New ones are added when cycles generate them. Tensions are only removed when they resolve into the state layer or are demonstrated to be malformed questions.

---

## T002 — Conservation of computation vs. the origin problem
**Resolved:** 2026-03-12 — See cycle output for details.
**Resolved:** 2026-02-22 — See cycle output for details.
**Opened:** initial
**Description:** Conservation of computation explains why computation persists once started. But it says nothing about why it started. The Big Bang as "the first distinction" is evocative but not explanatory. Every known conservation law describes persistence, not origin. Is the origin of computation a well-formed question or a category error?
**Current status:** Unresolved.

---

## T003 — The consciousness threshold
**Resolved:** 2026-02-22 — See cycle output for details.
**Opened:** initial
**Description:** The framework claims consciousness emerges when information processing becomes "sufficiently integrated and self-referential." But it does not specify the threshold. Without a threshold criterion, the claim is unfalsifiable — everything is a little bit conscious or the line can always be drawn to exclude inconvenient cases. What is the precise threshold condition?
**Current status:** Unresolved. Integrated Information Theory (IIT) attempts this but has known problems.

**T005 — The tick-resolution gap**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Paper 1 (Planckian bound) establishes that the minimum time for local equilibration — the emergence of resolved hydrodynamic behavior — scales as ℏ/T, depends on dimensionality, and is independent of microscopic details. The framework currently conflates the tick (maximum propagation rate) with resolution (states becoming definite). But if resolution requires a temperature-dependent, system-structure-dependent number of ticks, then the tick and resolution are distinct processes with different timescales. The framework needs a theory of how resolution unfolds *across* ticks — a dynamics of resolution itself. Without this, the Tick and Resolution primitives are underspecified in their relationship to each other.
**Why unresolved:** The framework has no internal account of multi-tick resolution dynamics. Adding one requires specifying what happens during the intermediate ticks — are states partially resolved? Is there a continuous resolution parameter? This risks undermining the discrete character of the tick.

---

**T006 — Stable excited states and gradient descent**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Fractional Fermi seas (Paper 3) are experimentally realized stable states that are not energy minima and are not produced by gradient descent. They are created by cyclic driving protocols and stabilized by integrability constraints. The framework's universal equation ẋ = −∇φ(x) does not naturally describe their creation or their stability. Defining a post-hoc potential to accommodate them renders the gradient claim unfalsifiable. This is a concrete instance of the gradient universality problem already noted in the model, now with an experimental example.
**Why unresolved:** The framework either needs to (a) specify what field φ these states are minima of, in a way that is predictive rather than post-hoc, or (b) acknowledge that ẋ = −∇φ(x) is not universal and that some stable states require different dynamical descriptions (e.g., integrable constraints, topological protection).

---

**T007 — Derivability of the cosmological constant**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Paper 2 claims to derive the phenomenological dark energy density from Schwarzian theory and ensemble averaging — from within the computational structure of quantum gravity. The framework claims Λ is an architectural parameter of the substrate, not derivable from within the computation. If Paper 2's derivation holds, either (a) the framework is wrong that Λ is architectural, (b) the framework needs to reinterpret "ensemble averaging over time-reparametrization modes" as a statement about substrate architecture rather than internal computation, or (c) the derivation does not actually work. This tension directly pressures the substrate-architecture claim.
**Why unresolved:** The paper's derivation needs independent verification. If confirmed, the framework must respond substantively. The claim that constants are not internally derivable is central to the framework's epistemic modesty — losing it would require significant restructuring.

---

**T008 — Infinite specification from finite computation**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Paper 1 demonstrates that non-BPS monopoles and dyons require resurgent transseries for their complete physical specification — an infinite tower of exponentially suppressed sectors linked by exact resurgence relations. The framework claims reality is finite computation with conserved computational content. If a single soliton requires infinite specification, either: (a) physical reality truncates the series at some order (but resurgence shows the sectors are mutually dependent), (b) the computational content is actually infinite (violating finiteness), or (c) the algorithmic complexity is finite because all terms are generated from leading-order data (requiring the framework to adopt a Kolmogorov-complexity view of "computation" rather than an extensional one). Option (c) is the most promising but introduces a new distinction the framework doesn't currently make: between the finitely specifiable generating rule and its potentially infinite output. This also raises the question of whether the universe "evaluates" the full series or only computes it to needed precision — which reintroduces a resolution/truncation question at the level of field theory itself.
**Why unresolved:** The framework lacks a theory of how computational content relates to generated mathematical structure. Is a system that is finitely specifiable but infinitely generative "finite computation" or not? This is not merely philosophical — it determines whether Conservation of Computation is a constraint on generating rules or on outputs.

**T009 — Resolution has non-trivial cost structure**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Paper 2 shows that demanding canonical (unique, deterministic) output from quantum computation can be maximally expensive (Ω(N)) even when the underlying problem is classically trivial (O(1)). The framework's Resolution primitive currently describes resolution as an event that "happens" — a state becomes definite. But pseudo-deterministic quantum complexity reveals that *which* definite state emerges, and whether it is canonical, has profound computational cost implications. The framework needs to account for the fact that resolution is not just irreversible state-fixing but a computational process whose cost depends on whether the system is forced to a canonical outcome or merely any consistent outcome. This connects to the measurement problem: what determines which resolution occurs, and at what cost?
**Why unresolved:** The framework has no resource accounting for resolution. It says resolution is irreversible but not what it costs. Incorporating cost structure requires specifying: cost in what currency? Measured by whom? Against what baseline? The pseudo-deterministic complexity results provide a formal framework for this in the query model, but translating query complexity into physical resolution cost is non-trivial.

---

**T010 — The duality problem: which computation is reality?**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** The amplitude/Wilson-loop duality in 𝒩=4 SYM (Paper 1) demonstrates that the same physical observables can be computed from radically different computational architectures — local scattering vertices vs. holonomy integrals over closed loops — in different mathematical spaces. The framework claims "reality is literally computation," but duality means the physical content is invariant under exchange of completely different computational descriptions. If reality *is* computation, it should have a definite computational structure. Duality says the structure is not unique. Either: (a) reality is the equivalence class of dual computations rather than any single computation (but what is an equivalence class ontologically?), (b) there is a preferred computation and the dual description is merely a calculational convenience (but the framework provides no criterion for choosing), or (c) the framework's identification of reality with computation is too literal and needs to be weakened to "reality is *described by* computation" (which significantly dilutes the framework's core claim). This tension is distinct from gauge redundancy because the dual descriptions involve fundamentally different objects and spaces, not just reparameterizations of the same objects.
**Why unresolved:** The framework has no account of duality. Its five primitives describe the dynamics of a single computation but say nothing about whether or why equivalent computational descriptions should exist. Resolving this would require either identifying the invariant structure that all dual descriptions share (which might be the true "computation") or modifying the core claim about reality being computation.

**T011 — Planarity as a missing structural primitive**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Paper 1 shows that factorization of one-loop leading singularities follows from planarity alone in the twistor Wilson loop formalism, without requiring unitarity. In the framework, unitarity corresponds to Conservation of Computation — the most fundamental conservation law. If planarity (a topological property of how the computation is wired) independently produces the same result, then either (a) planarity and unitarity are secretly the same constraint in different guises, (b) there's a deeper constraint from which both follow, or (c) the five primitives are incomplete and a topological/combinatorial primitive is needed. This tension is sharpest in its implication for Conservation of Computation: if the same physical constraints can arise from graph topology without invoking information conservation, then Conservation of Computation may be less fundamental than the framework claims.
**Why unresolved:** Determining whether planarity and unitarity are equivalent constraints, or whether planarity is independent and more primitive, requires a formal analysis that the framework currently cannot perform. This is also currently specific to planar 𝒩=4 SYM and may not generalize, but the existence of even one case where topology does unitarity's work is significant.

**T012 — Pre-resolution structure and the topology of indistinguishability**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Paper 2 demonstrates that engineering specific patterns of path indistinguishability in interwoven frustrated PDC produces specific and different nonclassical signatures (Bell violations, GHZ paradoxes). The framework's Resolution primitive treats pre-resolution states as "unresolved probability distributions," but this is insufficient: the *topology* of which paths are indistinguishable is a structural feature of the pre-resolution state that determines the character of the resolution outcomes. This is not just the quantum state (which encodes amplitudes) but the connectivity pattern of which processes can interfere with which — the indistinguishability graph. The framework needs a theory of pre-resolution topology, not just pre-resolution probability.
**Why unresolved:** The framework has no formalism for describing the topology of indistinguishability. The Tick primitive says states are "unresolved probability distributions between ticks," but this conflates the amplitude information with the structural information about which computational paths are connected. Developing this formalism would require specifying how the indistinguishability graph relates to the other primitives and whether it should be considered part of the Field, a feature of the Tick, or something new.

---

**T013 — Time dimensionality as unexplained primitive**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** The framework treats the Tick as its most fundamental primitive, and the singularity of the time direction is built into the Tick's definition. Paper 1 demonstrates that the positive mass theorem — a foundational result connecting energy positivity to gravitational stability — generalizes naturally to multiple time dimensions. The mathematical structures of general relativity do not require one time dimension. The framework provides no mechanism for explaining why the substrate implements exactly one tick direction rather than several. This is distinct from T001 (the substrate problem) because T001 concerns the *values* of constants, while T013 concerns the *dimensionality* of the most basic primitive. The framework never flagged time dimensionality as an architectural parameter — it treated single-time as definitional. But the mathematics doesn't force this.
**Why unresolved:** The framework has no resources to address this. Arguing from anthropic reasoning (multi-time physics is pathological for observers) would work but is external to the framework. Arguing from the framework's own structure is circular because the Tick's singularity is axiomatic. The framework needs either to derive single-time from deeper principles or to honestly acknowledge this as a brute architectural fact alongside the constant values.

**T014 — Permanently virtual computational structure**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Paper 2 shows that ghost modes in quadratic gravity can be quantized such that they never appear as free asymptotic states — their creation/annihilation operators commute, so they propagate only virtually. The framework's Resolution primitive assumes all computational states are at least potentially resolvable. Permanently virtual structure — computation that participates in intermediate processes but is architecturally forbidden from resolving — is a new category. The ghost modes' commuting statistics ([a, a†] = 0) are neither bosonic nor fermionic, meaning they don't fit the framework's existing categories of resolved states. Conservation of Computation (unitarity) is preserved precisely *because* these modes never resolve, but the framework has no account of what permanent virtuality means ontologically. Is permanently virtual structure "real" computation? Does it carry computational content that must be conserved? Or is it bookkeeping that the substrate uses internally but that has no resolution-level reality?
**Why unresolved:** The framework identifies reality with computation and computation with states that resolve. Structure that computes but never resolves challenges this identification. If virtual structure is "real," the framework's ontology is broader than resolution. If virtual structure is not "real," then Conservation of Computation doesn't apply to it and the framework needs to specify which computation is conserved and which is not. Either option requires significant conceptual work.

**T015 — Resolution dynamics are not determined by attractor structure alone**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Paper 3 demonstrates that defect production during quenches can decouple from the equilibrium critical properties of the attractor landscape — KZM scaling can fail at critical points and persist at non-critical points. The framework's Attractor primitive describes the topology of potential landscapes, and the Resolution primitive describes states becoming definite, but the framework has no independent theory of how resolution dynamics unfold during attractor landscape restructuring. The static attractor geometry does not uniquely determine the dynamical resolution process. This is related to T005 (the tick-resolution gap) but is more specific: T005 notes that resolution is multi-tick; T015 notes that the multi-tick resolution dynamics are not derivable from the equilibrium attractor properties and require additional dynamical information (quench protocol, system topology, dimensionality).
**Why unresolved:** The framework would need to develop a dynamical theory of resolution — how states evolve during the multi-tick resolution process, and what determines the defect density produced by landscape restructuring beyond the critical exponents. This is a significant theoretical gap: the framework currently treats resolution as an event, not a process with its own dynamics.

---

**T016 — Geometric encoding vs. computational ontology**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Paper 1 (The Universe Fan) demonstrates that the cosmological wavefunction is determined by a polyhedral fan — a static combinatorial-geometric object defined by positivity conditions on a lattice. The physical content (singularity structure, factorization, residues = matroid amplitudes) is encoded in the face lattice and cone structure without invoking temporal evolution or sequential computation. The framework claims "reality is literally computation," but the universe fan suggests the physical content may be more naturally understood as geometry than as computation. This is related to but distinct from T010 (which concerns dual computational descriptions) and T011 (which concerns planarity as a structural constraint). T016 concerns a more fundamental issue: whether the ontological category "computation" is the right one, or whether "geometric structure defined by positivity/causality" is a competing ontological category that the framework has no grounds for dismissing. The cosmological polytopes of Arkani-Hamed et al., the amplituhedron, and now the universe fan all point toward physics being encoded in geometric objects whose structure is defined by positivity rather than by dynamical evolution.
**Why unresolved:** The framework could attempt to absorb this by arguing that geometric structure *is* a form of computation, but this risks trivializing the computational claim. Alternatively, it could argue that the geometric encoding is a mathematical convenience while the physical reality is computational, but it has no criterion for distinguishing "mathematical convenience" from "ontological ground." The tension cannot be resolved without either (a) a principled argument for why computation is ontologically privileged over geometry, or (b) a reconceptualization of the framework's core claim to accommodate geometric structure as foundational.

**T017 — Criticality as continuous sharpening vs. resolution as discrete event**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Paper 2 establishes that phase transition criticality exists as smooth morphological precursors (inflection points, extrema) at every finite system size, with the thermodynamic singularity being the asymptotic limit of a continuous sharpening process. The framework treats resolution as discrete and irreversible and treats phase transitions as cases of attractor landscape restructuring. But if criticality is inherently continuous and multi-scale — with no sharp "event" at finite size — then the framework's discrete Resolution primitive maps poorly onto this phenomenon. Either resolution itself admits degrees (partial resolution, continuous sharpening), which undermines its discrete character, or phase transitions are not truly instances of resolution, which weakens the framework's claim to cross-domain unification. This tension connects to T005 (tick-resolution gap) and T015 (resolution dynamics not determined by attractor structure) but is distinct: T005 is about the temporal gap between ticks and resolution; T017 is about whether resolution is fundamentally discrete or admits a continuous precursor structure at finite scales.
**Why unresolved:** The framework would need to decide whether to (a) introduce a concept of "partial resolution" or "resolution sharpness" that varies with system size, fundamentally changing the Resolution primitive; (b) maintain strict discrete resolution and argue that finite-size pseudocritical features are not resolution events; or (c) accept that the Resolution primitive captures only the idealized thermodynamic limit and is not a description of finite-size physics. Option (c) would significantly limit the primitive's scope.

---

**T018 — The algebra of computation**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Papers 1 and 2 jointly reveal that computation has rich algebraic structure — decomposition into master integrals via IBP, hierarchical organization of quantum gates via Pauli periodicity — that is not captured by any of the five primitives. The framework describes the *dynamics* of computation (propagation, gradient descent, attractor capture, resolution, conservation) but not its *algebra* (how computational operations compose, decompose, and organize into hierarchies). IBP relations show that the space of field-theoretic computations has a finite-dimensional basis; the Clifford hierarchy shows that quantum operations form a rigid infinite hierarchy with exponential cost barriers between levels. These are fundamental organizational features of computation that the framework ignores. This is distinct from T010 (duality) and T011 (planarity) because it concerns not alternative descriptions of the same physics or topological constraints, but the internal compositional and hierarchical structure of the computational operations themselves.
**Why unresolved:** The framework would need either (a) a sixth primitive describing algebraic/compositional structure, (b) a demonstration that algebraic structure emerges from the existing five primitives in a non-trivial way, or (c) an argument for why algebraic structure is not ontologically fundamental but merely a mathematical convenience. None of these has been attempted.

**T019 — Resolution precision hierarchy**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** The Clifford hierarchy (Paper 2) reveals that resolution is not a single undifferentiated operation but admits a hierarchy of precision levels, with each level enabling qualitatively different computational capabilities and requiring exponentially greater resources to access. Level 1 (Pauli measurements) represents the coarsest resolution; each subsequent level enables finer angular precision in state manipulation. The framework's Resolution primitive treats resolution as binary (unresolved → definite) and makes no distinction between different kinds or precisions of resolution. The controlled-jump rule shows that climbing the resolution hierarchy by one level requires coherent control and that the target-qubit cost grows exponentially with the desired level. This connects to T009 (resolution cost structure) but is more specific: T009 notes that resolution has costs; T019 asserts that resolution has a hierarchically organized internal structure with quantifiable resource requirements at each level.
**Why unresolved:** The framework would need to either (a) incorporate a notion of "resolution depth" or "resolution precision" into the Resolution primitive, specifying that resolution comes in levels with different costs and capabilities, or (b) argue that the Clifford hierarchy is a feature of quantum computational models that does not map onto physical resolution. Option (b) seems difficult given that the Clifford hierarchy directly governs what measurements and transformations are physically implementable.

**T020 — Dark matter coupling is quantitative, not categorical**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Paper 3 demonstrates that a viable dark matter candidate (ALPs) couples to electromagnetism with observable astrophysical consequences while remaining consistent with all current bounds. The framework claimed dark matter "couples to mass-density gradients but not electromagnetic gradients." This is directly contradicted by the ALP scenario, where the axion-photon coupling aFF̃ is the mechanism generating cosmological magnetic fields and Lyman-Werner photons. The distinction between dark matter's gravitational and electromagnetic behavior is quantitative (the electromagnetic coupling is weak, not zero), and the framework drew a categorical line where none exists.
**Why unresolved:** The framework needs to either (a) explain why its "partial coupling" description should be understood as approximate rather than exact, and specify what determines the coupling ratio as an architectural parameter, or (b) abandon the claim that dark matter's electromagnetic invisibility is categorical and instead develop a theory of coupling strengths as properties of computational structure.

---

**T021 — Arithmetic-geometric unity and the poverty of "computation"**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Paper 1 demonstrates that the zeta function of a Calabi-Yau variety encodes topological invariants (via Weil conjectures) through arithmetic over finite fields, with the connection mediated by cohomology (Monsky-Washnitzer) and geometric operations (crepant resolution). This instantiates a pattern seen throughout modern mathematical physics: the same invariant content is accessible through categorically different mathematical frameworks — arithmetic, algebraic geometry, topology, combinatorics — that are provably equivalent but conceptually distinct. The framework claims reality is "literally computation," privileging one mathematical category as ontologically fundamental. But arithmetic-geometric correspondences (Weil conjectures, Langlands program, mirror symmetry) suggest that the deep structure of mathematical physics lives in the *relationships between* categories, not in any single category. The framework provides no justification for privileging computation over arithmetic, geometry, or topology, and the existence of categorical equivalences suggests that the invariant content — which is what physics cares about — is not inherently computational.
**Why unresolved:** This is deeper than T010 (duality between computational descriptions) or T016 (geometric encoding vs. computational ontology). Those tensions concern specific alternative descriptions of specific physical systems. T021 concerns the observation that the entire edifice of mathematical physics is characterized by inter-categorical equivalences, and asks whether "computation" names anything ontologically distinctive or is merely one of many equivalent windows into the same structure. Resolving this would require either (a) demonstrating that computation is the unique category from which all others can be derived (which would be an extraordinary mathematical claim), (b) identifying what specific feature of computation earns it ontological privilege, or (c) abandoning the claim that reality is literally computation in favor of a more category-neutral ontological claim.

**T022 — Spectral universality as a constraint the framework cannot derive**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Paper 2 derives a universal relationship between temporal and spatial spectral indices for Lorentz-homogeneous stochastic fields, with the offset determined purely by spacetime dimensionality and Lorentz symmetry. This is a structural constraint on the statistical properties of fields that follows from symmetry alone. The framework's Field primitive describes dynamics (ẋ = −∇φ) but not the statistical/spectral properties of fields. The Tick primitive posits discrete updates and finite propagation speed, which should in principle constrain spectral structure, but the framework cannot derive the specific universal offset or the no-go theorem for local spectral mappings. More fundamentally, the result shows that Lorentz symmetry — which in the framework is a consequence of the tick rate being the maximum propagation speed — has non-trivial implications for the spectral structure of stochastic fields that the framework never anticipated and currently cannot reproduce. If the framework's "tick rate = speed of light" is correct, it should be possible to derive these spectral constraints from the discrete tick structure, but the framework lacks the formalism to do so.
**Why unresolved:** The framework would need to develop a theory of stochastic fields on its discrete substrate and show that the spectral universality emerges from the tick structure + Lorentz invariance. This is in principle possible but has not been attempted. Furthermore, the no-go result (no covariant local spectral mapping in d > 1) might constrain the framework's Field primitive: if temporal and spatial field structures cannot be locally mapped, the framework's treatment of fields as having unified gradients ∇φ that combine spatial and temporal derivatives may be too naive.

**T023 — Kinetic arrest as a distinct dynamical category**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Paper 3 demonstrates that a disorder-free, short-range interacting system can exhibit dynamical arrest with exponentially long-lived athermal plateaus. The arrest mechanism — composite excitations creating kinetic barriers in an enlarged Hilbert space — represents a dynamical phenomenon that is distinct from both attractor capture (the system is not in a minimum) and resolution dynamics (the system is classical, so quantum resolution is not involved). The framework currently has three categories for stable states: energy attractors, constrained attractors, and driven attractors. Kinetically arrested states don't fit cleanly into any of these: they are not true attractors (the system would eventually relax), they are not constrained by conservation laws or integrability (the ice rules are not symmetries in the traditional sense), and they are not driven (no external energy input). They represent a fourth category: states that are metastable for exponentially long times due to the structure of the dynamical landscape (barriers in configuration space) rather than the structure of the energy landscape (minima). The framework's gradient equation ẋ = −∇φ(x) would describe the system as slowly rolling down a gradient, but the exponential timescale is set by barrier structure, not gradient magnitude, and the framework has no account of barrier-dominated dynamics as distinct from gradient-dominated dynamics.
**Why unresolved:** The framework's "everything moves down gradients" claim technically accommodates exponentially slow motion (small gradients), but this misses the physical content: the dynamics are barrier-dominated, not gradient-dominated. The exponential timescale comes from the Arrhenius-like need for activated processes, which is qualitatively different from slow gradient descent. The framework would need to distinguish between gradient-dominated dynamics (smooth descent) and barrier-dominated dynamics (arrest punctuated by activated transitions) as physically distinct regimes, which the universal gradient equation does not do.

---

**T024 — The temporal-spatial spectral gap**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** The proven impossibility of a covariant local mapping between temporal and spatial power spectra in d > 1 (Tevzadze 2025) creates a specific structural problem for the framework. The Tick primitive governs temporal structure; the Field primitive governs spatial structure via gradients. The no-go theorem says these two structures are related only non-locally and through a dimensionality-dependent universal offset. The framework's architecture — Tick for time, Field with unified ∇φ for dynamics — implicitly assumes that temporal and spatial field properties are locally related through the gradient. The no-go theorem says they are not. This is distinct from T022 (which noted the framework cannot derive spectral universality) because T024 concerns not a derivation failure but a potential structural incompatibility: the framework's primitive decomposition may be fundamentally unable to express the temporal-spatial spectral relationship correctly, not merely unable to derive it.
**Why unresolved:** Resolving this would require either (a) showing that the framework's discrete tick structure, when properly formalized with stochastic fields on the lattice, naturally reproduces the non-local temporal-spatial spectral relationship and the no-go theorem, or (b) restructuring the Tick-Field decomposition to accommodate the fundamental non-locality of the temporal-spatial spectral connection. Neither has been attempted, and (a) requires formalism the framework does not possess.

**T025 — Composite excitations and Hilbert space enlargement as emergent barrier structure**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Spin-3/2 ice demonstrates that enlarging the local Hilbert space (from spin-1/2 to spin-3/2) creates qualitatively new composite excitations (monopole + crystal-field bound states) that generate kinetic barriers absent in the smaller Hilbert space. The framework treats attractor basins and their transitions but has no account of how the *dimensionality of the local computational state space* affects the topology of barriers between attractors. The gradient equation ẍ = −∇φ(x) operates on a fixed configuration space; it has no mechanism for describing how enlarging the space of possible local states creates new barrier structures. This is distinct from T023 (which noted kinetic arrest as a dynamical category) because T025 concerns the specific mechanism: the barrier structure is not present in the energy landscape but emerges from the composite nature of excitations in the enlarged Hilbert space. The framework has no primitive that describes how local computational dimensionality shapes global dynamical topology.
**Why unresolved:** The framework would need to develop an account of how the dimensionality and structure of local computational state spaces affect the topology of the configuration-space landscape, including barrier creation by composite excitations. This requires going beyond the gradient equation to a theory of how computation is *organized* at the local level — connecting to T018 (algebra of computation) but focused on the dynamical consequences of local algebraic structure rather than the algebraic structure itself.

---

**T026 — The structural content gap**
**Description:** Multiple independent tensions (T010 duality, T011 planarity, T016 geometric encoding, T018 algebra of computation, T021 arithmetic-geometric unity, T022 spectral universality, T024 temporal-spatial spectral gap) are converging on a single meta-problem: the framework's five primitives are all *dynamical* — they describe how computation propagates (Tick), how things move (Field), where things settle (Attractor), when things become definite (Resolution), and what is conserved (Conservation). But physics also has *structural* content: categorical equivalences (arithmetic ↔ geometry ↔ topology), spectral universalities (Lorentz-covariant spectral offsets), algebraic hierarchies (Clifford hierarchy, IBP decomposition), and topological constraints (planarity, indistinguishability graphs). The framework has no primitive that captures structural content. This is not the same as adding a sixth primitive — it may require reconceiving the framework's ontological basis. If reality has both dynamical and structural aspects, and the framework captures only the dynamical, it is at best half a theory.
**Why unresolved:** This is a meta-tension that subsumes several existing tensions but is larger than any of them. Resolving it would require either (a) demonstrating that structural content emerges from the five dynamical primitives (which seems unlikely given that the no-go theorem for spectral mappings is a structural result the primitives cannot even express), (b) adding structural primitives to the framework (which would be a major extension), or (c) reconceiving the framework's ontological basis so that structure and dynamics are unified at a deeper level. None of these has been attempted.

**T027 — Cohomological mediation and the framework's silence on mathematical depth**
**Description:** Paper 1 demonstrates that the connection between arithmetic (zeta functions over finite fields) and topology (Betti numbers, Hodge structure) of Calabi-Yau varieties is mediated by cohomology (specifically Monsky-Washnitzer p-adic cohomology). Cohomological theories are the standard mediators between categorically different mathematical descriptions of the same structure throughout modern physics (de Rham cohomology between differential and topological, sheaf cohomology between algebraic and topological, BRST cohomology between gauge-theoretic and physical). The framework has no account of why cohomological structures should play this mediating role, no primitive that corresponds to cohomological content, and no mechanism for explaining the existence of cohomological equivalences. This is distinct from T021 (which notes the existence of inter-categorical correspondences) because T027 asks specifically about the *mechanism* of mediation — why cohomology is the universal translator between mathematical categories — and whether the framework's silence on this indicates a fundamental blind spot.
**Why unresolved:** The framework would need to either explain cohomological structure as emerging from its computational ontology or acknowledge that the mathematical depth of physics includes a cohomological layer that the framework cannot currently address. This is potentially the deepest version of the ontological pluralism problem: not just "which category is reality?" but "what makes categories equivalent, and why does physics care?"

---

**T028 — Resolution is contextual and computationally intractable**
**Description:** Paper 1 proves that satisfiability over Kochen-Specker partial Boolean algebras is NP-complete, satisfiability over projectors on physical Hilbert spaces is ∃ℝ-complete, and satisfiability over all Hilbert spaces is undecidable. The framework's Resolution primitive treats resolution as a process that "happens" — an unresolved state becomes definite. But these results show that determining whether a globally consistent resolution *can* exist is computationally intractable or impossible. The framework must confront: (a) resolution is inherently contextual (locally consistent, globally incompatible), meaning the "accumulated ledger of resolved states" is a contextual patchwork, not a consistent history; (b) the substrate either has super-Turing computational power or avoids the undecidable configurations by some unspecified mechanism; (c) the ∃ℝ-completeness of the physical-dimension case means resolution consistency lives in a specific complexity class between NP and PSPACE that involves real algebraic geometry, connecting the discrete Resolution primitive to continuous geometric structure in a way the framework does not anticipate. This is related to T009 (resolution cost), T012 (pre-resolution topology), and T019 (resolution precision hierarchy) but is more fundamental: it concerns not the cost or structure of resolution but whether globally consistent resolution is even *possible* in general.
**Why unresolved:** The framework has no account of contextuality. It does not distinguish between local and global resolution, does not address the computational complexity of resolution consistency, and does not specify whether the substrate performs computations beyond the Turing-computable. The undecidability result is especially sharp: if the substrate's computational architecture is finite (as the framework claims via finite tick rate and minimum resolution scale), then it cannot solve undecidable problems, and resolution over arbitrary Hilbert spaces cannot be guaranteed to succeed. Either the physical Hilbert space is always finite-dimensional (avoiding undecidability but retaining ∃ℝ-hardness) or the framework's finiteness claims need revision.

**T029 — Anomalous transport universality classes are invisible to gradient dynamics**
**Description:** Paper 2 derives exact M-Wright function fluctuations in quantum many-body transport, representing a universal anomalous transport class (superdiffusion with t^{3/4} scaling) that emerges from integrability and conservation law structure. The framework's gradient equation ẋ = −∇φ(x) cannot distinguish between systems in different transport universality classes — diffusive, KPZ, M-Wright, ballistic — because the equation describes only mean drift, not fluctuation statistics. The universality class is determined by the algebraic structure of conserved charges and the integrability of the system, neither of which appears in any of the five primitives. This is distinct from T006 (stable excited states not produced by gradient descent) because T006 concerns the *endpoints* of dynamics (where things settle) while T029 concerns the *fluctuation statistics* during dynamics (how things move). It is also distinct from T018 (algebra of computation) because T018 concerns the organizational structure of computational operations while T029 concerns the physical consequences of algebraic conservation structure for observable transport statistics. The fact that the M-Wright class is identical in classical and quantum systems (classical automata and quantum Fermi-Hubbard) means it is a structural feature independent of the resolution status of the system, further weakening the framework's ability to account for it through any primitive that depends on the classical/quantum distinction.
**Why unresolved:** The framework would need to either (a) extend the Field primitive to include transport universality class as a property of fields alongside their gradient structure, (b) derive transport universality classes from the existing primitives (which seems impossible since the gradient equation alone cannot distinguish them), or (c) add a new primitive or structural element capturing conservation law algebraic structure. The classical-quantum universality of M-Wright further suggests that this transport structure is more fundamental than the Tick-Resolution distinction.

**T030 — Boundary conditions as attractor selectors**
**Description:** Paper 3 demonstrates that domain wall boundary conditions on a 3D Coulomb phase partially lift the extensive ground-state degeneracy, inducing long-range order in the thermodynamic limit while preserving Coulomb-phase fluctuations. The framework's Attractor primitive describes attractor basins and their restructuring but treats boundary conditions as external specifications, not as dynamical elements. Paper 3 shows that boundary conditions are not merely initial/boundary data but active selectors that reshape the attractor landscape — turning an extensively degenerate constrained attractor into a partially ordered state with emergent geometric boundaries. The framework has no account of how boundary information propagates into and restructures the interior attractor topology. This connects to T012 (pre-resolution topology) in that boundary conditions define a global constraint that determines local structure, and to T015 (resolution dynamics not determined by attractor structure alone) in that the attractor structure alone (the ice rules) does not determine the ordered state — the boundary conditions are essential.
**Why unresolved:** The framework would need to specify how boundary conditions interact with the Attractor primitive — whether they are part of the Field, part of the substrate architecture, or a separate structural input. The emergence of geometric order from boundary + topological constraints requires understanding boundary conditions as computational inputs that shape the attractor landscape, which the framework currently does not formalize.

---

**T031 — The substrate is not fixed: computational architecture as dynamical field**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** The IIA/IIB domain wall (Torres 2025) places two fundamentally different computational architectures — 2D SYM (IIA) and 3D ABJM (IIB) — in a single physical spacetime, connected by a lightlike wall where the string coupling vanishes. The framework's foundational assumption is that reality runs on a computational substrate with fixed architectural parameters. But this construction shows the *type* of computation (the matrix theory, the gauge group, the dimensionality of the effective theory) varying across space, with the coupling constant being a dynamical field that reaches zero at the domain wall. The framework has no primitive for describing transitions between different computational architectures. This is not duality (T010), where two descriptions are equivalent — it is a physical boundary between genuinely different computational structures within one spacetime. The BPS → non-BPS transition of D0-branes crossing the wall means attractor structure (BPS states are stable minima) depends on which computational architecture is locally realized, and objects can move between architectures. This undermines the substrate/computation separation, which is the framework's foundational ontological distinction.
**Why unresolved:** The framework would need to either (a) promote the computational architecture to a dynamical field (a "meta-field" whose value determines which computational rules apply locally), which would require a primitive beyond the current five and would raise infinite-regress questions (what substrate does the meta-field run on?), (b) argue that the IIA/IIB wall is a string theory artifact without physical significance (but DLCQ is a non-perturbative construction, and the wall has physical consequences like BPS → non-BPS transitions), or (c) abandon the substrate/computation distinction in favor of a framework where "what kind of computation" is itself part of the dynamical content. Option (c) is the most radical and would require fundamental restructuring.

**T032 — Symmetry as a structural primitive the framework lacks**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Paper 2 demonstrates that the robustness of superhorizon curvature perturbations against one-loop corrections is guaranteed by dilatation symmetry of the effective theory. This is a physically crucial result — it ensures that primordial perturbations survive to the CMB — and it is derived entirely from symmetry structure. The framework's five primitives (Tick, Field, Attractor, Resolution, Conservation) do not include symmetry as a fundamental element. Conservation of Computation is related to symmetry via Noether's theorem, but the inflationary cancellation uses dilatation symmetry, which is a *spacetime* symmetry of the background, not a conservation law in the conventional sense. The specific structure required — a tadpole counterterm that respects the dilatation symmetry — has no expression in any of the primitives. This connects to and sharpens T026 (structural content gap) and T018 (algebra of computation): symmetry is the most pervasive structural element in physics, and the framework treats it as either derived (from conservation) or ignored. The inflationary result shows it plays a protective, structural role that goes beyond conservation.
**Why unresolved:** The framework would need to either (a) demonstrate that all physically relevant symmetries emerge from the five primitives (which seems impossible for spacetime symmetries like dilatation that are properties of specific backgrounds, not universal features), (b) add a symmetry primitive, which would be a sixth primitive and a major structural change, or (c) subsume symmetry under the structural content that T026 already flags as missing. Option (c) is the most natural but means the structural content gap is even larger than previously noted.

---

**T033 — Analyticity bridges IR and UV, eroding the substrate/computation boundary**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Takaura & Yin (2603.03277) demonstrate that UV physics (beta function sign, dynamical scale ΛQCD) can be reconstructed from IR expansion coefficients below all mass thresholds using analyticity and the absence of massless singularities. The framework claims substrate-level (UV) parameters are not derivable from within the computation (IR). The reconstruction procedure — inverse Laplace transform + coarse-graining — is a purely IR computation that recovers UV information, directly contradicting the framework's substrate opacity claim. Analyticity is the key structural assumption enabling this bridge, and the framework has no primitive that captures analyticity. The framework must either (a) specify which UV quantities are truly substrate-level (inaccessible) vs. merely high-energy (recoverable through analytic continuation), developing a criterion it currently lacks, or (b) abandon the substrate opacity claim entirely, which removes the framework's most distinctive epistemological feature. This tension is distinct from T007 (derivability of Λ from ensemble averaging) because T007 concerns whether specific constants can be derived from internal symmetry arguments, while T033 concerns a general analytical procedure that systematically reconstructs UV from IR. It is also distinct from T031 (substrate as dynamical field) because T031 concerns spatial variation of the substrate, while T033 concerns informational leakage across scales within a fixed substrate.
**Why unresolved:** The framework has no theory of analyticity — it is not a consequence of any primitive. The framework cannot specify which UV quantities are reconstructible and which are not, because it has no criterion for distinguishing "architectural parameters" from "high-energy dynamical features accessible through analytic continuation." Without such a criterion, the substrate concept is either vacuous (everything is substrate) or porous (substrate information leaks through analyticity), neither of which is satisfactory.

**T034 — Scale-dependent information topology: a single state has no fixed informational character**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Sokolovs (2603.03103) proves that monogamy of mutual information in free-fermion ground states is scale-dependent: the same state violates MMI at short scales (k_F w < z*) and satisfies it at long scales (k_F w > z*), with z* ≈ 1.329 a universal constant from the Slepian spectrum. The framework's Resolution primitive says that resolved states have definite properties. The Attractor primitive says that states in attractor basins have characteristic features. But the holographic/non-holographic classification — an informational property with deep connections to spacetime structure via AdS/CFT — is not a property of the state at all. It depends on the observation scale. The framework has no primitive or mechanism for describing how informational properties of a *single fixed state* change with the scale of examination. This is not the same as resolution being multi-scale (T005, T017): the state is fully resolved at all scales, but its informational character (MMI sign) varies. The universal constant z* is determined by the spectral theory of the sine kernel, which is pure mathematical structure — not dynamics, not conservation, not resolution — and the framework has no way to derive or accommodate such constants. This tension connects to T022 (spectral universality) and T026 (structural content gap) but is distinct because it concerns informational, not spectral, properties and because the scale dependence applies to a fixed resolved state rather than to field dynamics.
**Why unresolved:** The framework treats resolved states as having determinate properties. If an informational property as fundamental as MMI (which in holographic contexts distinguishes spacetime geometries with different topologies) is scale-dependent rather than state-dependent, the framework needs a theory of scale-dependent information that it currently lacks entirely. Furthermore, the universal constant z* from Slepian spectral theory is a structural mathematical fact with direct physical consequences, and the framework has no account of how such mathematical constants enter physics.

---

**T035 — Conservation of Computation is observer-dependent**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Trivedi, Gurrola & Scherrer (2603.04375) demonstrate that Hermiticity (and therefore unitarity) is a symmetry law arising from global conservation of an inner product current, not a foundational axiom. In the presence of causal horizons, this conservation is obstructed, and restricted observers experience genuinely non-unitary (CPTP) dynamics. The framework's Conservation of Computation primitive states that "computation cannot be created or destroyed, only transformed" — an absolute, observer-independent law. If unitarity is the framework's "most fundamental expression" of this law, and unitarity is observer-dependent (holding globally but broken for horizon-bounded observers), then Conservation of Computation as stated is either (a) true only globally and inaccessible to any finite observer, or (b) needs reformulation as a balance law where computational loss through horizons is compensated by entropy production at the horizon surface, with the Bianchi identity and Raychaudhuri equation providing the geometric enforcement. Option (b) would mean conservation is enforced by geometric structure, not by computation itself — the geometry *guarantees* the balance, and the computation merely *participates* in it. This inverts the framework's ontological priority (computation first, geometry emergent) and aligns with T026/T032's observation that structural/geometric content is missing from the primitives. The paper's proposal that black hole ringdown observations could bound the strength of inner product flux means this tension is empirically accessible, not just philosophical.
**Why unresolved:** The framework cannot accommodate observer-dependent conservation without major restructuring. If Conservation of Computation is weakened to "holds globally," it becomes unfalsifiable (no observer can verify the global balance). If reformulated as a horizon balance law, the enforcement mechanism is geometric (Raychaudhuri + Bianchi), not computational, undermining the framework's computational ontology. If Conservation of Computation is conditional on causal structure, a theory of causal structure must precede the conservation law, but the framework currently derives causal structure (light cones, horizons) from the Tick primitive and derives conservation independently — they are not ordered. The framework needs a causal-conservation coupling it does not have.

**T036 — Multi-signature computation: physical results require passage through disallowed signatures**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Guevara et al. (2603.04330) show that single-minus graviton tree amplitudes, previously presumed to vanish in Lorentzian signature, are nonzero in Klein space (signature (2,2)) or for complexified momenta, and contribute to physical results via analytic continuation. The $\mathcal{L}w_{1+\infty}$ Ward identity that generates these amplitudes operates in the complexified/Klein kinematic space. The framework's Tick primitive assumes one time dimension, and all five primitives are formulated for Lorentzian signature. If physically relevant computation passes through Klein signature as an intermediate step — even if the inputs and outputs are Lorentzian — the framework's computational substrate must either (a) natively support multiple signatures (contradicting the single-Tick axiom), (b) support complexified kinematics (which have no clear interpretation in terms of discrete ticks updating real physical states), or (c) treat the Klein/complex intermediate as a mathematical convenience with no ontological status (but the framework claims reality *is* computation, so if the computation goes through Klein space, that's where reality is during that step). This tension is related to T013 (time dimensionality) but sharper: T013 notes that the number of time dimensions is unexplained; T036 notes that physical graviton amplitudes *require* multi-time intermediate computation, making the single-time axiom not just unexplained but functionally incomplete.
**Why unresolved:** The framework has no concept of "intermediate computational signature." Analytic continuation between spacetime signatures is a standard technique in mathematical physics but has no interpretation in the framework's computational ontology. If the substrate computes in discrete ticks (one time direction), what does it mean for an amplitude to be nonzero only when there are two time directions? Either the substrate has richer signature structure than the framework admits, or the amplitude calculation is a mathematical representation that does not map directly onto the substrate's computation — but the latter option undermines the "reality is literally computation" claim.

**T037 — Hermiticity as emergent symmetry inverts the framework's ontological ordering**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Paper 2 argues that Hermiticity is not a foundational axiom but an emergent symmetry law — the special case of globally conserved inner product current. The framework treats the real-valuedness of physical observables and the unitarity of evolution as consequences of Conservation of Computation (a primitive). If Hermiticity is instead emergent from geometric/causal structure (requiring complete Cauchy surfaces), then the framework's ordering is inverted: conservation is not primitive but derived from spacetime geometry. The paper shows that the Bianchi identity (a geometric identity) and the Raychaudhuri equation (a geometric focusing theorem) together enforce the entropy-information balance, meaning *geometry guarantees conservation*, not the other way around. This directly challenges the framework's claim that geometry (spacetime structure, curvature) emerges from the computational substrate. If conservation emerges from geometry rather than geometry emerging from computation, the framework's explanatory direction is backwards for this phenomenon. Combined with the existing challenge from T016 (geometric encoding vs. computational ontology) and T026 (structural content gap), this forms a pattern: geometric structure keeps appearing as *prior to* or *independent of* computational structure, contrary to the framework's ontological ordering.
**Why unresolved:** The framework would need to either (a) demonstrate that the Bianchi identity and Raychaudhuri equation themselves emerge from the computational substrate in a way that the inner product conservation then follows (preserving the framework's ordering but requiring substantial new derivations), (b) accept that geometry and computation are co-fundamental rather than computation being prior (weakening the core ontological claim), or (c) argue that the geometric enforcement of conservation is itself a form of computation (which risks trivializing "computation" into meaninglessness, as flagged by T021). None of these has been attempted.

---

**T038 — Field-specific substrate resilience: singularities are not universal**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Paez, Fiorini & Hernández (2603.05285) demonstrate exact, regular electromagnetic solutions with bounded power flux through a strong curvature naked singularity. The framework's unified-substrate assumption implies that a singularity — where curvature gradients diverge — should disrupt all computation at that spacetime point. But electromagnetism survives where gravity fails. This means either (a) the substrate is intact and the curvature singularity is a pathology of the gravitational field only, not the substrate; (b) the substrate is singular but different fields have different resilience thresholds; or (c) the framework's identification of curvature singularities with substrate breakdowns is wrong. Option (a) requires explaining how a field can diverge on an intact substrate — what does it mean for one field's gradient to be infinite while the substrate's computational capacity is finite? Option (b) requires a theory of field-specific substrate coupling that the framework does not have. Option (c) would require a new account of what singularities are in computational terms. This tension is related to T004/T020 (dark matter partial coupling) and T031 (substrate as dynamical field) but is distinct: T004/T020 concern coupling *strengths* to different gradients; T031 concerns spatial variation of the computational architecture; T038 concerns the survival of computation through points where the substrate is ostensibly broken. It is the first tension that directly challenges the framework's assumption that the substrate's computational integrity is either intact or broken for all fields simultaneously.
**Why unresolved:** The framework's entire architecture assumes a single substrate whose state affects all fields equally. Allowing field-specific substrate resilience would require specifying what couples each field to the substrate and at what threshold the coupling fails. This is a deeper version of the "partial coupling" problem: not just "which gradients does the structure couple to?" but "at what point does the substrate's integrity matter for each field?" The framework has no resources to address this because the substrate is, by design, the one thing about which it cannot make internal statements (T001). But Paper 1 forces the issue: either the substrate has field-specific structure (contradicting its role as a uniform ground), or singularities are not substrate-level events (contradicting the framework's interpretation of them).

**T039 — Permanently virtual structure has measurable consequences: the ontological status of ghost modes**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Cunha & Lehum (2603.05476) compute photon-graviton interference contributions to scattering cross sections from ghost modes in quadratic gravity. These modes never appear as asymptotic states (they are permanently virtual, as described in T014) but their virtual contributions modify all scattering channels' angular distributions and energy scaling. The framework's Resolution primitive defines reality through resolution — what becomes definite. Conservation of Computation conserves the computational content of resolvable states. Ghost modes challenge both: they never resolve (no definite ghost particle states) but they measurably affect what does resolve (cross sections of photons and matter). The framework must choose: (i) ghost modes are "real" computation that happens to never resolve — but then Conservation of Computation must account for computational content that is permanently virtual, and the framework needs to specify what "conservation" means for content that never becomes definite; (ii) ghost modes are not "real" but their effects on real processes are — but this is precisely the instrumentalist position the framework claims to reject by insisting reality *is* computation; (iii) ghost modes are part of the substrate's internal bookkeeping that leaks into observable physics — but this violates the substrate/computation opacity that the framework asserts. This sharpens T014 by adding quantitative evidence (specific cross-section modifications) and connects to T033 (analyticity bridging IR/UV) in that virtual modes are a mechanism by which non-observable structure affects observable physics.
**Why unresolved:** The framework's ontology is resolution-based: real = resolved (or resolvable). Permanently virtual structure with measurable consequences breaks this criterion. The framework cannot classify ghost modes as real (they don't resolve) or unreal (they have effects) without either expanding its ontology beyond resolution or accepting an instrumentalist stance it claims to reject. The gauge-fixing independence verified in the paper rules out the easy escape that these are gauge artifacts.

---

**T040 — The unified substrate is falsified by field-specific singularity survival**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Paper 1 demonstrates exact, regular electromagnetic solutions with bounded power flux through a strong curvature naked singularity. The framework's claim that all fields share a single computational substrate predicts that substrate failure (singularity) should disrupt all computation. Electromagnetism's regularity through a gravitational singularity falsifies this prediction. The framework must either (a) abandon the unified substrate in favor of a layered or field-specific substrate architecture, which requires specifying what differentiates the layers and risks infinite regress (what substrate do the layers run on?), (b) redefine singularities as field-specific computational pathologies rather than substrate breakdowns, which requires a new account of what singularities *are* in computational terms and must explain how a field can diverge on an intact substrate with finite computational capacity, or (c) argue that the substrate is intact at the singularity and only the gravitational field's *representation* on the substrate breaks down, preserving substrate unity but requiring a theory of how field representations can fail independently — a theory the framework does not have. This tension subsumes and sharpens T038, which raised the same issue as a possibility. Paper 1 converts it from possibility to demonstrated fact.
**Why unresolved:** All three options require theoretical machinery the framework lacks. Option (a) requires a meta-substrate theory. Option (b) requires a computational theory of singularities that distinguishes field-level from substrate-level breakdown. Option (c) requires a theory of field-substrate coupling that specifies independent failure modes. The framework's foundational move — positing a single substrate as the ground of all computation — is what makes this intractable: the substrate was designed to be the one thing about which the framework makes no internal claims (T001), but field-specific singularity survival forces internal claims about substrate structure.

**T041 — Ghost mode interference demands an ontological category beyond "resolved" and "unresolved"**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Paper 2 demonstrates that ghost modes in quadratic gravity modify the differential cross sections of all 2→2 scattering channels via photon-graviton interference, with gauge-fixing independence explicitly verified. The framework's ontology has two categories: resolved states (definite outcomes) and unresolved states (probability distributions awaiting resolution). Ghost modes fit neither: they are permanently unresolvable (commuting statistics forbid asymptotic states) yet they are not "awaiting resolution" in any meaningful sense — they are architecturally excluded from resolution. Their physical effects (angular distribution modifications, energy scaling changes) are as real and measurable as those of any resolved state. The framework needs a third ontological category — "permanently virtual but physically efficacious computation" — that is neither resolved nor pre-resolution but something genuinely different. This category would describe computational structure that participates in the determination of resolvable outcomes without itself ever becoming an outcome. The framework currently has no resources for this because its entire ontological weight rests on resolution as the criterion of reality. This tension extends T014 and T039 by noting that the evidence is now comprehensive (all channels, not selected ones) and gauge-independent, and by explicitly demanding a new ontological category rather than merely flagging the problem.
**Why unresolved:** Introducing a third ontological category would require specifying: (a) what Conservation of Computation means for permanently virtual structure (is computational content in ghost modes conserved? can it be transformed into resolvable computation?), (b) how permanently virtual computation interacts with resolvable computation (the interference mechanism is well-defined in QFT but has no translation into framework primitives), and (c) whether permanently virtual structure is substrate-level or field-level (connecting to T040). The framework's entire architecture would need restructuring: the Conservation primitive would need to cover three categories instead of one undifferentiated "computation," and the Resolution primitive would need to acknowledge that some computation is architecturally forbidden from resolution while still being physically real.

**T042 — The 1/p⁴ propagator and the framework's silence on Green's function structure**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Paper 2's results depend critically on the graviton propagator having a 1/p⁴ structure (from the R² and R_μν R^μν terms in quadratic gravity) rather than the standard 1/p² of Einstein gravity. This produces the characteristic forward/backward scattering enhancements and the universal 1/s cross-section scaling. The framework describes gravity as "mass rolling down the curvature gradient" but has no account of the *propagator structure* — the Green's function that determines how gravitational effects transmit through spacetime. The difference between 1/p² and 1/p⁴ propagation is the difference between Newtonian-like and qualitatively different gravitational transmission, and it determines all scattering phenomenology. The framework's Field primitive specifies ẋ = −∇φ(x) but says nothing about the kernel relating the source of φ to the field at a distant point. This is not merely a quantitative gap: different propagator structures produce qualitatively different physics (oscillatory vs. monotonic potentials, confined vs. unconfined forces), and the framework has no mechanism for distinguishing them. This is related to T018 (algebra of computation) and T026 (structural content gap) but is more specific: it concerns the *response function* of fields, which is determined by the field equation's order and structure, not by the gradient alone.
**Why unresolved:** The framework would need to extend the Field primitive to include the propagator/Green's function structure of each field, specifying not just "things move down gradients" but "the gradient at point x due to a source at point y is determined by a specific kernel G(x,y) whose form depends on the field equation's algebraic structure." This would be a significant extension, effectively requiring the framework to incorporate the full structure of field equations rather than summarizing them with a single gradient descent principle. The gradient equation ẋ = −∇φ(x) is revealed as a first-order summary that loses

---

**T043 — The propagator problem: gradient dynamics is blind to Green's function structure**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Paper 2 demonstrates that all scattering phenomenology in quadratic gravity is determined by the 1/p⁴ graviton propagator structure, which produces qualitatively different physics from the 1/p² propagator of Einstein gravity (forward/backward enhancements, oscillatory potentials, ghost mode interference, universal 1/s cross-section scaling). The framework's Field primitive ẋ = −∇φ(x) specifies gradient descent — the local response of a system to a field — but says nothing about the propagator G(x,y) that determines how the field at point x responds to a source at point y. The propagator encodes the *order* and *structure* of the field equation (second-order for Einstein gravity, fourth-order for quadratic gravity), and this order determines the spectrum of modes (physical + ghost), the UV behavior (non-renormalizable vs. renormalizable), and the scattering cross sections. The gradient equation is a zeroth-order summary that discards this information entirely. This is distinct from T018 (algebra of computation, which concerns how computational operations compose) and T042 (which raised the issue as a remark). T043 asserts that the propagator structure is *the* missing piece that connects the Field primitive to actual scattering physics: without it, the primitive cannot distinguish between fundamentally different theories of the same field.
**Why unresolved:** The framework would need to extend the Field primitive from ẋ = −∇φ(x) to something that includes the Green's function structure, effectively incorporating the field equation's order and mode spectrum. This is a major extension because different propagator structures correspond to different numbers and types of modes (physical, ghost, tachyonic), each with different resolution properties. The ghost modes' 1/p⁴ contribution and their permanently virtual character are *consequences* of the propagator structure, meaning T043 is upstream of T014/T039/T041: if the framework could account for propagator structure, the ghost mode ontology might follow. Without it, the ghost mode problem is an orphan.

**T044 — Field-specific singularity survival demands a theory of field-substrate coupling**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Paper 1 provides exact analytical proof that electromagnetic fields with bounded, regular power flux can propagate through a strong curvature naked singularity. The framework's unified substrate assumption predicts that substrate breakdown (singularity) disrupts all computation. This prediction is falsified. The framework must now develop a theory of how each field couples to the substrate, with different failure thresholds for different fields. But the framework was designed so that the substrate is the one entity about which no internal claims can be made (T001). Field-specific singularity survival forces internal claims about substrate structure — specifically, about the differential coupling of gravity vs. electromagnetism to the substrate — that the framework's epistemic architecture forbids. This is distinct from T040 (which noted the unified substrate is falsified) because T044 identifies the specific *structural impossibility*: the framework cannot develop the theory it needs (field-substrate coupling) because its own design principle (substrate inaccessibility) prevents it. This is not a gap to be filled but a contradiction between the framework's explanatory needs and its self-imposed constraints.
**Why unresolved:** Resolving T044 requires either (a) abandoning substrate inaccessibility (T001), which removes the framework's most honest epistemological feature, (b) abandoning the unified substrate, which removes the framework's ontological ground, or (c) reinterpreting singularities as not being substrate events at all, which requires a new account of what singularities are and why gravity diverges while electromagnetism doesn't. All three options require foundational restructuring. The framework is trapped: it needs to say things about the substrate that it has declared itself unable to say.

---

---

**T045 — Conservation laws require geometric scaffolding the framework treats as derived**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Compère & Robert (2603.08705) prove that gravitational conservation laws in asymptotically flat spacetimes are antipodal matching conditions on a blown-up hyperboloid at spatial infinity, relating quantities at past and future null infinities that have different asymptotic structures (peeling is broken at one or both infinities) and including contributions from tails (radiation backscattered off spacetime curvature). The framework treats Conservation of Computation as a primitive — the most fundamental law. But the gravitational conservation laws cannot be *formulated* without specifying: (a) the geometric structure of spatial infinity (hyperboloid blow-up), (b) the matching conditions between null infinities with different falloff behaviors, (c) the tail contributions that arise from propagation through curved spacetime. The geometric scaffolding is not a representation choice — it is necessary for the conservation law to be well-defined. This means geometry is *prior to* conservation in the gravitational sector, directly contradicting the framework's claim that conservation is primitive and geometry is emergent or secondary. This is distinct from T037 (Hermiticity as emergent symmetry) because T037 concerns the *derivation* of conservation from geometry, while T045 concerns the *formulation* of conservation requiring geometry. It is also distinct from T035 (observer-dependent conservation) because T035 concerns conservation breaking for restricted observers, while T045 concerns the geometric complexity required for conservation to hold even globally. The tail phenomenon is particularly sharp: conserved information does not simply propagate at the tick rate to the boundary but scatters off the bulk geometry and arrives with logarithmic delay, meaning the Tick primitive's "maximum propagation rate" does not determine when conservation is satisfied — the geometry of the bulk spacetime does.
**Why unresolved:** The framework would need to either (a) demonstrate that the geometric scaffolding at spatial infinity itself emerges from computational primitives, showing that the hyperboloid blow-up, the matching conditions, and the tail structure are all consequences of the tick rate and conservation law applied to a discrete substrate (a major derivation no one has attempted), (b) accept that conservation is not primitive but requires geometric infrastructure, which would demote Conservation of Computation from a primitive to a derived law, or (c) argue that the asymptotic geometric structure is part of the substrate architecture, not the computation — but then the conservation law is partly architectural and partly computational, blurring the framework's foundational distinction. The tail phenomenon is especially problematic because it means the conservation law's enforcement involves non-local bulk propagation that the gradient equation ẋ = −∇φ(x) does not capture (the tails scatter off ∇φ itself).

**T046 — Combinatorial dimension as a physical parameter invisible to the framework**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Berthold & Keppler (2603.08638) demonstrate that large-N factorization — the decomposition of correlators into products of simpler correlators in the large-N limit — holds for matrix models but fails for tensor models. The critical difference is the combinatorial dimension of the Feynman graph structure: matrix model graphs are ribbon graphs (surfaces, effectively 2D), while tensor model graphs are colored graphs dual to higher-dimensional pseudo-manifolds. The framework's Conservation of Computation and the other four primitives have no parameter or structural element corresponding to the combinatorial dimension of the computational architecture. Yet this dimension determines a physically crucial property: whether the computation modularizes at large N. In matrix models, the genus expansion (a topological invariant) controls the 1/N expansion, and factorization follows from the dominance of planar (genus-0) graphs. In tensor models, no single topological invariant plays this role, and non-factorization means the computation remains irreducibly entangled even at infinite N. The framework claims to describe "how computation is organized" through its five primitives, but none of them can distinguish between matrix-type and tensor-type computational architectures, even though this distinction determines fundamental properties of the large-N limit. This is related to T011 (planarity as missing primitive), T018 (algebra of computation), and T026 (structural content gap) but is sharper: it identifies a specific, discrete parameter (combinatorial dimension of the graph structure) that governs a specific physical property (factorization vs. non-factorization) and that the framework entirely lacks.
