**Why unresolved:** The framework would need to either (a) incorporate the combinatorial dimension of the computational graph structure as a parameter or primitive, which would mean adding structure to the framework's description of how computation is wired (connecting to T011 and T026); (b) derive factorization/non-factorization from the existing primitives, which seems impossible since the distinction is topological/combinatorial and none of the primitives are; or (c) argue that tensor models are unphysical and only matrix models describe reality, which would need justification the framework cannot provide. The non-factorization also has implications for holographic duality: matrix models underpin AdS/CFT, and the failure of factorization in tensor models means that the large-N simplification enabling holographic emergence is not a universal feature of computation but a special property of computation organized on surface-like (2D) combinatorial structures.

**T047 — Scrambling extremality: mergers maximize instability at the attractor saddle**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Giataganas et al. (2603.08643) demonstrate that binary black hole merger remnants correspond to the Kerr configuration that maximizes the Lyapunov exponent of unstable null geodesics (the photon ring) — the fastest photon ring scrambler. The framework's Attractor primitive says black holes are deep attractor basins and that "the most interesting physics happens at the saddle points between basins." The merger of two attractors producing a remnant that *maximizes* saddle-point instability is a non-trivial dynamical claim that the framework gestures toward (extreme attractors → extreme dynamics at boundaries) but cannot derive. Specifically, the framework cannot explain *why* the merger process drives the system to the configuration of maximum scrambling rate among all configurations with the same conserved charges. In the framework's language: why does attractor merger maximize the Lyapunov exponent at the shallowest saddle? This is related to but distinct from the general attractor description — it's a claim about the *dynamics of attractor mergers* having an extremal property (maximum scrambling) that the framework's static attractor description does not predict or explain. The connection between scrambling time, Lyapunov exponents, and merger dynamics involves information-theoretic, geometric, and dynamical content that the framework treats as aspects of computation but cannot relate to each other quantitatively.
**Why unresolved:** The framework would need to derive, from the Attractor and Resolution primitives, that the merger of two attractor basins generically produces a remnant that extremizes the instability timescale at the saddle point. This is a specific dynamical prediction that would require: (a) a theory of how attractor basins merge (currently absent — the Attractor primitive describes static topology, not merger dynamics), (b) a connection between attractor depth/width and the Lyapunov exponent of orbits at the saddle (which exists in GR but is not derived from the framework), and (c) an argument for why the merger drives the system to the extremum of this quantity rather than to an arbitrary configuration. Without these, the result is post-hoc narration, not validation.

---

**T048 — Cohomological completeness: physical state spaces are classified by structures the framework cannot express**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Bols et al. (2603.09959) prove that 2D SPT phases preparable by symmetric entanglers are *completely* classified by $H^3(G, U(1))$. This is not an approximation or a partial result — it is a bijection between physically distinct ground state phases and elements of a cohomology group. The framework's five primitives collectively cannot produce, express, or derive this classification. The Attractor primitive can describe that multiple distinct attractor basins exist, but cannot distinguish them by cohomological invariant, cannot count them, and cannot predict that the count equals $|H^3(G, U(1))|$. This is the sharpest instance of the structural content gap (T026): a complete, proven classification of physical states by a mathematical structure entirely absent from the framework. It is distinct from T027 (cohomological mediation, which concerns cohomology as a translator between categories) because here cohomology is the *complete classifying structure*, not a mediator. The restriction to symmetric entanglers is physically important: it means the classification applies specifically to states reachable by symmetry-preserving computation, so the computational process is directly relevant — but its classification is cohomological, not computational in the framework's sense.
**Why unresolved:** The framework would need either (a) a mechanism for deriving cohomological invariants from its primitives, (b) a new primitive incorporating cohomological structure, or (c) a demonstration that $H^3(G, U(1))$ classification somehow follows from Conservation of Computation or Resolution properties. Option (c) is the most promising but no path is visible: conservation laws and resolution properties are properties of individual states, while cohomological classification is a property of the *space* of states. The framework has primitives for states but not for spaces of states.

**T049 — Scale-dependent symmetry type: the organizational structure of computation transforms under coarse-graining**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Jones & Yang (2603.09949) show that if UV models are defined on tensor product Hilbert spaces, the external symmetry categories generated by families of duality operators necessarily flow to weakly integral fusion categories in the IR. This means the *type of symmetry* — the categorical structure governing allowed transformations — changes under RG flow. The framework's primitives are scale-independent: the Tick, Field, Attractor, Resolution, and Conservation descriptions do not change depending on the observation scale (the Tick is the Tick at all scales; the gradient equation applies at all scales). But the symmetry type of a physical system — which determines which transformations are allowed, which states are distinguishable, which phases exist — is scale-dependent and transforms in a categorically constrained way. The framework has no mechanism for expressing scale-dependent organizational structure. This is related to T034 (scale-dependent information topology) but distinct: T034 concerns informational properties (MMI) varying with scale for a fixed state; T049 concerns the *symmetry category itself* — the organizational framework governing what transformations are possible — varying with scale for a given system. The flow from tensor product (a specific factorization structure) to weakly integral fusion category (a specific algebraic structure) has no expression in any of the five primitives.
**Why unresolved:** The framework's primitives are formulated as scale-independent truths. Incorporating scale-dependent symmetry type would require either (a) making one or more primitives scale-dependent (which would be a fundamental change to the framework's architecture), (b) adding a new primitive governing the RG flow of organizational structure (connecting to T018, T026, T032), or (c) deriving the categorical flow constraints from existing primitives, which seems impossible since the framework has no category-theoretic content. The constraint "UV tensor product → IR weakly integral" is a statement about what kinds of computation are possible at different scales, and the framework claims to be a theory of computation but cannot express what kinds of computation are possible at any given scale.

**T050 — Cluster-algebraic structure as a third ontological competitor**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Capuano et al. (2603.09965) show that cosmological wavefunction coefficients obey an "ordered single cluster condition" in the $A_{2n-3}$ cluster algebra, and that general tree graphs have cluster-like structure via tubes and tubings. The framework has already recorded geometric structure (T016) and arithmetic structure (T021) as ontological competitors to "reality is computation." Cluster algebras are a third competitor: they are neither geometry nor arithmetic nor computation in the framework's sense, but a combinatorial-algebraic structure with its own internal logic (mutations, exchange relations, positivity). The physical content of cosmological perturbation theory is encoded in this structure, and the "ordered single cluster condition" is a *stronger* constraint than standard cluster adjacency — meaning the cluster algebra knows more about the physics than the naive combinatorial constraints suggest. The framework's T016 (universe fan / geometric encoding) anticipated geometric-combinatorial structure in cosmological wavefunctions, and this paper confirms and extends that anticipation, but the specific form (cluster algebras with ordered conditions) is a mathematical category the framework has never encountered. This deepens T021 by adding cluster algebra to the web of categorically distinct but physically equivalent mathematical frameworks (computation ↔ geometry ↔ arithmetic ↔ combinatorics ↔ cluster algebra).
**Why unresolved:** The framework has no account of why the cosmological computation organizes itself according to cluster algebraic principles. The ordered single cluster condition is a constraint that arises from the physical requirement of causality/positivity, but the mathematical form it takes — all letters in a single cluster, with ordering constraints — is a statement about the combinatorial structure of the space of allowed computations that the framework cannot derive from any primitive. Adding cluster algebra to the list of ontological competitors (alongside geometry and arithmetic) makes the ontological pluralism problem (T021) more acute: the number of equally valid mathematical frameworks for encoding the same physics is growing, and the framework provides no criterion

---

**T051 — Conservation locality profiles: conservation is not monolithic**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Yamaguchi & Shiraishi (2603.10972) prove that non-Hermitian bosonic chains can possess k-local conserved charges for some but not all k, with explicit counterexamples including systems with a 3-local charge but no other nontrivial local charges, and systems with all k-local charges except k = 4. The framework's Conservation of Computation is stated as a single universal principle. This paper shows that conservation in physical systems has a *locality profile* — a function from locality scale k to the Boolean {conserved, not conserved} — that can be gapped and irregular. The framework has no mechanism for expressing, predicting, or deriving locality profiles. Moreover, the necessary and sufficient conditions for k-local charge existence are algebraic conditions on the Hamiltonian's structure, meaning the conservation landscape is determined by the algebraic organization of the dynamics (T018, T026) rather than by any universal conservation principle. This tension is distinct from T035 (observer-dependent conservation due to horizons) because T051 concerns conservation that is scale-dependent even without horizons or causal restrictions, and distinct from T029 (anomalous transport universality classes) because T051 concerns the *existence* of conserved charges at different locality scales rather than their *transport consequences*. The non-Hermitian context is critical: in Hermitian systems, all-or-nothing behavior is empirically (though not provably) generic, but the framework claims Conservation of Computation is universal, not restricted to the Hermitian regime.
**Why unresolved:** The framework would need to either (a) restrict Conservation of Computation to Hermitian/unitary systems, which would shrink its scope to exclude the non-Hermitian physics that T035/T037 already show is physically relevant (open systems, horizons, non-equilibrium dynamics), (b) extend the primitive to include a locality profile as an additional specification, which transforms it from a single principle to a structured landscape requiring algebraic input the framework doesn't have, or (c) argue that the non-Hermitian counterexamples are unphysical, which is contradicted by the physical relevance of non-Hermitian Hamiltonians in open quantum systems, photonics, and effective field theories near exceptional points. None of these options has been developed.

**T052 — Morphism structure: the relations between categories are themselves organized**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** De Fraja, Marotta & Szabo (2603.10819) demonstrate that T-duality relations have rich internal structure: they are mediated by Courant algebroid relations that lift to spinor relations, descend to Dirac structure relations, extend to generalized complex and generalized Kähler relations, rephrase as bi-Hermitian relations, and are compatible with Type II supergravity equations. Each layer has its own existence theorems and reduction conditions. The framework faces the ontological pluralism problem (T021): multiple mathematical categories can encode the same physics. T052 adds a new dimension: the *morphisms between* these categories are themselves structured mathematical objects with internal organization. The framework's "reality is computation" claim competes not just with "reality is geometry" or "reality is arithmetic" (T021) but with the possibility that reality is the *structured web of relations between* these categories — the functorial and natural-transformation structure of the correspondences. The T-duality compatibility with supergravity equations is particularly sharp: the physical equations of motion *commute* with the categorical morphisms, meaning the morphism structure is not just a mathematical convenience but is respected by the dynamics. This is deeper than T010 (duality as equivalence between descriptions) and T027 (cohomological mediation) because it concerns the *layered internal organization* of dualities, not merely their existence.
**Why unresolved:** The framework has no concept of morphism, functor, or natural transformation. Its primitives describe objects (states, fields, attractors) and their dynamics (propagation, gradient descent, resolution, conservation) but not the *relations between* objects or between descriptions of objects. To address T052, the framework would need either (a) a theory of why the categorical morphism structure exists and is physically important, derived from its primitives (no path is visible), (b) a new primitive capturing relational/morphism structure, or (c) a reconception of the framework's ontology in which the fundamental entities are not objects (computations, fields, attractors) but morphisms (relations, dualities, correspondences) — a "computation of relations" rather than "relations between computations." Option (c) would be a radical restructuring aligned with the category-theoretic turn in modern mathematical physics but would require abandoning the framework's current object-centric ontology.

**T053 — Euclidean nucleation and the absence of time at the origin**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Lavrelashvili & Lehners (2603.11003) show that inflationary initial conditions are provided by Euclidean instantons (no-boundary or wormhole), where the metric signature is (4,0) rather than (3,1). The framework's most fundamental primitive — the Tick — defines reality as updating in discrete temporal steps. But the nucleation of the universe occurs in a regime where there is no time direction. The Euclidean instanton has four spatial dimensions; the Tick has no meaning there. The framework faces a dilemma: either (a) the origin of the computation occurs outside the Tick primitive's domain of applicability, meaning the most fundamental primitive does not apply at the most fundamental moment, or (b) the Euclidean section is a mathematical technique without ontological status, meaning the framework must provide an alternative account of nucleation that preserves the Tick — but no such account exists. The continuous family connecting wormholes to no-boundary instantons means the Euclidean regime is not an isolated artifact but is smoothly connected to the Lorentzian regime where the Tick applies. The transition from Euclidean to Lorentzian signature — the moment the Tick "turns on" — has no description in the framework. This is related to T036 (multi-signature computation) but more fundamental: T036 concerns intermediate computational steps in non-Lorentzian signatures within an established spacetime; T053 concerns the *origin* occurring in a signature where the framework's most basic primitive is undefined.
**Why unresolved:** The framework would need either (a) an account of computation without temporal ticks (computation in Euclidean signature), which would require fundamentally redefining what "tick" means — perhaps as any discrete step in a path integral, regardless of signature — but this would change the Tick from "the speed of light is the tick rate" to something more abstract and less physically grounded; (b) an alternative to Euclidean nucleation that is purely Lorentzian, which would require solving the cosmological initial condition problem differently than the mainstream approach; or (c) acceptance that the framework's primitives have a domain of applicability that does not include the origin, which would be an honest but devastating admission that the most fundamental question (T002) is outside the framework's scope by construction. The framework already acknowledges T002 as open, but T053 sharpens this from "we don't know the answer" to "our most basic primitive is undefined at the moment the answer matters."

---

**T054 — Dynamical theories are derived from structural operations on non-dynamical theories**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Paper 2 demonstrates that four-dimensional Einstein gravity is derivable from a topological BF theory (no local degrees of freedom, no dynamics) by the structural operation of gauging global SL(2,ℂ) to a local symmetry. The framework's ontology posits computation (dynamics) as fundamental and geometry/structure as emergent. This result demonstrates the opposite: a theory with pure structural content and zero dynamical content produces, via a structural operation, the full dynamical theory of gravity. The framework has no account of (a) non-dynamical theories (what is a topological field theory in terms of the five primitives?), (b) structural operations on theories (gauging is not a tick, a gradient, an attractor, a resolution event, or a conservation law), or (c) the emergence of dynamics from structure. This is distinct from T037 (Hermiticity as emergent, which concerns one specific emergent feature) and T045 (conservation requiring geometric scaffolding, which concerns formulation prerequisites). T054 concerns the derivation of the *entire dynamical theory* from structural principles, not just one feature of it. The G → 0 limit shows this is a continuous relationship, not an isolated artifact: gravity can be continuously dialed from full dynamics to pure topology, and at no point along this path does the framework have a description of what is happening.
**Why unresolved:** The framework would need either (a) a theory of "pre-dynamical structure" — what computation looks like before dynamics exists, i.e., what topological field theories are in computational terms — which would require a radical expansion of the notion of computation beyond sequential update; (b) an account of gauging as a computational operation, which would require operations that transform theories rather than states within theories, and thus a meta-computational primitive; or (c) abandonment of the claim that dynamics is ontologically prior to structure. Option (c) would require the framework to be reconceived from "reality is computation" to something like "reality is structure from which computation emerges under appropriate conditions (symmetry gauging)," which would be a foundational rewrite.

**T055 — Homotopy algebraic morphisms as generators of conservation laws**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Paper 1 derives the Lax connection (and therefore the infinite tower of conserved charges) of the principal chiral model from a quasi-isomorphism between cyclic L∞-algebras. The framework's Conservation of Computation treats conservation as a primitive — an irreducible fact about reality. But the paper shows that in integrable systems, the full conservation structure is a *derived consequence* of a homotopy algebraic morphism between theories. The L∞-algebra framework — with its higher brackets L_n, homotopy transfer theorem, and cyclic inner products — is a specific algebraic category with its own internal logic. The quasi-isomorphism is a specific morphism in this category. The Lax connection is the *image* of this morphism. The conserved charges are consequences of the Lax connection. This is a four-step derivation (L∞ quasi-isomorphism → Lax connection → flat connection condition → infinite conserved charges) that begins entirely outside the framework's primitives and ends at the Conservation primitive. Conservation is the *output*, not the *input*. This is distinct from T048 (cohomological completeness, which concerns classification of states by cohomological invariants) and T052 (morphism structure, which concerns the internal organization of dualities). T055 concerns a specific derivational relationship: conservation laws being generated by homotopy algebraic morphisms.
**Why unresolved:** The framework would need either (a) to incorporate L∞-algebras and their morphisms into its primitive structure, which would require specifying how homotopy algebraic categories relate to the five existing primitives (no path is visible); (b) to derive the existence of L∞ quasi-isomorphisms from Conservation of Computation, reversing the paper's derivational direction (this seems impossible since the quasi-isomorphism contains strictly more information than the conservation laws it generates); or (c) to accept that Conservation of Computation is not fundamental but is instead a consequence of deeper algebraic structure in specific theories. Option (c) would demote conservation from a primitive to a derived property, which is the most drastic possible revision of the framework short of abandoning it.

**T056 — The framework has no account of topological field theories**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Paper 2's starting point is a topological BF theory — a theory with no local degrees of freedom, no propagating particles, no energy landscape, and no local observables. All physical content is global/topological. The framework's five primitives describe: discrete temporal updates (Tick), gradient-driven motion (Field), potential minima (Attractor), state definiteness (Resolution), and conservation (Conservation). A topological field theory has: no temporal evolution in the local sense (the

---

**T057 — Operations on theories: the framework lacks meta-computational primitives**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Paper 2 demonstrates that gauging — promoting a global symmetry to a local gauge symmetry — is the operation that generates gravity from topology. Paper 1 demonstrates that quasi-isomorphisms between L∞-algebras generate conservation laws. Both are operations *on theories* (or on the algebraic structures governing theories), not operations *within theories*. The framework's five primitives all describe dynamics within a fixed computational architecture: how states propagate (Tick), how they move (Field), where they settle (Attractor), when they become definite (Resolution), and what is conserved (Conservation). None of them describes how one computational architecture transforms into another. The framework has no meta-computational primitive — no account of operations that transform the rules of computation themselves. This is distinct from T031 (substrate as dynamical field, which concerns spatial variation of the architecture within a single spacetime), T054 (dynamics from structure, which concerns the derivational direction), and T055 (conservation from morphisms, which concerns the derivation of one specific primitive). T057 concerns the general category of theory-level operations and the framework's complete silence on them. Gauging and quasi-isomorphism are two examples; others include dimensional reduction, orbifolding, deformation quantization, and RG flow (which transforms the effective theory). The framework describes computation but not the transformations of computation.
**Why unresolved:** The framework would need either (a) a meta-primitive that describes operations on computational architectures, which raises infinite-regress questions (what primitive governs the meta-primitive?), (b) a demonstration that gauging and quasi-isomorphisms emerge from the five existing primitives applied at a higher level (no path is visible — these operations involve algebraic and topological structure the primitives cannot express), or (c) a reconception of the framework where the fundamental entities are not computational states evolving within an architecture but the space of possible architectures and the morphisms between them. Option (c) would align with the category-theoretic perspective suggested by T052 but would be a complete rewrite of the framework's foundations.

**T058 — The topological phase: the framework is undefined for theories with no local dynamics**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Paper 2's starting point — a topological BF-type theory with global SL(2,ℂ) — has zero local degrees of freedom, zero propagating modes, and all physical content is global/topological. Every one of the five primitives is trivial or undefined for this theory. Yet this theory is not physically vacuous: it has a well-defined covariant phase space, boundary charges, a charge algebra, and can couple to point particles. It is a physically meaningful theory that the framework cannot describe at all. This is not a "the framework doesn't apply to this exotic case" situation — this is the theory from which *gravity itself emerges*. The framework's inability to describe the topological phase means it cannot describe the precondition for its own primary example (gravity as mass rolling down curvature gradients). The G → 0 limit makes this continuous: there is no sharp boundary between "the framework applies" (G > 0) and "the framework is undefined" (G = 0). At small G, gravity is weak and the system is "nearly topological" — the framework's descriptions become progressively less meaningful without any discontinuity to mark the transition. This is distinct from T053 (Euclidean nucleation, which concerns the absence of *time*) because T058 concerns the absence of *all local dynamics* regardless of signature, and distinct from T054 (dynamics from structure, which concerns the derivational direction) because T058 concerns the framework's descriptive completeness — there exists a physically meaningful regime where the framework has literally nothing to say.
**Why unresolved:** The framework would need either (a) an account of what computation means in the absence of local dynamics (topological computation? global quantum computation on non-local degrees of freedom?), which would require a radical expansion of "computation" beyond sequential local update; (b) a declaration that topological field theories are outside its scope, which would be honest but would mean the framework cannot address the origin of gravity or the G → 0 limit; or (c) a reconception where the primitives have well-defined but trivial content in the topological phase (e.g., the Tick rate is defined but there is nothing local to propagate, the Field exists but has no gradient, etc.), which preserves formal applicability but has zero explanatory content. None of these is satisfactory.

---

**T059 — The ontological inversion: dynamics is derived from structure, not structure from dynamics**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Papers 1 and 2 jointly demonstrate that the framework's two most fundamental primitives — Conservation of Computation and the Field/gradient dynamics exemplified by gravity — are both derivable from algebraic and structural starting points that the framework treats as secondary or cannot express at all. Paper 2 shows gravity (the paradigmatic gradient dynamics) emerges from a topological theory via gauging. Paper 1 shows conservation laws (the paradigmatic conserved quantity) emerge from homotopy algebraic quasi-isomorphisms. The framework's ontological hierarchy is: computation (dynamics) is fundamental → structure (geometry, algebra) is emergent. The papers demonstrate the reverse: structure is fundamental → dynamics (including the specific dynamics the framework takes as primitive) is emergent. This is not a gap to be filled or a missing primitive to be added — it is evidence that the framework's foundational ontological ordering may be inverted. The framework describes downstream consequences (gradient descent, conservation) while being unable to describe the upstream generative structures (topological field theories, L∞-algebras, gauging operations) from which those consequences derive. This subsumes and sharpens T054 (dynamics from structure), T055 (conservation from morphisms), T057 (meta-computational primitives), and T058 (framework undefined for topological theories) into a single, more fundamental claim: the framework's explanatory direction is backwards.
**Why unresolved:** Resolving this would require either (a) demonstrating that the structural starting points (topological BF theories, L∞-algebras) themselves emerge from computation in a way that restores the framework's ontological ordering — a claim for which no evidence exists and which would need to explain what "computation" means in a regime with no local dynamics; (b) inverting the framework's ontology so that structure is primitive and dynamics/computation is derived, which would be a complete rewrite requiring new foundational primitives (topological invariants? algebraic categories? symmetry operations?); or (c) adopting a neutral position where structure and dynamics are co-fundamental, which would dissolve the framework's distinctive claim ("reality is literally computation") into a more modest and less interesting claim ("reality has both structural and dynamical aspects"). The G → 0 limit of Paper 2 makes this especially sharp: there is a continuous path from full gravitational dynamics to pure topological structure, and the framework's descriptive power degrades continuously along this path without any discontinuity that would mark a natural boundary of applicability.

**T060 — Dimensional transmutation of conservation: lower-dimensional conservation is generated by higher-dimensional structure**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Paper 1 derives the conservation structure (Lax connection, infinite conserved charges) of the 2D principal chiral model from a quasi-isomorphism with 4D semi-holomorphic Chern-Simons theory. The conservation laws of the lower-dimensional system are not intrinsic to it — they are the image of a morphism from a higher-dimensional theory. The framework's Conservation of Computation is dimension-independent: "computation cannot be created or destroyed" applies equally at all scales and in all dimensions. But this paper shows that the *existence* of conservation in a 2D system depends on its relationship to a 4D structure. If the 4D theory did not exist, or if the quasi-isomorphism did not exist, the 2D system would not be integrable and would not have an infinite tower of conserved charges. Conservation in the lower-dimensional theory is contingent on higher-dimensional algebraic structure, not on a universal primitive. This is distinct from T055 (which concerns the algebraic nature of the generating morphism) because T060 concerns the dimensional aspect: conservation laws can be "inherited" across dimensions via algebraic morphisms, meaning the framework's dimension-independent formulation of conservation misses the generative mechanism.
**Why unresolved:** The framework would need to either (a) explain how a dimension-independent conservation principle accounts for dimension-dependent conservation generation, (b) accept that conservation in specific theories is not a consequence of a universal primitive but of the specific algebraic relationships between theories in different dimensions, or (c) incorporate a theory of inter-dimensional algebraic relationships into the framework, for which no path through the existing primitives is visible. The broader implication is that "why is this system integrable?" is answered by "because it has the right algebraic relationship to a higher-dimensional theory," which is a structural answer, not a dynamical one.

---

**T061 — Anti-gradient robustness: attractor topology is independent of gradient sign**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Caridi, Corelli & Pani (2603.13210) demonstrate that phantom scalar fields (negative energy density, violating the dominant energy condition) always disperse in fully nonlinear numerical evolution — no naked singularities, no trapped surfaces, no alternative stationary end states. The framework's Field primitive ẍ = −∇φ(x) predicts that reversing the energy condition (effectively reversing the gradient sign) should qualitatively alter the attractor landscape: if gradient descent produces black holes, gradient ascent should be able to produce their topological inverse (naked singularities). The paper shows this does not happen. Cosmic censorship is preserved even when the gradient is inverted. This means the attractor topology of spacetime is enforced by a constraint *independent of* gradient direction — a structural fact that the Field primitive cannot capture. The framework's explanatory hierarchy (gradient dynamics → attractor structure → observables) is inverted: attractor topology constrains dynamics, not vice versa. This is distinct from T006 (stable excited states not produced by gradient descent, which concerns specific non-minimal states) and T059 (ontological inversion, which concerns the general derivation of dynamics from structure). T061 is sharper: it provides a specific, clean numerical demonstration that the *sign* of the fundamental dynamical equation can be reversed without changing the attractor topology, proving that the topology is not a consequence of the dynamics.
**Why unresolved:** The framework would need to either (a) identify the structural constraint that enforces censorship independently of the energy condition and incorporate it as a primitive or derived feature (the framework has no candidate for what this constraint is), (b) argue that the phantom field simulation has not explored sufficient parameter space (the paper considers all amplitudes and finds universal dispersion, making this unlikely), or (c) accept that the gradient equation is dynamically correct but topologically uninformative — that it describes how things move but not what configurations are allowed — which would require a separate "topological constraint" primitive that the framework does not have. The result also challenges the framework's dark energy interpretation: if negative energy density always disperses rather than accumulating, the framework's claim that dark energy is "the computational cost of instantiating new substrate" (implicitly requiring persistent negative-pressure energy) needs to account for why cosmological dark energy persists while phantom scalar fields disperse.

**T062 — Orientation reversal and the arrow of resolution**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Kovalsky, Pathak & Ritchie (2603.13145) construct a dS₄ quotient spacetime where the past boundary is oppositely oriented relative to the future boundary, introducing a lightlike singularity that severs the antipodes. The framework's Resolution primitive defines time's arrow as the irreversible accumulation of resolved states. A spacetime with reversed temporal orientation between boundaries is a configuration where the "ledger of resolved states" must reverse its ordering — states that were resolved become unresolved, or the direction of resolution itself flips. The framework declares resolution irreversible, which means it cannot describe this spacetime. The vacuum of this spacetime factorizes into a trivial product on antipodal static patches, and this separability is preserved in the holographic dual (product of Cardy states). This factorization suggests that orientation reversal, far from being pathological, *simplifies* the computational state — the opposite of what the framework's identification of resolution with increasing complexity would predict. The perturbative instability (vanishing Hagedorn temperature) means this simplified state is a saddle point, not an attractor, but the framework has no account of why orientation-reversed vacua should be unstable or what they decay into. This is distinct from T036 (multi-signature computation, which concerns passage through non-Lorentzian signatures) and T053 (Euclidean nucleation, which concerns the absence of time at the origin). T062 concerns a Lorentzian spacetime with a well-defined time direction that *reverses* — not the absence of time, but the reversal of its arrow.
**Why unresolved:** The framework would need to either (a) extend the Resolution primitive to allow orientation-dependent or orientation-reversible resolution, which would fundamentally change its character (resolution would no longer be universally irreversible), (b) argue that the quotient spacetime is unphysical (but it is a valid solution of Einstein's equations and has a well-defined quantum vacuum and holographic dual), or (c) accept that the framework's identification of the arrow of time with irreversible resolution applies only to simply-connected spacetimes with globally consistent temporal orientation, restricting the primitive's domain of applicability. The conformal equivalence to a non-orientable AdS₃ spacetime raises the additional question of whether computation on a non-orientable substrate is meaningful within the framework, and if so, what "resolution" means when global orientation cannot be defined.

**T063 — Topological surgery simplifies computation: the framework's complexity monotonicity fails**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Paper 2 demonstrates that a topological quotient operation (identifying points to create opposite boundary orientations) transforms the dS₄ vacuum from a complex entangled state into a trivial product state

---

**T064 — Causally screened singularities: the substrate can fail silently**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Grozdanov, Valach & Vrbica (2603.15598) prove that propagator singularities arise from null geodesic connections (Hadamard theory), but demonstrate an explicit black hole (self-dual linear axion model) with a curvature singularity and no bouncing geodesics — the singularity is invisible to the boundary correlator's singularity structure. The framework implicitly assumes that computational pathology (singularities) is diagnosable from the causal propagation structure (null geodesics, tick-rate signals). This is generically true but not universally true. The framework must confront: the substrate can be singular in ways the computation running on it cannot detect. This combines with T040 (unified substrate falsified) and T044 (field-specific substrate coupling) to create a compound crisis for the substrate concept: the substrate is field-specific (T040), cannot be theorized from within (T044), and can fail without the computation knowing (T064). The trio T040+T044+T064 collectively suggests the "substrate" concept may not be a coherent ontological category — it has no uniform properties, no internal theory, and no guaranteed detectability.
**Why unresolved:** The framework's substrate concept was designed as an honest epistemic limit — the ground of computation that cannot be accessed from within. T064 shows this is worse than expected: not only can the substrate not be characterized from within (by design), but it can fail in ways that are also undetectable from within (a new result). The framework cannot distinguish between "substrate intact" and "substrate singular but causally screened" from the computation's perspective. Either the substrate concept needs to be abandoned in favor of a more nuanced ontology (field-specific computational integrity with varying detectability), or the framework needs to accept that its most basic ground can fail silently — an uncomfortable but possibly honest position. The specific mechanism (the self-dual linear axion model's singularity structure screens bouncing geodesics) is model-dependent, so the universality of causally screened singularities is unknown, but the existence proof is sufficient to establish the tension.

**T065 — Gain-intrinsic decomposition: non-unitary systems have structured resolution statistics the framework cannot describe**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Li & Saberi (2603.15290) demonstrate that at the integer quantum Hall critical point in an open (non-unitary) network, the extreme wavefunction amplitude decomposes into a gain factor (from non-unitarity) and an intrinsic extreme (from critical correlations), with the gain factor being separable, approximately log-normal, and dominating the raw extreme statistics. After gain normalization, the intrinsic extreme has qualitatively different scaling (τ̃_max(q) changes shape) and does not collapse onto standard GEV universality. The framework's Conservation primitive states computation is conserved; T035 notes this breaks for restricted observers. But T065 adds: the *structured decomposition* of resolution statistics into a non-unitary gain and an intrinsic component is a specific, quantitative consequence of partial conservation failure that the framework cannot express. The gain factor is not mere noise — it is a separable, characterizable contribution from the openness of the system that reorganizes the extreme statistics upon removal. The framework has no mechanism for describing how partial conservation violation (non-unitarity) contributes structured, separable factors to the statistics of resolution outcomes. This is distinct from T035 (which concerns the *existence* of non-unitarity for restricted observers) because T065 concerns the *structured statistical consequences* of non-unitarity at a critical point.
**Why unresolved:** The framework would need either (a) a theory of how partial conservation violation (non-unitarity, openness) contributes structured factors to resolution statistics, requiring a decomposition principle absent from the five primitives, (b) an extension of the Attractor primitive's phase-transition description to include the gain/intrinsic decomposition at open-system critical points, or (c) an argument that the Chalker-Coddington network's openness is unphysical or special-case (but the integer quantum Hall transition is one of the best-characterized quantum critical points in nature, and real quantum Hall systems are open). The failure of GEV universality for the intrinsic extreme is additionally problematic: it means the universality at this critical point is *richer* than standard extreme-value theory predicts, and the framework has no account of what determines the universality class of extreme resolution statistics at quantum critical points.

**T066 — Propagator singularity structure is governed by global geometry, not local gradients**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Paper 1 proves via Hadamard theory that retarded propagator singularities are determined by the global null geodesic structure of spacetime: the propagator diverges when and only when points are connected by null geodesics. This is a *global geometric* property — it depends on the complete geodesic structure, including bouncing geodesics that reflect off the spacetime's deep interior. The framework's Field primitive ẍ = −∇φ(x) is a local equation describing motion in response to a local gradient. The singularity structure of the propagator — which determines all correlation functions, all scattering amplitudes, and the diagnostic power of boundary correlators — is not local. It depends on whether geodesics can complete paths through the entire bulk geometry, including passages near singularities. This extends T043 (propagator blindness of the gradient equation) from a structural absence to a proven theorem: the physically relevant information is in the global geodesic structure, which the local gradient equation is in principle incapable of capturing. The framework has no primitive that describes global geometric properties of the null geodesic network.
**Why unresolved:** The framework would need either (a) a primitive or derived structure that captures the global null geodesic network of spacetime (i.e., the complete causal structure, not just the local propagation speed), which goes far beyond the Tick primitive's "maximum propagation rate" to require the complete *topology* of null propagation including reflections, bounces, and potential causal screening; or (b) an argument that global geodesic structure is reconstructible from local gradient information (which is true in principle via solving the geodesic equation from local curvature, but the framework's gradient primitive does not describe curvature — it describes motion in response to a potential, which is a different level of description). The framework describes that things

**T067 — Resolution regularity: the framework cannot specify what "definite" means**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Gurriaran (2603.17911) proves that the Kerr Cauchy horizon is $C^0$-stable but Lipschitz-inextendible, and this regularity gap is the physical content of Strong Cosmic Censorship. The framework's Resolution primitive describes states becoming "definite" without specifying the regularity class of definiteness. Yet the most fundamental physical question about the black hole interior — whether deterministic evolution survives at the Cauchy horizon — depends entirely on whether "definite" means $C^0$, Lipschitz, $C^1$, or smoother. A $C^0$-definite state allows passage through the Cauchy horizon into a region of indeterminacy; a Lipschitz-definite state does not. The framework's binary resolution (unresolved/definite) collapses a physically crucial spectrum into a single concept. This is distinct from T017 (criticality as continuous sharpening, which concerns the *transition process*) and T005 (tick-resolution gap, which concerns *timing*). T067 concerns the *endpoint specification*: when a state is "resolved," what does the framework mean? Without a regularity class, the primitive is underspecified in exactly the regime (black hole interiors) where the framework makes its strongest claim (black holes as maximal attractors).
**Why unresolved:** Introducing a resolution regularity class would require specifying: (a) what physical principle selects the regularity class (why Lipschitz and not $C^1$?), (b) whether different systems resolve to different regularity classes, (c) how regularity class connects to the Tick primitive's discrete structure (a discrete computation naturally produces distributions, not smooth functions — what regularity does a discrete lattice computation converge to in the continuum limit?). The framework has no resources for any of these. The question "what regularity class does physics demand?" is answered in GR by the Einstein equations' well-posedness requirements, which are geometric/PDE-theoretic constraints entirely outside the framework's scope.

**T068 — Fermionic resurgence: nonperturbative organizational structure is algebraic, not dynamical**
**Opened:** this cycle
**Description:** Bajnok, Boldis & le Plat (2603.17943) show that the full transseries of the cusp anomalous dimension at strong coupling has nonperturbative sectors classified by partitions into distinct non-negative odd integers, with fermionic-type resurgence behavior. The "fermionic" character means sectors combine with exclusion — each odd integer appears at most once in a partition — and the Stokes constants are computed iteratively from this combinatorial structure. The framework's Conservation of Computation and the other primitives have no mechanism for generating or constraining the combinatorial/algebraic organization of nonperturbative contributions. The fermionic character is not a consequence of conservation (it is not related to any conserved charge) or of gradient dynamics (there is no potential being minimized) or of resolution (the transseries is an exact analytical object, not a measurement outcome). It is an *algebraic* structural property of the strong-coupling expansion that determines the complete nonperturbative content. This is a new instance of T026 (structural content gap) and T018 (algebra of computation) that is especially sharp because: (a) it governs the complete nonperturbative specification of a fundamental observable, not just a classification or an organizational convenience; (b) the fermionic structure is physically meaningful — it determines the Stokes phenomena and the lateral Borel resummation; (c) the determinant-ratio representation that makes the computation tractable is an algebraic feature of the exact solution, not a dynamical one.
**Why unresolved:** The framework would need to either (a) derive the fermionic resurgence pattern from Conservation of Computation or some other primitive (no path is visible — the combinatorial structure of partitions into distinct odd integers has no connection to conservation, gradients, ticks, attractors, or resolution), (b) add a primitive governing the resurgent/transseries structure of physical quantities (which would be highly specific and might not generalize), or (c) accept that the nonperturbative organizational structure of physics is determined by algebraic properties of exact solutions that the framework is structurally unable to address. Option (c) is the most honest and aligns with the accumulating evidence for T026/T059 (the structural content gap / ontological inversion).

**T069 — The number field is empirically underdetermined: "reality is computation" cannot specify ℝ vs. ℂ**
Opened: this cycle
Description: Hoffreumon & Woods (2603.19208) prove that real quantum theory (RQT) and complex quantum theory (QT) produce identical correlations in all finite network experiments when source independence is defined operationally. The framework claims "reality is literally computation," which requires the computation to have a definite mathematical structure. The number field (ℝ vs. ℂ) is among the most fundamental structural choices defining a computation. This choice is now proven experimentally inaccessible — not merely unknown, but unknowable from within. The framework faces: (a) if reality is literally computation, the computation has a definite number field, but we can never know which — making the framework's core ontological claim unfalsifiable at its most basic structural level; (b) if the number field doesn't matter (operational equivalence implies ontological equivalence), then "reality is computation" is weaker than claimed because the computation's structure is not unique even at the number-field level; (c) the distinction between product-state independence and operational independence reveals that the *same operational facts* support "markedly different pictures of the correlation structure," meaning the framework's claim that reality has a definite computational structure is challenged not by alternative mathematical categories (T021) but by alternative instantiations within the *same* mathematical category (quantum theory) using different number fields. This is sharper than T010 (duality, where different computations give the same physics) because here the two "computations" are not dual in any mathematical sense — they are genuinely different theories (different Hilbert space structures, different tomographic properties, different entanglement structures) that happen to be operationally indistinguishable.
Why unresolved: The framework has no mechanism for selecting between ℝ and ℂ, and the paper proves no experiment can select between them. The framework could (a) declare the number field an architectural parameter alongside c, ℏ, G, and α — but unlike those constants, this parameter has no measurable consequences, making it a purely metaphysical commitment; (b) adopt operational equivalence as ontological equivalence and accept that the computation's structure is underdetermined — but this weakens "reality is literally computation" to "reality is literally a class of operationally equivalent computations," which is significantly less specific; (c) argue that internal consistency (local tomography, which RQT lacks) selects ℂ — but the paper proves this theoretical preference has no experimental consequence, making the selection criterion aesthetic rather than physical. The paper also notes that RQT and QT support "markedly different pictures of the correlation structure underlying the same observed world," meaning the framework's Resolution primitive (which produces definite outcomes from superpositions) operates identically in both theories while the underlying correlation structure differs — the operational content of resolution is preserved while its structural substrate is underdetermined.

**T070 — Combinatorial interaction-network topology determines qualitative physics: the gradient is necessary but not sufficient**
Opened: this cycle
Description: Kankaria et al. (2603.19180) demonstrate via DNS that decimating the triadic interaction network of 3D Navier-Stokes — while preserving the equation's form — systematically suppresses intermittency and eliminates anomalous dissipation. Structure-function exponents collapse to K41 dimensional values, the multifractal spectrum contracts, and the velocity field regularizes. The gradient dynamics (Navier-Stokes equation) is identical in full and decimated systems; what differs is the *combinatorial density and topology* of Fourier-mode couplings. The framework's Field primitive ẍ = −∇φ(x) captures the gradient dynamics but is completely blind to the interaction-network topology. This is distinct from T046 (combinatorial dimension in matrix vs. tensor models, which is a mathematical physics result about large-N limits) because T070 concerns a concrete physical system (turbulence) with direct numerical evidence. It is distinct from T018 (algebra of computation, which concerns the organizational structure of computational operations) because T070 concerns not how operations compose but which *subsets of modes are permitted to interact* — the topology of the coupling graph, not the algebra of the coupling. It is distinct from T029 (anomalous transport universality classes) because T029 concerns fluctuation statistics during transport, while T070 concerns the *existence* of anomalous scaling as a function of interaction-network completeness. The specific finding that anomalous dissipation vanishes means the K41 theory (which follows from dimensional analysis of the gradient equation alone) is *correct* when the interaction network is sparse — the gradient equation is sufficient in the decimated case. The deviation from K41 (intermittency, anomalous dissipation) arises *solely* from the combinatorial richness of the full interaction network. The gradient equation is necessary but not sufficient for the qualitative physics of turbulence.
Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) extend the Field primitive to include the interaction-network topology as a structural parameter alongside the gradient — effectively specifying not just ẍ = −∇φ(x) but also "which modes couple to which" — which would require the primitive to become a network-theoretic object rather than a differential equation; (b) derive the interaction-network structure from the existing primitives, which is impossible since the gradient equation does not specify it (the gradient is the same for full and decimated systems); or (c) add a new primitive governing the combinatorial structure of mode couplings, which would be a major structural change. The continuous dependence of intermittency suppression on decimation fraction means this is not a threshold effect but a graded phenomenon: the qualitative physics varies continuously with network density, and the framework has no continuous parameter to track this variation. The result also has implications for the framework's dark energy claim: if the "computational cost of new substrate" interpretation is correct, the rate of computational expansion should depend on the interaction-network structure, not just the gradient dynamics — but the framework cannot express this dependence.

**T071 — The unfalsifiability-insufficiency trap: the framework's core claim is simultaneously too specific and too vague**
Opened: this cycle
Description: Paper 1 (Hoffreumon & Woods 2025) proves that the number field of quantum theory (ℝ vs. ℂ) is experimentally underdetermined for all finite operational procedures. Paper 2 (Kankaria et al. 2025) proves via DNS that the gradient dynamics (Navier-Stokes equation) is identical for full and decimated turbulence while the qualitative physics differs completely. Together, these create a trap for the framework's core claim "reality is literally computation": (a) if reality is literally a computation, that computation must have definite structure, including a number field — but the number field is provably unknowable, making the ontological claim unfalsifiable at its most basic structural level; (b) even granting a definite computational structure, the framework's dynamical content (gradient descent, conservation) does not determine the qualitative physics without additional structural specification (interaction-network topology) that no primitive captures — making the framework's predictions insufficient. The claim is too specific (asserts a definite structure that cannot be verified) and too vague (its dynamical content does not determine the physics). This is distinct from T069 (which noted the number field problem in isolation), T070 (which noted the interaction-network problem in isolation), and T021 (which noted ontological pluralism across mathematical categories). T071 concerns the *joint* diagnosis: the framework cannot be falsified at the level of basic structure (Paper 1) and cannot determine physics at the level of dynamics (Paper 2), leaving it in the worst possible epistemic position for a theory claiming to be the literal ontology of reality.
Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) demonstrate that operationally equivalent computations are ontologically identical (adopting a purely operational ontology, which abandons "reality is literally computation" in favor of "reality is literally the equivalence class of operationally indistinguishable computations" — a significantly weaker and less interesting claim), (b) find a criterion beyond experiment for selecting computational structure (aesthetic, self-consistency, minimality — all of which are metaphysical rather than physical), or (c) add structural primitives (interaction-network topology, number field) that the existing five lack, while acknowledging these cannot be empirically selected. None of these preserves the framework's distinctive character.

**T072 — Continuous structural parameters: the framework has no mechanism for graded qualitative change**
Opened: this cycle
Description: Paper 2 demonstrates that the qualitative character of turbulence — intermittent vs. Gaussian, anomalous vs. normal dissipation, multifractal vs. monofractal — varies *continuously* with the decimation fraction of the triadic interaction network. The framework's primitives are all qualitative categories: resolved/unresolved, attractor/saddle, conserved/not conserved. Phase transitions (attractor landscape restructuring) are the framework's only mechanism for qualitative change, and they are described as topological events (minima merge, split, appear, vanish). But Paper 2 shows a qualitative change (turbulent → Gaussian) that is continuous, graded, and controlled by a structural parameter (network density) with no discontinuity, no phase transition, and no attractor landscape restructuring. The framework has no mechanism for describing how qualitative physical character varies continuously with a structural parameter. This is distinct from T017 (continuous sharpening of criticality, which concerns phase transitions becoming sharper with system size) because T072 concerns a continuous qualitative change that is not a phase transition at all — it is a graded structural modification of the dynamical coupling. It is distinct from T070 (which identifies the interaction-network gap) because T072 concerns the *continuous* nature of the dependence, which creates a specific problem beyond mere insufficiency: the framework cannot represent a continuous dial between qualitatively different physics.
Why unresolved: The framework would need either (a) a continuous structural parameter (like network density) as an input to its primitives, which would require specifying how the primitives' outputs depend on this parameter — effectively making the framework's predictions functions of structural inputs rather than consequences of universal equations; (b) a theory of how gradient dynamics on a network generates qualitatively different statistics as the network's connectivity varies, which is essentially the theory of turbulence and does not currently exist; or (c) acceptance that the framework's qualitative categories (attractor/saddle, resolved/unresolved) are idealizations that do not capture the graded structural dependence of real physical systems. Option (c) would be honest but would limit the framework to a coarse-grained description that misses the physically interesting variation.

**T073 — Modular invariance as organizational principle: the perturbative/non-perturbative distinction is structurally dissolved**
Opened: this cycle
Description: Brown, Dorigoni & Wen (2603.20083) demonstrate that giant graviton integrated correlators in 𝒩=4 SYM have large-N expansion coefficients that are non-holomorphic Eisenstein series — modular forms encoding the full SL(2,ℤ) duality symmetry of the complexified coupling τ. This means the perturbative expansion (powers of g²_YM) and non-perturbative contributions (instantons, anti-instantons) are not independent computational processes but are intertwined by modular transformations that exchange strong and weak coupling. The framework's implicit computational picture — things are computed perturbatively, with non-perturbative corrections representing tunneling between attractor basins — is structurally wrong for this system. The organizational principle is modular invariance, which treats perturbative and non-perturbative contributions as facets of a single modular object. The framework has no primitive for symmetries that act on the coupling constant itself (as opposed to symmetries of the fields), no mechanism for explaining why the coupling constant space has SL(2,ℤ) symmetry, and no vocabulary for describing how this symmetry organizes the complete computational content. The Eisenstein series are eigenfunctions of the hyperbolic Laplacian on the SL(2,ℤ) fundamental domain — their number-theoretic properties (divisor sum Fourier coefficients, functional equations) determine the physical content. This is number-theoretic organization of physics that no primitive can express. The doubly non-perturbative structure (non-perturbative in both coupling and N) further complicates the picture: the computational content is organized along two independent non-perturbative axes, each with its own modular/number-theoretic structure.
Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) incorporate modular invariance as a structural principle governing how computational content at different coupling strengths is related, which would require a theory of how the framework's "computational substrate" respects or generates SL(2,ℤ) symmetry — but the coupling constant is not a substrate parameter in the usual sense, and modular invariance is not a conservation law; (b) derive the Eisenstein series structure from some combination of Conservation of Computation and the other primitives, which appears impossible since the modular group is an arithmetic object with no dynamical origin in the framework; or (c) accept that the organizational structure of non-perturbative physics is governed by number-theoretic symmetry that the framework is constitutively unable to address, further deepening T026 and T059. The universality between SU(N) and U(N) in the coupling-dependent sector is additionally unexplained: the framework would treat the extra U(1) as a structural difference, but the physics doesn't care.

**T074 — Supersymmetry gap rigidity: attractor classification is determined by algebraic deformation theory, not by dynamical principles**
Opened: this cycle
Description: Di Bella, de Graaf & Santi (2603.19923) prove that 11d supergravity backgrounds with >26 Killing spinors, 4-form rank ≤ 6, and Euclidean support must be Minkowski or AdS₇×S⁴. The proof uses the bijective correspondence between filtered deformations of Lie superalgebras and highly supersymmetric backgrounds. The framework's Attractor primitive describes basins in potential landscapes and claims that basin structure determines what a system can produce. This paper shows the opposite: the *algebraic* structure of Lie superalgebra deformations determines which basins *can exist*. The classification is not "which basins does the potential landscape have?" but "which filtered deformations does the algebra admit?" — and the physical answer (Minkowski or AdS₇×S⁴) follows from the algebraic answer (no non-trivial deformations above the threshold). The threshold dimension 26 is determined by representation-theoretic constraints on Spin(10,1) spinors, the rank condition by the Grassmannian orbit structure of the 4

**T075 — Precision-Induced Irreversibility: the arrow of time does not require Resolution**
Opened: this cycle
Description: Foa Torres et al. (2603.22284) demonstrate that an operational arrow of time emerges from non-normal linear dynamics with finite dynamic range, requiring no quantum mechanics, no entanglement, no nonlinearity, and no measurement. The framework attributes the arrow of time exclusively to the accumulation of resolved quantum states (Resolution primitive). PIR shows a second, independent arrow arising from representational incapacity: mathematically invertible evolution becomes practically irreversible when finite precision cannot represent the amplified state components needed for reversal. The predictability horizon τ_p scales linearly with available precision (number of significant digits) divided by the amplification rate (determined by the pseudospectral structure of the non-normal operator). This is not a quantum phenomenon — it occurs in fully classical, fully resolved, fully deterministic systems. The three necessary ingredients (amplification, non-normality, finite dynamic range) are individually insufficient; all three are jointly necessary and sufficient. The framework faces a trilemma: (a) expand Resolution to include classical precision loss, which trivializes the primitive by removing its quantum character; (b) acknowledge multiple independent arrows of time, which falsifies "the arrow of time is nothing other than the accumulation of resolved states"; (c) classify PIR as a substrate effect (finite dynamic range = finite substrate precision), which implicates the substrate in generating the arrow of time — a role reserved for Resolution. Option (c) is most promising but requires the substrate to play an active role in irreversibility beyond providing the tick rate, which is a significant expansion of the substrate concept.
Why unresolved: The framework's entire temporal architecture is built on Resolution as the unique source of irreversibility. PIR provides a clean, quantitative counterexample operating in a domain (classical, linear, deterministic) where Resolution has no role. Resolving this requires either restructuring the framework's account of the arrow of time to accommodate multiple sources, or demonstrating that PIR is somehow a special case of Resolution (which seems impossible given that PIR requires no quantum mechanics). The non-normality condition is algebraic structure the framework cannot express (T026), meaning even classifying when PIR occurs is beyond the framework's current capacity.

**T076 — The real computation of confinement is ontologically unreal by the framework's criterion**
Opened: this cycle
Description: Comitini (2603.22181) demonstrates that the gluon mass — the physical quantity responsible for color confinement — is generated through a nonperturbative Higgs-Schwinger mechanism involving a composite Goldstone boson that is a bound-state superposition of two-gluon, three-gluon, and ghost-antighost pairs. The ghost fields, the Kugo-Ojima charge, and the Goldstone boson are all permanently virtual: they never appear as asymptotic (resolvable) states. The broken symmetry (Kugo-Ojima) is itself unphysical. The corrected color charge remains unbroken. The entire mechanism — from symmetry breaking to mass generation to confinement — operates through computational structure that the framework classifies as ontologically unreal (because it never resolves). Yet the output (gluon mass, confinement, the visible mass of the universe) is as real as any physical quantity. This is not merely another instance of T041 (permanently virtual structure having effects) — it is the sharpest possible instance: the computation generating the most fundamental mass in nature is ontologically "unreal" by the framework's criterion. The framework must either (a) abandon the identification of "real" with "resolvable," which undermines the Resolution primitive's ontological role; (b) argue that the gluon mass is "real" but its origin is "unreal," which is incoherent; or (c) expand the ontology to include a category of "constitutively virtual but physically generative" computation that is neither resolved nor awaiting resolution but is the permanent computational infrastructure from which resolvable physics emerges.
Why unresolved: Option (c) is the most promising but would represent a fundamental restructuring: the framework would need three ontological categories (resolved, unresolved-but-resolvable, permanently-virtual-but-generative) instead of two, and Conservation of Computation would need to account for computational content in all three categories. The ghost-antighost contribution is especially sharp because ghosts are introduced as mathematical devices to maintain gauge invariance — they are "computational bookkeeping" — yet they participate essentially in a physical mechanism. The framework has no account of how bookkeeping becomes physical.

Description: Paper 1 demonstrates that non-normality — the property that an operator does not commute with its adjoint — is the decisive structural feature enabling precision-induced irreversibility. Non-normal and normal operators with identical eigenvalue spectra produce qualitatively different physics: the non-normal system develops an arrow of time while the normal system does not.
