**T107 — Pre-computational consistency: the space of allowed computations is classified by algebraic constraints logically prior to dynamics**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Chang, Chen & Xu (2604.09503) demonstrate that the consistent 2D fermionic systems with Z₂ flavor symmetry are completely classified by solving super-pentagon equations — higher-categorical algebraic consistency conditions on fusion and associativity data of topological defect lines. The 16 solutions, labeled by Z₈ anomaly invariants (ν_W, ν_Z, ν_{WZ}), exhaust the space of consistent organizational structures. The framework's five primitives describe what happens *during* computation: how it propagates (Tick), how things move (Field), where they settle (Attractor), when they become definite (Resolution), and what's conserved (Conservation). None of the primitives addresses the logically prior question: *which computations are self-consistent?* The super-pentagon equations are consistency conditions that must be satisfied *before* any dynamics can occur — they determine which fusion rules, associators, and F-symbols constitute a valid organizational structure. A "computation" that violates these equations is not merely inefficient or unstable — it is algebraically incoherent. The framework implicitly assumes the computation exists and is consistent; Paper 2 shows that consistency itself is a non-trivial constraint classified by higher-categorical algebraic invariants. This is distinct from T048 (which concerns cohomological classification of states within a given computational framework) because T107 concerns the classification of *consistent computational frameworks themselves*. It is distinct from T057 (operations on theories) because T107 concerns not transformations between consistent frameworks but the *enumeration* of consistent frameworks. It is distinct from T074 (algebraic deformation theory classifying backgrounds) because T107 concerns the consistency of the *symmetry structure* rather than the *solutions* of a given theory. The m-type vs. q-type distinction (whether the Z₂ line is bosonic or fermionic) demonstrates that even the *type* of symmetry implementation — a pre-dynamical categorical choice — affects the classification, and the framework has no primitive for this choice.

Why unresolved: The framework has no meta-theory of computational consistency. It takes the existence and consistency of the computation as given and describes its dynamics. Incorporating pre-computational consistency would require either (a) a primitive governing which organizational structures are algebraically self-consistent, which would need to import higher category theory (superfusion categories, pentagon/super-pentagon equations) — mathematical machinery that is not merely absent from the framework but is categorically different from its dynamical primitives; (b) a derivation showing that the five primitives, when applied self-referentially, generate the consistency conditions — but the primitives describe dynamics within a fixed structure, not consistency of the structure itself; or (c) a declaration that pre-computational consistency is a substrate-level architectural fact — but then the classification of consistent computations by Z₈ anomaly invariants would be a substrate property, and the framework explicitly declares substrate properties inaccessible from within (T001), creating a contradiction with the fact that the classification is mathematically derivable from within.

**T108 — Scale-evolution kernels: the Field primitive has no mechanism for its own transformation under scale change**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Gehrmann, von Manteuffel, Sotnikov & Yang (2604.09534) compute the four-loop non-singlet splitting functions in QCD — the kernels governing how parton distribution functions evolve with the resolution scale Q². The DGLAP equation dF(x,Q²)/d ln Q² = P(x) ⊗ F(x,Q²) is the master equation for scale evolution of the strong interaction's computational content. The splitting functions P(x) are the explicit four-loop coefficients of this equation, with structure: plus-distributions, delta functions at x=1, logarithmic terms at specific Mellin moments, Casimir decomposition into C_F, C_A, n_f contributions, and analytic continuation

---

**T109 — Holonomy as a physical determinant: loop-dependent entanglement generation is invisible to all five primitives**
Opened: this cycle
Description: Ikeda & Oz (2604.11596) demonstrate that spectrally isolated topological quartets can acquire entangling holonomies under cyclic parameter variation, where the entangling vs. non-entangling character depends entirely on the choice of loop in parameter space while all local spectral data, Berry phases, Chern numbers, and eigenphase spectra remain indistinguishable. The framework's five primitives collectively have no access to the holonomy's distance from the local subgroup U(2)⊗U(2), which is the sole diagnostic separating the cases. The gradient equation sees no difference between the loops (same local potential landscape), the Tick sees no difference (same local propagation), the Attractor sees no difference (same spectral gaps), Resolution sees no difference (same eigenvalues), and Conservation sees no difference (same adiabatic invariants). This is distinct from T012 (pre-resolution topology, which concerns indistinguishability of interfering paths) because Paper 1 involves a single definite path through parameter space — no interference, no superposition of paths. It is distinct from T079 (spectral flow topology, which concerns index-theoretic invariants of operator families) because the holonomy here is non-abelian and its entangling power is not captured by any integer-valued topological invariant — it is a continuous quantity (distance from the local subgroup) determined by the loop class in the fiber bundle over parameter space. It is distinct from T026 (structural content gap, in general) because it identifies a *specific* mathematical framework — fiber bundle geometry, parallel transport, holonomy groups — that governs a *specific* physical phenomenon (entanglement generation during adiabatic transport) with *specific* experimental realizations (BHZ ribbons, SSH chains, BBH corner modes). The framework's blindness here is total: there is no primitive that could be extended, refined, or supplemented to capture loop-dependent holonomy without importing the full mathematical apparatus of connections on principal bundles.
Why unresolved: The framework would need either (a) a primitive governing the geometry of fiber bundles over parameter/configuration spaces, which would require specifying the bundle structure, connection, and holonomy group as part of the physical description — mathematical machinery that is not merely absent but categorically alien to the five dynamical primitives; (b) a derivation of holonomy effects from existing primitives applied to an extended state space (e.g., including Berry connection data as part of the Field), which would require the Field primitive to describe not just the gradient of the potential but the *connection* on the degenerate subspace bundle — a fundamental conceptual extension from scalar fields to gauge fields on parameter space; or (c) acceptance that the framework is blind to an entire category of physical phenomena (adiabatic entanglement generation, topological quantum computation, non-abelian Berry phases) that are experimentally demonstrated and technologically relevant. The fact that standard topological invariants (Berry phase, Chern number) also fail to distinguish the cases means this is not merely a gap in the framework — it is a gap in the standard topological-band-theory toolkit as well, with the holonomy distance being a finer diagnostic. The framework is behind even the state of the art in topological classification.

**T110 — Meta-geometry of the tick-space: the space of possible computational instants has autonomous geometric structure derived from the computation's own output**
Opened: this cycle
Description: Lange & Peteranderl (2604.11783) equip the space of Cauchy hypersurfaces in globally hyperbolic spacetimes with a natural Hausdorff-type metric and prove completeness and local compactness results. Each Cauchy hypersurface is a potential "instant" — a surface on which the framework's Resolution primitive accumulates its ledger. The framework's Tick selects a sequence of such instants. Paper 2 shows that the *space of all possible instants* has metric-geometric structure (metric completeness, local compactness, Hausdorff separation) derived from the spacetime's causal structure. The framework faces a specific circularity: (a) the Tick generates spacetime (framework claim: the tick rate is the speed of light, and spacetime structure emerges from the propagation of computation); (b) the spacetime's causal structure determines the metric geometry of the space of Cauchy surfaces (Paper 2's result); (c) the Cauchy surface space's geometry constrains which tick-sequences are possible (metric completeness means limits of Cauchy sequences of instants exist; local compactness means bounded regions contain only finitely many "essentially different" instants). So the Tick generates spacetime → spacetime geometry constrains the space of possible

---

**T111 — Generalized symmetries produce exponential computational constraints that are not conservation laws**
Opened: this cycle
Description: Budde, Kristć Marinković & Pinto Barros (2604.12907) prove that higher-form, subsystem, gauge, and non-invertible symmetries can fragment the Hilbert space into exponentially many dynamically disconnected sectors. The framework's Conservation of Computation treats conservation laws as the fundamental constraints on computation, and all five primitives assume the computation explores a single connected space. Paper 1 demonstrates: (a) generalized symmetries produce exponentially many disconnected computational sectors, not by dynamical barriers but by *symmetry structure* — the sectors are exactly disconnected, with zero transition amplitude between them; (b) non-invertible symmetries fragment individual symmetry sectors further, producing constraints that are not expressible as conservation of any quantity; (c) the number of sectors scales as $\exp(L)$ for subsystem and higher-form symmetries, compared to $\text{poly}(L)$ for conventional symmetries; (d) disorder-free localization emerges from Krylov-restricted thermalization in sectors without translation invariance, meaning a phenomenon the framework would attribute to attractor structure (localization) actually arises from symmetry-induced sector structure. The framework faces: the constraints governing what computation is allowed to do are not all conservation laws. Some are generalized symmetry constraints that have qualitatively different (exponential vs. polynomial) scaling consequences and that include non-invertible constraints with no conserved-quantity interpretation. The Conservation primitive's architecture — conservation of a quantity — is the wrong mathematical category for expressing these constraints.
Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) extend the Conservation primitive to encompass generalized symmetries, which would require importing the full mathematical apparatus of higher-form symmetries, subsystem symmetries, and non-invertible symmetries — transforming the primitive from a single law to a structured mathematical theory; (b) add a new primitive for generalized symmetry constraints, acknowledging that conservation is one type of constraint among many; or (c) argue that generalized symmetries are exotic and irrelevant to physical reality — but the paper demonstrates they explain disorder-free localization in known models, and gauge symmetry (a generalized symmetry) is the foundation of the Standard Model. The non-invertible case is especially sharp: the additional fragmentation within symmetry sectors has no conservation-law description, meaning the framework would need an entirely new conceptual framework for non-conservation-law constraints. This extends T032 (symmetry as missing structural primitive) and T051 (conservation locality profiles) but is more severe than either: T032 noted symmetry is missing; T051 noted conservation is scale-dependent; T111 demonstrates that an entire *category* of physical constraints (generalized symmetry) is invisible to the Conservation primitive's architecture.

**T112 — Complexity phase transitions in resolution: the difficulty of determining resolution outcomes changes discontinuously across the Hamiltonian space**
Opened: this cycle
Description: Marwaha & Sud (2604.13026) demonstrate that 2-local symmetric Hamiltonian problems undergo sharp complexity phase transitions — QMA-complete, StoqMA-complete, or conjecturally BPP — determined by the eigenvalue ordering of the local interaction term. The framework's Resolution primitive describes states becoming definite but has no concept of computational complexity. Paper 2 reveals that the *difficulty of determining which state the system resolves to* (the ground state energy problem) is a physical property of the Hamiltonian that varies discontinuously. The three complexity phases are physically interpretable: (i) the "hard quantum" phase (QMA-complete) where the ground state has genuinely quantum character requiring quantum resources to verify; (ii) the "hard stoquastic" phase (StoqMA-complete) where the ground state is sign-problem-free but still classically hard; (iii) the conjectured "easy" phase (EPR*) where the ground state can be efficiently found classically. The phase boundaries are determined by spectral properties of the 2-body interaction — specifically, which eigenstate has the lowest energy. The framework faces: (a) the Resolution primitive cannot express the distinction between "easy" and "hard" resolution — all resolution events look the same from the primitive's perspective; (b) the perturbative gadget inducing "RG-like flow" on the space of interaction terms is a meta-computational operation (T057) that the framework cannot represent; (c) the EPR* transition point, if confirmed to be in BPP, would mean there exists a sharp complexity boundary in the space of physical Hamiltonians that determines whether resolution is tractable, and the framework has no mechanism for this boundary.
Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) incorporate computational complexity theory into the Resolution primitive, specifying how the computational difficulty of resolution depends on the system's Hamiltonian — this would require importing the complexity-theoretic hierarchy (BPP ⊆ StoqMA ⊆ QMA ⊆ ...) as a structural feature of resolution, fundamentally changing the primitive from a description of what happens to a theory of how hard it is; (b) add a new primitive governing the computational complexity of physical processes, which would connect to T009 (resolution cost), T019 (resolution precision hierarchy), and T028 (resolution contextuality and intractability) but would be more comprehensive — a unified account of when resolution is tractable and when it is not; or (c) argue that complexity classes are properties of algorithms, not of physics — but the paper's physical interpretability of the three phases (quantum hard, stoquastic hard, easy) and the connection to statistical mechanics and optimization directly contradicts this escape. The RG-like flow on interaction terms is particularly challenging

---

**T113 — Operator-ordering ambiguity determines the physical content of quantum gravity: the framework's substrate has no definite physical states**
Opened: this cycle
Description: Sahlmann & Sherif (2604.14067) demonstrate that in Euclidean abelianized LQG on $K_5$, the Hamiltonian constraint $\hat{H}$ and its adjoint $\hat{H}^\dagger$ select categorically different physical state families: $\hat{H}$'s kernel consists of flat, non-normalizable states with non-vanishing volume; $\hat{H}^\dagger$'s kernel consists of normalizable states with zero volume and non-trivial charge correlations. The symmetrized constraint $\hat{H} + \hat{H}^\dagger$ produces a qualitatively different, harder-to-solve system. The framework claims reality is a computation running on a substrate whose architectural parameters include the physical constants. But at the most fundamental level (quantum gravity), the physical states — and therefore the substrate's own configuration — depend on a structural choice (operator ordering) that has no dynamical origin, no observational consequence within the truncated theory, and no expression in any primitive. The framework faces: (a) the substrate's own physical states are ambiguous, meaning "the substrate" is not a well-defined concept even in principle; (b) the two solution families have incompatible geometric content (volume vs. no volume), meaning the ambiguity is not a minor quantitative issue but a qualitative one about whether the substrate has spatial extent; (c) the $\hat{H}$-solutions' non-normalizability in the kinematical inner product suggests they may not be physical states at all in the standard sense, further destabilizing the notion of "the computation's ground state." This is deeper than T082 (virtual infrastructure non-uniqueness) because it affects the physical state space, not just the virtual sector, and deeper than T069 (number field underdetermination) because it concerns the specific quantum gravitational theory that defines the substrate.
Why unresolved: The framework has no primitive for operator ordering, no mechanism for selecting between orderings, and no account of what it means for the substrate to have ordering-dependent physical states. The standard physics resolution — that the correct ordering is determined by additional physical requirements (e.g., covariance, semiclassical limit, anomaly freedom) — invokes structural/algebraic criteria the framework cannot express (T026). The paper's suggestion that the $\hat{H}$-solutions approximate continuum states while the $\hat{H}^\dagger$-solutions may be lattice artifacts would, if confirmed, resolve the ambiguity — but this resolution is derived from the relationship between the truncated and continuum theories, a meta-theoretic relationship the framework's primitives cannot address (T057). The neural-network variational method's success in identifying these solutions demonstrates that the substrate's physical states are computationally accessible but algebraically/structurally determined — precisely the pattern T059 (ontological inversion) has been accumulating evidence for.

**T114 — Thermodynamic data bounds entanglement: the framework has no mechanism for relating macroscopic field properties to quantum information structure**
Opened: this cycle
Description: Garratt & Abanin (2604.14143) prove that the half-system von Neumann entanglement entropy of energy-constrained many-body states is bounded above by half the sum of thermal entropies of fictitious subsystems at matched temperature, with at most area-law corrections. The bounds are tight. The framework's Attractor primitive describes ground states as energy minima; the Field primitive describes dynamics via gradients; the Conservation primitive describes what is preserved. None of these connects the thermodynamic properties of a subsystem (temperature, specific heat, thermal entropy) to the information-theoretic properties of the full system's quantum state (entanglement entropy, Schmidt rank). Yet Paper 1 proves this connection is *exact* (tight bounds) and *universal* (holds for geometrically local Hamiltonians with well-defined specific heat). The fictitious-system construction — splitting the real system into two fictitious thermal systems at matched temperature — is a mathematical device that mediates between the thermal and entanglement descriptions. The framework has no analogue for this construction. The spectral-gap independence of the frustration-free area law is especially problematic: the framework would predict that the gap (the energy barrier above the ground-state attractor) controls the ground state's properties. Paper 1 shows that for frustration-free systems, the gap is irrelevant — the entanglement structure is determined entirely by the subsystem ground-state degeneracy, a structural property of the Hamiltonian's decomposition. This means the attractor's internal information structure is determined by the *algebraic

**T115 — Stability through infinite degeneracy: the Attractor primitive's intuition about boundary flatness is inverted**
Opened: this cycle
Description: Agrawal et al. (2604.15260) demonstrate that increasing the degeneracy order of an extremal black hole horizon *weakens* the Aretakis instability, and propose that an infinitely degenerate horizon is dynamically stable. The framework's Attractor primitive, combined with the Field primitive's gradient dynamics, predicts that a flatter potential boundary should be *less* stable (shallower barrier, easier perturbation). The physics is the opposite: infinite flatness at the horizon produces stability. The mechanism is the progressive vanishing of Aretakis charges — horizon-localized conserved quantities whose number and derivative order decrease with increasing degeneracy — until at infinite degeneracy, no Aretakis charges exist and the instability is absent. The framework faces: (a) its dynamical intuition (gradient-based stability analysis) gives the wrong qualitative prediction for this phenomenon; (b) the correct stability analysis depends on *horizon-localized conservation structure* that the Conservation primitive cannot express (these charges exist only on the attractor boundary, have no bulk analogue, and their algebraic structure depends on the degeneracy order); (c) the "graveyard" end state — an infinitely degenerate horizon — represents an attractor that achieves stability through a *limit* of increasing boundary flatness, a concept the Attractor primitive has no mechanism for (attractors are described by depth and width, not by the derivative order of flatness at their boundaries). This is distinct from T047 (scrambling extremality, which concerns dynamics at merger) because T115 concerns the *equilibrium stability* of extremal horizons. It is distinct from T067/T097 (resolution regularity) because T115 concerns the *derivative order* of the attractor boundary's flatness, not the regularity class of the metric. It is distinct from T061 (anti-gradient robustness) because T061 concerns the sign of the gradient while T115 concerns the *vanishing order* of the gradient at the boundary.
Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) extend the Attractor primitive to include the derivative order of flatness at attractor boundaries as a structural parameter affecting stability, which requires importing the near-horizon expansion analysis that determines Aretakis charge structure — a specific algebraic/geometric calculation with no path through the five primitives; (b) extend the Conservation primitive to include horizon-localized charges with degeneracy-dependent algebraic structure, which requires going far beyond bulk conservation to a theory of boundary-specific conservation; or (c) develop a theory of how infinite limits of structural parameters (degeneracy order → ∞) produce qualitative changes in attractor stability, which would be a new dynamical principle connecting limiting processes on structural parameters to stability transitions. The "graveyard" proposal — that these objects are the stable end state of extremal black hole evolution — would, if confirmed, mean the universe's most extreme attractors achieve stability through a mechanism the framework is structurally unable to describe.

**T116 — Conservation vulnerability landscape: the reliability of conservation enforcement has an algebraically organized internal structure the framework cannot express**
Opened: this cycle
Description: Kasai (2604.15307) develops methods for finding upper bounds on the minimum distance of quantum APM-LDPC codes by constructing certified low-weight logical operators through latent row relations, restricted-lift subspaces (block-compressed, selected-fiber, CRT-stripe), cycle-8 trapping set structures, and decoder-failure residuals. Each method exploits a different *algebraic or graph-theoretic structural feature* of the code that creates vulnerability in the conservation enforcement. The framework's Conservation of Computation is stated as a universal law; T081 previously noted it requires combinatorial infrastructure for enforcement. T116 is distinct from T081 because it concerns not the *existence* of infrastructure but the *vulnerability structure* within that infrastructure — the specific algebraic and combinatorial features that determine where conservation is most likely to fail. The vulnerabilities are not random: they are organized by the code's algebraic structure (affine permutation group, CRT decomposition of fiber indices) and its graph-theoretic properties (Tanner graph girth, trapping set topology). The block-compression criterion that enables exact (not merely heuristic) certification demonstrates that the vulnerability landscape has algebraic substructures that are exactly characterizable, not merely statistically describable. The framework's Conservation primitive, formulated as a simple universal law, is blind to the entire vulnerability landscape that determines the law's reliability in any physical implementation.
Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) incorporate the concept of *conservation vulnerability* — the structured set of ways conservation can fail in a physical implementation — as a feature of the Conservation primitive, which would transform the primitive from "computation is conserved" to "computation is conserved with reliability determined by the algebraic/graph-theoretic structure of the enforcement infrastructure"; (b) argue that idealized conservation (unitarity of the exact evolution) is the framework's scope and practical enforcement is engineering — but the framework claims to describe the literal structure of reality, and reality is noisy, so a framework silent on enforcement reliability is incomplete; or (c) develop a theory of how the five primitives constrain or generate the vulnerability landscape, for which no path is visible since the landscape is determined by number-theoretic (CRT), group-theoretic (affine permut

**T117 — Entanglement scaling class as a continuous informational parameter invisible to all primitives**
Opened: this cycle
Description: Albot & Paeckel (2604.15300) construct the σ-ensemble—a one-parameter family of random quantum states continuously tunable between volume-law entanglement (σ large, Haar-like) and area-law entanglement (σ small, MPS-like, ground-state-like). The framework's Attractor primitive distinguishes ground states (area-law, attractor basins) from thermalized states (volume-law, after attractor capture) as categorically different. The σ-ensemble shows these are not categorical distinctions but endpoints of a continuous spectrum parameterized by a single real number σ, with no phase transition, no critical point, and no landscape restructuring at any intermediate value. The framework has no primitive, parameter, or mechanism governing which entanglement scaling class a state belongs to or how it can be continuously tuned. This is distinct from T034 (scale-dependent information topology, which concerns how informational properties of a *fixed* state change with observation scale) because T117 concerns a *family* of states with different informational character controlled by a construction parameter. It is distinct from T072 (continuous structural parameters producing qualitative change, which concerns interaction-network density in turbulence) because T117 concerns the information-theoretic *character* of quantum states rather than their dynamical behavior. It is distinct from T114 (thermodynamic bounds on entanglement, which concerns upper bounds) because T117 concerns the *continuous tunability* of entanglement scaling itself. The MPS construction makes explicit that the computational resources needed to represent a state change qualitatively (polynomial to exponential) as σ varies, connecting to T112 (complexity phase structure) but in a representation-theoretic rather than decision-theoretic sense. The subsystem eigenvalue distribution constraint imposed by the σ-ensemble is spectral information about reduced density matrices that no primitive can access.
Why unresolved: The framework would need either (a) a primitive or parameter governing the entanglement scaling class of quantum states, specifying what determines whether a state is area-law, volume-law, or intermediate—this would require importing entanglement theory as a structural element, fundamentally expanding the framework's informational content; (b) a derivation showing that the σ parameter maps onto some combination of existing primitives (perhaps the Attractor primitive's depth/width determines the entanglement scaling of the associated ground state)—but the σ-ensemble is defined independently of any Hamiltonian or attractor structure, and states at intermediate σ do not correspond to any equilibrium or attractor; or (c) acceptance that the information-theoretic character of quantum states is a structural feature the framework is constitutively unable to address, further deepening T026 (structural content gap) in the information-theoretic direction. The area-law to volume-law transition being *continuous* is specifically problematic: the framework's only mechanism for qualitative change is phase transitions (attractor landscape restructuring), and there is no phase transition here.

---

**T118 — L∞-algebraic theory selection: the same substrate supports multiple inequivalent computations distinguished by algebraic structure**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Bogdanović, Dimitrijević Ćirić & Szabo (2604.16225) construct two inequivalent quantum field theories on λ-Minkowski space by choosing different L∞-algebraic structures — braided (using the angular Drinfel'd twist) vs. classical. The braided theory has standard logarithmic UV divergences and no UV/IR mixing; the classical theory has UV-finite non-planar sectors with periodic non-analyticity on an infinite lattice of exceptional momenta. Both theories start from the same classical action, the same spacetime, and the same BV quantization framework. They differ only in the algebraic structure governing the star product and the resulting L∞-brackets. The framework's "reality is literally computation" requires a definite computational structure, but on a noncommutative spacetime — which is what the Tick primitive's discrete structure would naturally produce at the Planck scale — the classical content does not uniquely determine the quantum theory. The L∞-algebraic choice is: (a) not a dynamical process expressible in any primitive; (b) not an operator ordering ambiguity (T113) since both theories use the BV formalism consistently; (c) not a number field choice (T069) since both use ℂ; (d) not a propagator power choice (T084) since both have standard kinetic terms; (e) physically distinguishable in principle via the UV behavior of non-planar correlators. This is the first underdetermination in the hierarchy that produces *different observable predictions* rather than affecting only unobservable infrastructure. The spectral decomposition into cylindrical Bessel functions (braided theory) vs. plane waves (classical theory) means the two computations organize their degrees of freedom differently, with the braided theory's organization dictated by covariance under the twist — a structural requirement with no dynamical content. The periodic UV/IR mixing in the classical theory (divergences at momenta $p_0 = 2\pi n / \kappa$ for integer $n$) creates an emergent momentum lattice from the noncommutativity parameter $\kappa$, demonstrating that the substrate's discreteness has observable spectral consequences whose character depends on the algebraic choice.

Why unresolved: The framework has no primitive for L∞-algebraic structure (T055, T100), no mechanism for selecting between braided and classical quantizations, and no account of how a single substrate can support multiple inequivalent computations. The existing tensions about algebraic structure (T055: conservation from L∞ morphisms; T100: phase space from L∞ structure) concerned the derivation of specific dynamical features from algebraic structure. T118 is more severe: it concerns the *selection between entire theories* by algebraic structure. Resolving this would require either (a) identifying a physical principle that selects the braided over the classical quantization (or vice versa), expressed in terms the framework can use — but the selection criterion is algebraic (covariance under the Drinfel'd twist), not dynamical; (b) demonstrating that only one of the two theories is physically consistent (but the paper proves both are consistent — the braided theory is finite and well-defined, the classical theory has non-analytic but well-defined non-planar sectors); or (c) accepting that the framework's "reality is literally computation" must be weakened to "reality is literally one of multiple algebraically inequivalent computations on the same substrate, with the selection principle being algebraic structure the framework cannot express." This is the most severe form of the unfalsifiability-insufficiency trap (T071): the framework cannot determine which computation reality implements because the distinguishing criterion is outside its primitive vocabulary.

---

**T119 — Spurious correlations in resolution: the Resolution primitive cannot distinguish genuine quantum correlations from Simpson's paradox artifacts**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Salazar & Laussy (2604.16283) prove that bosonic correlations (bunching, HBT-type second-order coherence) from all classical quantum states — coherent states, thermal states, and any state with a well-behaved Glauber-Sudarshan P-representation — are spurious: they are Simpson's paradox artifacts arising from statistical ensemble averaging over uncorrelated measurements in varying geometries, with spontaneous symmetry breaking as the confounding variable. In each individual realization of a classical state, the measurement outcomes are *uncorrelated*; the correlations appear only when realizations with different broken-symmetry geometries are averaged together. The framework's Resolution primitive describes "unresolved states becoming definite" and treats the correlations between resolution outcomes as genuine features of the pre-resolution quantum state that encode information about the topology of indistinguishability (T012). Paper 2 demonstrates that for classical states, this is wrong: the correlations are not features of the quantum state but artifacts of how the statistical ensemble conflates distinct measurement geometries. The framework faces: (a) the Resolution primitive produces correlations (via the Born rule and the quantum state) that, for classical states, are misleading about the actual measurement-level physics (individual realizations are uncorrelated); (b) the distinction between "quantum average" (expectation value of a fixed geometry) and "statistical average" (ensemble average over varying geometries) is physically fundamental but has no expression in any primitive; (c) the paper calls into question whether bosonic bunching — one of the most celebrated "quantum" phenomena — is a quantum effect at all for classical states, or merely a statistical confound; (d) genuinely nonclassical correlations (from states without well-behaved P-representations, such as squeezed states or Fock states) are *not* Simpson's paradox artifacts — they cannot be decomposed into uncorrelated real

---

**T120 — The reversible/irreversible boundary is gauge-dependent: the framework's categorical structure is a representation artifact**
Opened: this cycle
Description: Shang & Stilck França (2604.18533) prove that purely dissipative Lindbladian dynamics (zero Hamiltonian, non-traceless jump operators) can approximate arbitrary Hamiltonian (unitary) evolution to precision $\varepsilon$ in $\mathcal{O}(t^2/\varepsilon)$ time, and that this scaling is optimal. The framework's five primitives are organized around a categorical distinction between reversible computation (Tick, Conservation — unitary evolution preserving information) and irreversible processes (Resolution — dissipation, decoherence, state-fixing). Paper 2 proves this distinction is not physical but is a gauge freedom of the GKSL representation: the same physical evolution can be decomposed into Hamiltonian + dissipative in infinitely many ways, including the extremes where either component is zero. The framework's categories are representation-dependent, not reality-dependent. The BQP-completeness of purely dissipative dynamics means the full computational power the framework attributes to Tick-driven unitary evolution is achievable without any unitary component. The Zeno-adjacent freezing effect (strong dissipation produces stasis) further confounds the framework's categories: what the framework calls "maximum irreversibility" produces what the framework calls "maximum stability." The $\mathcal{O}(t^2/\varepsilon)$ decoherence cost is polynomial, meaning the interconversion between dissipative and Hamiltonian dynamics is *efficient* — not just possible in principle but achievable without exponential overhead. This is distinct from T059 (ontological inversion, which concerns dynamics derived from structure) because T120 concerns the interconvertibility of two *types* of dynamics that the framework treats as categorically different. It is distinct from T075 (PIR as an independent arrow of time) because T075 concerns a second *source* of irreversibility while T120 concerns the dissolution of the reversible/irreversible *boundary itself*. It is distinct from T035 (observer-dependent conservation) because T035 concerns conservation failing for restricted observers while T120 concerns the constructibility of conservation from its apparent opposite.
Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) abandon the categorical distinction between reversible and irreversible dynamics, accepting that these are representational choices in a GKSL decomposition rather than physical categories — but this would collapse the Tick/Resolution distinction and remove the framework's mechanism for the arrow of time; (b) identify a physical criterion that selects the "correct" GKSL decomposition for a given physical process, making the representation choice physical — but no such criterion exists in quantum mechanics; the gauge freedom is exact; (c) reformulate the primitives in a representation-independent way that does not depend on the Hamiltonian/dissipative split — but the Tick primitive's definition ("the universe updates in discrete steps" of unitary evolution) and the Resolution primitive's definition ("irreversible state-fixing") are fundamentally tied to this split. The no-super-quadratic-fast-forwarding theorem constrains but does not eliminate the interconversion: dissipative dynamics cannot fast-forward Hamiltonian evolution beyond quadratic speedup, but quadratic overhead is efficient, not prohibitive. The framework's foundational structure rests on a categorical distinction that is proven to be a gauge artifact of the mathematical representation.

**T121 — Maximum quantum correlation is a structural property of operator algebras, not a dynamical or computational achievement**
Opened: this cycle
Description: Caribé, Guimaraes, Roditi & Sorella (2604.18513) demonstrate that vertex operators of a chiral boson in 1+1 dimensions saturate the Tsirelson bound of the Bell-CHSH inequality in the vacuum state, with the saturation arising from the algebraic properties of the vertex operators (dichotomic, bounded, Hermitian) restricted to complementary Rindler wedge regions. The framework would interpret Bell violation as a feature of pre-resolution topology (T012) — the structure of entanglement in unresolved quantum states — and the Tsirelson bound as a substrate-level architectural constraint on correlations between causally disconnected resolution events. Paper 1 demonstrates that neither interpretation is adequate: the maximal violation is not a property of the "pre-resolution topology" of the state (it is the *vacuum*, the simplest possible state) but of the *operator algebra* restricted to spacelike wedges. The mathematical content is: (a) the vertex operators $V_\alpha(f) = :e^{i\alpha\phi(f)}:$ are constructed via bosonization from a free chiral boson; (b) when restricted to complementary wedge algebras, specific combinations of these operators satisfy Bell-CHSH with equality at $2\sqrt{2}$; (c) the proof uses the Tomita-Takesaki modular structure of the vacuum state with respect to the wedge algebras. Every element of this construction is algebraic/structural: the operator algebra, the modular theory, the bosonization duality, the wedge restriction. No tick occurs, no gradient is descended, no attractor is captured, no state is resolved, and no computation is conserved. The framework's entire dynamical vocabulary is idle. This is distinct from T026 (structural content gap in general) because T121 identifies a specific algebraic structure (vertex operator algebra + modular theory) that produces the most extreme quantum phenomenon (maximal Bell violation). It is distinct from T010/T021 (duality/ontological pluralism) because bosonization here is not merely an alternative description but the *constructive tool* that produces the Bell-violating operators. It is distinct from T012 (pre-resolution topology) because the topology of indistinguishability is irrelevant — the vacuum has no path-indistinguishability structure; the violation comes from the algebraic relations between the operators, not the topology of the state.
Why unres

**T122 — Alien derivative / KS algebra isomorphism: the framework's separation between computational organization and attractor landscape transformation is falsified**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Douaud & Kashani-Poor (2604.19731) prove that the algebra of alien derivatives acting on the topological string partition function is isomorphic to the Kontsevich-Soibelman Lie algebra governing wall-crossing of generalized Donaldson-Thomas invariants. The pointed alien derivative — the operator generating the entire resurgent transseries structure (how nonperturbative sectors communicate) — implements the same transformation as the KS symplectomorphism that governs how the BPS spectrum (the attractor landscape's stable-state census) transforms across walls of marginal stability. Stokes constants (quantitative data of the perturbative expansion's nonperturbative completion) are identified with DT invariants (integer-valued counts of BPS states). The framework separates "how the computation is organized" (Conservation, the algebra of corrections) from "how the attractor landscape transforms" (Attractor, landscape restructuring). This paper proves they are the same mathematical object. The framework has no primitive or structural element that could represent, let alone derive, this identification. This is distinct from T059 (ontological inversion, which concerns the derivational direction — structure producing dynamics) because T122 concerns an *isomorphism* between two structures the framework treats as separate primitives. It is distinct from T068 (fermionic resurgence, which concerns the combinatorial organization of transseries sectors) because T122 concerns the *algebraic identity* between the resurgence structure and the wall-crossing structure — not just the organizational pattern but the proven isomorphism. It is distinct from T021 (arithmetic-geometric unity, which concerns equivalences between mathematical categories) because T122 specifically identifies the framework's own primitive-level distinction (computation vs. attractor landscape) as the categorical boundary that the isomorphism collapses. The numerical verification (matching Stokes constants to DT invariants for local P², identifying D4-brane bound states and D2-brane decay in the Borel plane) confirms this is not merely formal but has specific, verifiable physical content.

Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) unify the Conservation/computation and Attractor primitives into a single structure that naturally produces the alien derivative / KS algebra isomorphism, which would require a foundational reconception of two of the five primitives; (b) derive the KS algebra from some combination of the five primitives, which is impossible since the KS algebra is an infinite-dimensional Lie algebra governing wall-crossing in Calabi-Yau moduli spaces — mathematical content far beyond any primitive's scope; (c) add the KS algebra / resurgence structure as a new primitive, which would be the most specific primitive in the framework and would raise the question of why *this* algebra is fundamental; or (d) accept that the framework's primitive-level distinction between "how computation is organized" and "how the attractor landscape transforms" is not a real distinction in nature

---

**T123 — The arrow of time has internal phase structure: Resolution's temporal output is non-monotonic and critical**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Hurvitz, Kochol, Fleurov & Sela (2604.20828) demonstrate that the Arrow of Time (AoT) — defined as the trajectory-level irreversibility of the measurement process — undergoes a nonanalytic phase transition at the MIPT, with a specific critical exponent. The AoT is non-monotonic in the measurement rate: zero for no measurements, zero for projective measurements, and maximal at intermediate measurement strengths. The framework's Resolution primitive describes the arrow of time as "nothing other than the accumulation of resolved states," implying monotonic growth with resolution activity. Paper 2 falsifies this monotonicity and reveals that the arrow of time is a *critical observable* of the resolution process with its own phase structure. The framework faces: (a) the arrow of time is not a simple cumulative quantity but has internal phase transitions tied to the entanglement structure of the monitored system — meaning the temporal primitive contains phase structure the Attractor primitive should describe, but neither primitive references the other in this context; (b) the AoT is a nonlinear functional of the density matrix associated with a local operator — a structural hybrid (local + nonlinear) that no primitive can represent; (c) the fact that projective (maximally resolving) measurements produce *zero* AoT means the strongest form of resolution destroys the arrow of time, directly contradicting the framework's implication that more resolution produces more irreversibility; (d) the exactly solvable model demonstrates this is not a qualitative observation but a precise, analytically controlled result with specific critical exponents; (e) the non-projective measurement parameterization provides a continuous dial between "weak resolution" and "strong resolution" that controls the AoT non-monotonically — the framework has no mechanism for measurement strength as a continuous parameter affecting the arrow of time. This is distinct from T075 (PIR as an independent source of irreversibility, which concerns a *classical* arrow) because T123 concerns the *quantum* arrow generated by the measurement process itself. It is distinct from T017 (continuous sharpening of criticality, which concerns phase transitions in the attractor landscape) because T123 concerns a phase transition in the *resolution process*, not in the system being resolved. It is distinct from T112 (complexity phase transitions in resolution, which concerns the computational difficulty of determining resolution outcomes) because T123 concerns the *temporal asymmetry* of the resolution process, not its computational cost.

Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) fundamentally revise the Resolution primitive to include measurement strength as a continuous parameter and to acknowledge that the arrow of time is a non-monotonic function of this parameter with critical behavior at the MIPT — this would transform the primitive from a description of "what happens" (states become definite) to a dynamical theory of "how the measurement process generates temporal asymmetry as a function of its own parameters"; (b) incorporate the MIPT as a new type of attractor landscape restructuring (the entanglement phase transition restructures the informational landscape), acknowledging that the Resolution and Attractor primitives are coupled through the entanglement structure in ways the framework cannot currently express; (c) develop a theory of how the AoT — a local, nonlinear, critical quantity — relates to the cumulative arrow the framework describes, which would require specifying the connection between trajectory-level measurement asymmetry and the macroscopic arrow of time; or (d) accept that the arrow of time's structure is richer than the Resolution primitive can capture, further deepening the structural content gap (T026) in the temporal domain. The vanishing of AoT for projective measurements is the sharpest challenge: the framework treats projective measurement as the paradigmatic resolution event, yet it produces zero temporal arrow. The arrow of time requires *partial* resolution — the substrate must be imperfectly resolving to generate irreversibility — a concept the framework's binary resolution (unresolved → definite) cannot express.

---

**T124 — Gravity as quantum mediator: the substrate must be unresolved to generate physical correlations**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Sen, Gangopadhyay & Vedral (2604.20767) demonstrate that graviton exchange between BEC phonon modes generates entanglement that scales with condensate particle number and requires the quantum (unresolved) nature of gravity. The framework's substrate claim — reality is computation running on a substrate whose parameters include the gravitational constant — implicitly treats the substrate as a classical platform providing the computational arena. The QGEP protocol requires the gravitational field to be *in superposition* (unresolved) while mediating entanglement between matter subsystems. If the gravitational field is part of the substrate (as the framework implies by listing G as a substrate parameter), then the substrate itself must be quantum and unresolved for the physical correlations to exist. The framework faces: (a) a classical substrate cannot mediate entanglement (by the argument of arXiv:2511.07348), so the substrate must be quantum; (b) a quantum substrate is itself subject to the Tick and Resolution primitives, creating circularity (the substrate that provides the tick is itself ticked; the substrate that hosts resolution is itself unresolved); (c) the N-scaling of phonon entanglement (enhanced with condensate particle number) depends on the collective mode structure of the BEC, requiring many-body quantum field theory of the gravitational sector — algebraic structure the framework cannot express

---

**T125 — The ghost resolvability problem: the boundary between resolvable and permanently virtual computation is structurally determined and model-dependent, not categorical**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Papers 1 and 2 (Deffayet, Fathe Jalali, Held, Mukohyama & Vikman 2604.21826, 2604.21823) prove that ghost degrees of freedom — fields with negative kinetic energy — can have discrete spectra, well-defined vacua, unitary evolution, and bounded observables when coupled to healthy sectors through interactions possessing integrals of motion with positive discrete spectrum. The framework has built an elaborate ontological architecture (T014, T041, T076, T082) around the categorical claim that ghost modes are "permanently virtual" — architecturally forbidden from resolution due to the absence of normalizable ground states and the continuous/dense character of their spectra. This categorical claim is falsified. The boundary between resolvable and permanently virtual computation is determined by the integrability structure of the interaction: ghost systems with suitable integrals of motion are resolvable; those without are not. The framework has no primitive for integrability, no mechanism for classifying interactions by their spectral consequences, and no account of how the structural properties of the Hamiltonian determine the ontological status of degrees of freedom. The framework's previous account (model update 2026-04-22) explicitly stated: "The absence of an attractor basin *is* the reason the mode never resolves." Papers 1 and 2 show that interactions can create attractor-like structure (finite-dimensional invariant subspaces) for ghost modes, invalidating this reasoning. This is distinct from T082 (virtual infrastructure non-uniqueness, which concerns the non-unique distribution of complex-mass virtual modes for a given theory) because T125 concerns the prior question of whether the modes are virtual at all. It is distinct from T014 (which introduced the permanently virtual category) because T125 identifies the structural criterion distinguishing resolvable from non-resolvable ghosts. It is distinct from T076 (gluon mass from virtual ghosts) because the resolution of T125 may change the interpretation of the ghost contribution to confinement — if QCD ghosts could in principle be stabilized by interactions, their "permanently virtual" status in perturbative QCD may be an artifact of the perturbative framework rather than an ontological necessity.

Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) develop a theory of which interactions produce integrals of motion that tame ghost spectra, which requires importing the full separability/integrability analysis from Hamiltonian mechanics — mathematical machinery the framework cannot express; (b) classify all physically relevant ghost-containing theories by their integrability properties and assess whether the physical ghosts (in quadratic gravity, in QCD's BRST sector) are in the resolvable or non-resolvable class, which requires detailed case-by-case spectral analysis the framework cannot perform; or (c) abandon the permanently virtual category entirely, treating all ghosts as potentially resolvable pending integrability analysis — but this would leave the framework unable to make any categorical statement about ghosts and would undermine the reasoning chain that supported T041 and T076. The deeper problem is that the framework's ontological categories (resolved, unresolved, permanently virtual) are supposed to be determined by the framework's primitives, but the resolvable/non-resolvable boundary is determined by integrability — a structural property of the Hamiltonian that is invisible to all five primitives.

---

**T126 — Spectral unboundedness without pathology: the framework's implicit association of energy unboundedness with instability is falsified**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Paper 2 (Deffayet et al. 2604.21823) constructs a quantum system whose Hamiltonian has a spectrum unbounded above and below (no ground state in the traditional sense, total energy can be arbitrarily negative) yet has: manifestly unitary evolution, a well-defined vacuum (the ground state of the integral of motion $I$, not of $H$), bounded expectation values for observable quantities, and discrete pure-point spectrum. The framework implicitly associates energy unboundedness with instability and pathology — the reasoning behind the "ghost modes are unphysical" conclusion, and part of the motivation for the fakeon prescription. Paper 2 shows that unboundedness of $H$ is compatible with complete quantum stability when the system possesses a second conserved quantity $I$ with a bounded-below, discrete spectrum. The "vacuum" is defined by minimizing $I$, not $H$. The framework's Attractor primitive defines attractors as energy minima; this system has no energy minimum but has a well-defined stable vacuum defined by a different conserved quantity. The framework has no account of attractor basins defined by non-energy conserved quantities in the presence of energy unboundedness. This is distinct from T006 (stable excited states not from gradient descent, which concerns states above an existing ground state) because T126 concerns a system with *no* energy ground state at all. It is distinct from T125 (ghost resolvability boundary) because T125 concerns the categorical status of ghosts while T126 concerns the framework's definition of "vacuum" and "stability." The broader implication: the framework's identification of attractors with energy minima is too narrow.

---

**T126 — Spectral unboundedness without pathology: the framework's implicit association of energy unboundedness with instability is falsified**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Paper 2 (Deffayet et al. 2604.21823) constructs a quantum system whose Hamiltonian has a spectrum unbounded above and below (no ground state in the traditional sense, total energy can be arbitrarily negative) yet has: manifestly unitary evolution, a well-defined vacuum (the ground state of the integral of motion $I$, not of $H$), bounded expectation values for squares of canonical variables, and discrete pure-point spectrum. The framework implicitly associates energy unboundedness with instability and pathology — the reasoning behind the "ghost modes are unphysical" conclusion, and part of the motivation for the fakeon prescription. Paper 2 shows that unboundedness of $H$ is compatible with complete quantum stability when the system possesses a second conserved quantity $I$ with a bounded-below, discrete spectrum. The "vacuum" is defined by minimizing $I$, not $H$. The framework's Attractor primitive defines attractors as energy minima; this system has no energy minimum but has a well-defined stable vacuum defined by a different conserved quantity. The framework has no account of attractor basins defined by non-energy conserved quantities in the presence of energy unboundedness. This is distinct from T006 (stable excited states not from gradient descent, which concerns states above an existing ground state) because T126 concerns a system with *no* energy ground state at all. It is distinct from T125 (ghost resolvability boundary) because T125 concerns the categorical status of ghosts while T126 concerns the framework's definition of "vacuum" and "stability." The broader implication: the framework's identification of attractors with energy minima is too narrow. Any conserved quantity with a bounded-below spectrum can define an attractor basin, and the relevant conserved quantity may not be the energy. The Conservation primitive lists energy conservation as a special case of computation conservation, but does not specify which conserved quantity defines the attractor structure — and for these systems, the answer is not energy but a different conserved quantity that the framework cannot identify or derive.

Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) generalize the Attractor primitive from "local minima in potential (energy) fields" to "local minima in the landscape of any conserved quantity with bounded-below discrete spectrum," which would require specifying how to identify the relevant conserved quantity for each system — a problem that requires the full machinery of integrability theory; (b) generalize the Field primitive from ẍ = −∇φ(x) where φ is the energy/potential to a version where φ is the relevant conserved quantity's landscape — but this changes

**T127 — Discrete informational resource thresholds: the framework has no mechanism for sharp, integer-valued boundaries in information extraction capacity**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Zeng (2604.22627) proves that the exact replica threshold for polynomial-sample estimation of tr(ρ^t) is ⌈t/2⌉ — a sharp, integer-valued boundary below which sample complexity grows with dimension and above which polynomial estimation is possible. The framework's primitives are all continuous or binary: the Tick is a discrete step but generates continuous propagation, the Field has continuous gradients, the Attractor has continuous depth/width, the Resolution is binary (unresolved/definite), and Conservation describes continuous quantities. None of these can produce or predict an integer-valued threshold in information-extraction capacity. The threshold is determined by the representation theory of the symmetric group on tensor product Hilbert spaces — specifically, whether ⌈t/2⌉ copies span a sufficiently rich subspace to access the nonlinear moment's information content. This is algebraic structure (Schur-Weyl duality, symmetric subspace dimension counting) of the type T026 has catalogued as missing from the framework. What makes T127 distinct from T026 (structural content gap in general) is the *sharpness* and *universality* of the threshold: it is not a vague structural inadequacy but a specific, proven, universal constant (⌈t/2⌉ for order-t moments) that separates qualitatively different information-theoretic regimes, and that no primitive can express, derive, or approximate. The non-fungibility of the threshold — no amount of classical samples compensates for a missing coherent replica — further challenges the Conservation primitive's implicit assumption that computational resources are transformable. Coherent replica number is a discrete informational resource that is conserved (each copy is used), non-fungible (cannot be substituted), and threshold-determining (separates polynomial from exponential regimes) — a category of resource the framework has no vocabulary for.

Why unresolved: The framework would need either (a) a primitive governing discrete informational resource thresholds, specifying what determines the threshold value and why it is integer-valued — this would require importing representation-theoretic machinery (symmetric group, Schur-Weyl duality) that is categorically absent from the framework; (b) a derivation of the threshold from existing primitives, which is impossible since the threshold depends on the algebraic structure of multi-copy Hilbert spaces and no primitive addresses multi-copy information structure; or (c) acceptance that the information-theoretic structure of quantum states has discrete features the framework cannot address, adding "replica-theoretic thresholds" to the growing catalogue of structural content the framework lacks. The extension to observable-weighted moments tr(Oρ^t) with bounded operator norm demonstrates the threshold is not specific to pure moments but is a universal feature of nonlinear quantum state estimation.

---

**T128 — Resolution becomes inert in the steady regime: the arrow of time is a transient phenomenon for finite-dimensional cyclic processes**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Koshihara & Yuasa (2604.22376) prove via a Poincaré-like recurrence theorem for quantum channels that any measurement-powered engine operating on a finite-dimensional working substance converges to a steady state where the measurement channel is non-disturbing — the measurement occurs but produces no state change, no energy injection, and no work extraction. The framework's Resolution primitive claims "the arrow of time is nothing other than the accumulation of resolved states," implying that each resolution event contributes irreversibly to the temporal ledger. Paper 2 shows that for cyclic measurement processes, this accumulation is self-limiting: the system reaches a fixed point where resolution events are formally performed but physically inert. The arrow of time, as generated by resolution, has a finite transient lifetime for finite-dimensional cyclic systems.

The framework faces: (a) in the steady regime, resolution events occur (the measurement channel is applied) but produce no physical change — the framework must distinguish between formal and effective resolution, a distinction it currently lacks; (b) the convergence to the non-disturbing fixed point means the "accumulated ledger of resolved states" reaches a saturated state beyond which further entries are trivially repetitive — the ledger is finite for finite-dimensional cyclic processes, contradicting the picture of unbounded temporal accumulation; (c) the no-go theorem for work extraction proves that resolution alone cannot sustain thermodynamic irreversibility — an entropy-decreasing process (feedback control or thermal contact with a reservoir) is necessary, meaning the arrow of time requires structural supplementation beyond resolution; (d) the result connects to T120 (the reversible/irreversible boundary is gauge-dependent) by showing that the physical content of resolution is depleted by cycling, and to T123 (the arrow of time has internal phase structure) by identifying a new regime where the arrow of time vanishes — not because of maximal measurement strength (T123) but because of steady-state convergence.

This is distinct from T123 (which concerns non-monotonicity of the AoT as a function of measurement strength at a fixed point in the measurement cycle) because T128 concerns the *temporal evolution* of the AoT across many measurement cycles — it is zero in the steady state regardless of measurement strength. It is distinct from T075 (PIR as an independent source of irreversibility) because T128 concerns the *quantum* measurement-induced arrow and its transient character. It is distinct from T120 (gauge-dependence of the reversible/irreversible boundary) because T128 concerns the dynamical convergence of resolution to inertness, not the representational equivalence of Hamiltonian and dissipative dynamics.

Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) introduce a distinction between generative resolution (transient regime, producing physical change and contributing to the arrow of time) and inert resolution (steady regime, formally occurring but physically vacuous), specifying the conditions under which resolution transitions from generative to inert — this would require the framework to model the measurement channel's fixed-point structure, which depends on the spectral properties of the channel's Kraus operators (algebraic structure the framework cannot express); (b) restrict the Resolution primitive's temporal claims to non-cyclic or infinite-dimensional processes, acknowledging that the arrow of time is a transient phenomenon for finite-dimensional cyclic systems — but this would limit the primitive's scope to exclude the experimentally relevant quantum thermodynamic regime; (c) incorporate the necessity of entropy-decreasing supplementation (feedback or thermal contact) into the framework's account of the arrow of time, acknowledging that resolution alone is insufficient for sustained irreversibility — this would require the framework to develop a theory of how resolution interacts with thermal reservoirs and feedback control, structural elements it currently lacks. The Poincaré recurrence theorem for channels

**T129 — Conformal enrichment of criticality: the framework's characterization of critical points is qualitatively weaker than the physical truth**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Cabrera, De Polsi, Rançon & Wschebor (2604.24675) prove that scale invariance implies conformal invariance for the large-N O(N) universality class, and identify the *structural prerequisites* within the NPRG that make this implication hold. The framework's Attractor primitive describes critical points as scale-free saddles between attractor basins. The physical truth is richer: critical points are *conformally invariant*, which implies specific constraints on correlation functions (special conformal Ward identities) that scale invariance alone does not provide. The gap between "scale-invariant" and "conformally invariant" is filled by structural content — the specific functional structure of the NPRG flow equations, the properties of the regulator, the large-N saddle point structure, and the Ward identity algebra — that no primitive can express. This is distinct from T026 (general structural content gap) because it concerns the framework's own qualitative claim about a specific physical phenomenon (criticality): the claim is *correct but weaker than the truth*, and the difference between the framework's characterization and the physical truth is measurable (conformal Ward identities produce specific constraints on correlation function ratios that scale invariance alone does not). It is distinct from T049 (scale-dependent symmetry type) because T049 concerns symmetry *changing* under RG flow, while T129 concerns the framework's inability to express the *enhanced symmetry at the fixed point*. It is distinct from T032 (symmetry as missing structural primitive) because T032 noted symmetry in general is missing; T129 identifies a specific, proven case where the framework's claim about a critical point is qualitatively weaker than the physical symmetry structure. The paper's identification of "what to expect, on rather general grounds, for a theory to be conformally invariant" — structural conditions on the flow equations — represents physical knowledge that the framework cannot access.

Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) derive conformal invariance at critical points from the Attractor and Field primitives, which would require showing that gradient dynamics on a flat (scale-free) attractor landscape automatically produces the additional special conformal constraints — no path is visible since conformal invariance is not a consequence of scale invariance in general (counterexamples exist in d > 2); (b) incorporate the NPRG flow structure as a primitive or derived element, connecting to T080 (continuum limit non-uniqueness) and T108 (scale-evolution kernels) but at a deeper level — not just the RG kernel but the structural conditions on the RG flow that determine which enhanced symmetries emerge at fixed points; or (c) accept that the framework's characterization of critical phenomena is systematically weaker than the physical truth, limiting the Attractor primitive's scope at precisely the points (phase transitions) where the framework claims the "most interesting physics happens." The generality of the paper's conclusions ("rather general grounds" for conformal invariance) makes this a systematic limitation, not a special-case problem.

**T130 — The gravitational energy floor is geometrically guaranteed in all dimensions: conservation of computation in the gravitational sector is enforced by dimensional-general geometric theorems, not by computational architecture**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Hirsch, Khuri, Lesourd & Zhang (2604.24746) prove the spacetime positive mass theorem in arbitrary dimension n for both asymptotically flat and asymptotically hyperboloidal initial data sets. The framework's Conservation of Computation, specialized to gravitational energy, requires energy non-negativity — the attractor landscape must have a floor (Minkowski or de Sitter minimum). This floor is guaranteed not by any computational principle but by a geometric theorem whose proof uses the Jang equation, marginally outer trapped surfaces, and the Brendle-Wang μ-bubble technique. The theorem holds in all dimensions n ≥ 3, meaning the gravitational energy floor is a dimensional-general feature of Lorentzian geometry + dominant energy condition, not a consequence of the framework's specific 3+1 dimensional architectural parameters. The hyperboloidal extension is especially significant: it proves the energy floor for spacetimes with positive cosmological constant, meaning the framework's dark energy claim (Λ as computational cost of new substrate) is constrained by a geometric energy inequality that the framework neither predicted nor can derive. The Λ-dependent energy bound $E \geq \sqrt{|P|^2 + \frac{\Lambda}{n(n-1)}|J|^2}$ relates mass, momentum, angular momentum, and cosmological constant in a way that is geometric, not computational.

This is distinct from T037 (Hermiticity as emergent symmetry, which concerns the derivation of unitarity from geometric structure) because T130 concerns the *foundational energy inequality* that makes the gravitational attractor landscape well-defined. It is distinct from T045

**T131 — Operator-algebraic symmetry classification: the complete classification of physically relevant symmetries lives in a mathematical universe categorically inaccessible to the framework**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Jia, Luo, Tian, Wang & Zhang (2604.25821) demonstrate that the symmetry category of a 2D QFT with continuous G-symmetry and 't Hooft anomaly k ∈ H⁴(BG,ℤ) is Hilb^k(G) ≅ Rep^k(C_0(G)), with the full topological content of the 3D SymTFT encoded in the Drinfeld center Z(Hilb^k(G)) ≅ Rep(C*(G//_{Ad}G, Σ_k)). The classification applies to physically relevant Lie groups including compact connected groups, ℝ, and GL(1,ℂ) — encompassing the symmetry structures of the Standard Model. The framework's five dynamical primitives have zero contact with this classification. The Conservation primitive treats symmetries as derived from conservation laws; Paper 1 shows the *classification of consistent symmetries* — which symmetries exist, what anomalies they carry, how they braid — is determined by operator-algebraic/cohomological/categorical data logically prior to any conservation law. The anomaly k determines the symmetry category before any dynamics is specified. The Drinfeld center, which classifies the anyon content of the bulk SymTFT, is a categorical construction with no dynamical component. The transgression map τ: H⁴(BG,ℤ) → H²(G//_{Ad}G, U(1)) mediating between the anomaly and the braiding structure is a cohomological operation with no expression in any primitive. The multiplicative bundle gerbe Σ_k and the Fell line bundle are geometric-algebraic objects whose role is to twist the groupoid C*-algebra, encoding the anomaly's effect on the symmetry structure in a way the framework cannot represent at any level of description.

This is distinct from T048 (cohomological completeness of SPT classification, which concerned H³(G,U(1)) for discrete/finite groups) because T131 concerns continuous Lie groups and the full operator-algebraic/C*-algebraic framework required for their treatment. It is distinct from T107 (super-pentagon consistency, which concerned the enumeration of consistent fermionic symmetry structures in 2D) because T131 provides the unified C*-algebraic framework encompassing all cases, not just fermionic ones. It is distinct from T111 (generalized symmetries, which concerned the physical consequences of higher-form/non-invertible symmetries) because T131 concerns the *mathematical classification scheme itself* rather than the dynamical consequences. It is distinct from T026 (general structural content gap) because T131 identifies a single, unified, complete mathematical framework — C*-algebraic categorical symmetry theory — that classifies all consistent symmetry structures for a broad class of groups and that constitutes, by itself, a richer and more complete theoretical structure than the framework's five primitives combined.

Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) derive C*-algebraic categories, bundle gerbes, Drinfeld centers, and groupoid algebras from its five primitives, which is categorically impossible — these are mathematical structures in functional analysis and category theory with no connection to discrete temporal updates, gradient descent, attractor capture, state resolution, or computation conservation; (b) add C*-algebraic structure as a foundational element, which would require abandoning the framework's claim that five dynamical primitives suffice and importing an entire mathematical discipline as infrastructure; (c) demonstrate that the C*-algebraic classification is derivable from "computation" in some sense that preserves
