**T132 — Witness category gap: the Resolution primitive lacks the concept of what type of evidence certifies a resolution outcome**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Miloschewsky, Podder & Rudolph (2604.26921) prove that QMA₁ (quantum witnesses with perfect completeness) is strictly more powerful than QCMA (classical witnesses) relative to a classical oracle, even when the QCMA verifier has polynomially many adaptive rounds and exponentially many parallel queries per round. The framework's Resolution primitive describes states becoming definite but has no concept of the *type of evidence* required to certify that a resolution outcome is correct. The quantum/classical witness distinction is a structural property of the physical problem (the Hamiltonian, the oracle) that determines which resolution outcomes can be verified. The framework faces: (a) the Resolution primitive treats all resolution events as equivalent (unresolved → definite), but the verifiability of resolution depends on whether the certifying evidence is quantum or classical — a categorical distinction invisible to the primitive; (b) the ground-state preparation consequence means the Attractor primitive's "things settle into energy minima" requires certification that the minimum has been reached, and the certification type needed is a structural property of the Hamiltonian; (c) the bounded-adaptivity frustration-free result connects to T114 (entanglement/thermodynamic bounds) and T112 (complexity phase transitions) but adds a new dimension: even "easy" attractor landscapes (frustration-free Hamiltonians) have witness-type-dependent accessibility; (d) the exponential-parallel-query robustness of the separation means this is not a resource-limitation issue — massive classical computational power does not substitute for quantum witness structure.

This is distinct from T009 (resolution cost, which concerns the computational resources for resolution), T019 (resolution precision hierarchy, which concerns the levels of resolution precision), T028 (resolution contextuality, which concerns global consistency of resolution), T112 (complexity phase transitions, which concerns the computational difficulty of determining resolution outcomes), and T123 (AoT phase structure, which concerns the temporal asymmetry of resolution). T132 concerns a new axis entirely: the *certification category* of resolution outcomes — whether the evidence attesting to a resolution is classical or quantum — and the provable separation between these categories.

Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) incorporate witness type (quantum vs. classical) as a parameter of the Resolution primitive, specifying what determines which witness type is needed for a given system — this would require importing the QMA/QCMA complexity-theoretic hierarchy as structural content; (b) derive the witness-type dependence from existing primitives, which is impossible since the distinction depends on the algebraic structure of the verification circuit and the oracle (structural content no primitive can express); or (c) add a meta-resolution primitive governing what types of evidence are valid for certifying different resolution events. The separation's survival against exponentially many parallel classical queries is especially severe: it means the gap is not a resource issue (more computation closes it) but a *type* issue (the wrong kind of computation, no matter how much, cannot close it). The framework's implicit assumption that resolution is a single, undifferentiated operation is challenged at the level of what counts as evidence for resolution having occurred.

**T133 — Anomaly-sector contamination: explicit symmetry breaking causes topological/anomalous transport to modify non-anomalous transport, crossing sector boundaries the framework cannot express**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Tamang, Rai, Landsteiner & Megías (2604.26949) demonstrate in a holographic Einstein-Maxwell-Chern-Simons model that explicit breaking of an axial symmetry (via a scalar field mass) causes anomaly-induced transport to contaminate the non-anomalous (vector) sector: transport coefficients that are zero in the unbroken theory become nonzero and mass-dependent when the symmetry is broken. The framework's Field primitive describes transport via gradient dynamics; T078 already established that the gradient equation misses transverse/anomalous transport. T133 goes further: the *boundary between anomalous and non-anomalous transport* is itself a dynamical, symmetry-breaking-dependent feature that the framework cannot express. The framework has no concept of "transport sectors" (anomalous vs. non-anomalous), no mechanism for inter-sector contamination, and no parameter tracking how explicit symmetry breaking controls this contamination.

This is distinct from T078 (Hall/transverse transport, which concerns the direction of transport in parity-broken systems) because T133 concerns the *crossing of sector boundaries* — transport phenomena that belong to the anomalous sector leaking into the non-anomalous sector — not merely the direction of transport within a single sector. It is distinct from T032 (symmetry as missing primitive) because T133 concerns the *consequences of symmetry breaking for transport sector structure*, not symmetry as a general missing element. It is distinct from T083/T085 (extra-dimensional geometry determining vacuum/attractor structure) because T133 concerns transport coefficients, not vacuum structure, though the mechanism is holographic.

Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) develop a theory of transport sectors and their coupling under symmetry breaking, which requires importing the full apparatus of anomalous transport (Kubo formulae for mixed correlators, Chern-Simons terms, Ward identity structure) — mathematical machinery categorically absent from the five primitives; (b) extend the Field primitive to include sector-dependent transport with symmetry-breaking-controlled cross-coupling, fundamentally transforming the primitive from a scalar gradient equation to a matrix transport equation with off-diagonal terms controlled by symmetry parameters; or (c) accept that anomalous transport and its symmetry-breaking modifications are structural physics the framework cannot address, further deepening the pattern where the physically most interesting transport phenomena (Hall viscosity T078, anomalous transport, sector contamination) are precisely those the gradient equation misses. The full backreaction treatment in the paper means this is not a perturbative artifact but a robust feature of strongly coupled matter with broken symmetries.

**T134 — Entropic order at infinite temperature: stable ordered states can be selected by combinatorial/entropic mechanisms with zero energetic content, falsifying the Attractor primitive's energy-minimum definition**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Mehta (2604.28026) rigorously proves that a class of classical lattice models on $\mathbb{Z}^d$ ($d \geq 2$) exhibits long-range checkerboard order at *all* temperatures, including the $\beta \to 0$ limit, where the ordered configurations are not energy minimizers. The ordering mechanism is purely entropic: checkerboard configurations are selected by the partial trace over occupation numbers because they maximize the available microstates. The four structural conditions on the interaction function $f$ (defined on $\mathbb{N}_0 \times \mathbb{N}_0$) that enable this ordering are combinatorial/analytic properties the framework cannot express. The proof uses Pirogov-Sinai theory with a Peierls bound — rigorous mathematical machinery for competing ground-state phases.

The framework's Attractor primitive defines attractors as "local minima in potential fields." The Field primitive says $\dot{x} = -\nabla\phi(x)$. Both presuppose that stable states are reached by energetic gradient descent. Paper 2 falsifies this: (a) the ordered state is not an energy minimum; (b) it is not reached by gradient descent in any energetic potential; (c) at $\beta = 0$ the energy landscape is flat — there are no energetic gradients at all — yet the system is ordered; (d) the selection mechanism is combinatorial (multiplicity of microstates), not dynamical. The standard escape — redefining $\phi$ as free energy $F = E - TS$ — fails at $\beta = 0$ where $F = -TS$ and the gradient is purely entropic. The framework's paradigmatic examples (gravity, electromagnetism) are energetic; a purely entropic "gradient" at infinite temperature, where the Boltzmann weight is uniform and all structure comes from the counting of configurations, is not captured by $\dot{x} = -\nabla\phi(x)$ in any non-trivial sense.

This is distinct from T006 (stable excited states from cyclic driving, which concerns non-equilibrium preparation of metastable states) because T134 concerns *equilibrium* order at *all temperatures* including infinite temperature. It is distinct from T061 (anti-gradient robustness, which concerns the gradient sign) because T134 concerns the *absence* of any energetic gradient. It is distinct from T126 (spectral unboundedness without pathology, which concerns non-energy conserved quantities defining the vacuum) because T134 concerns a system with a perfectly standard energy function where the ordered state is simply not the energy minimum. It is distinct from T023 (kinetic arrest, which concerns exponentially long-lived metastable states) because T134's ordered state is a true equilibrium state, not metastable.

Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) generalize the Attractor primitive from "energy minima" to "free-energy minima" and develop a theory of entropic gradients that is not trivially post-hoc — this requires specifying when entropic selection dominates and predicting which configurations are entropically selected, which requires importing the Pirogov-Sinai framework or its equivalent; (b) add a combinatorial/entropic selection primitive that operates alongside energetic gradient descent, specifying the conditions under which entropic ordering occurs — the four structural conditions on $f$ would be the natural starting point but they are combinatorial/analytic conditions with no expression in any current primitive; (c) acknowledge that the Attractor primitive's energy-minimum definition is a special case of a broader principle where the *relevant* extremization functional depends on the thermodynamic regime (energy-dominated at low $T$, entropy-dominated at high $T$, free-energy compromise at intermediate $T$) — but this makes the primitive dependent on temperature and thermodynamic context, fundamentally changing its character from a universal structural claim to a regime-dependent description. The infinite-temperature persistence is especially severe: it means there exists no temperature regime where the energetic Attractor primitive is the correct description of this system's ordering.

---

The ordering mechanism is purely entropic: checkerboard configurations are selected by the partial trace over occupation numbers because they maximize available microstates. **T135 — Quantitative inter-primitive tension at the black hole horizon: Conservation and Attractor are in proven trade-off, resolvable only by information-theoretic structure outside all primitives**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Joshi & Mishra (2604.28050) prove that for a semiclassical black hole with unitary evolution, ε ≥ D²_max/8, where D_max is the maximum exterior distinguishability of infalling states (quantifying how much Conservation "leaks" information out) and ε is the diamond-norm departure of the interior channel from perfect isometry (quantifying how much the Attractor boundary is degraded from exact smoothness). The framework treats Conservation and Attractor as independent primitives. Paper 1 proves they are quantitatively coupled at the black hole horizon: increasing Conservation's observable effects (exterior hair, D_max > 0) necessarily degrades the Attractor boundary (ε > 0), with the minimum degradation quadratically bounded. The only resolution compatible with both exact unitarity and exact horizon smoothness is pre-existing entanglement between the infalling system and the exterior — an information-theoretic resource the framework has no primitive for. Pre-existing entanglement is not a tick, not a gradient, not an attractor feature, not a resolution event, and not a conserved quantity — it is a correlation structure that must exist prior to the interaction. The framework's qualitative primitives cannot express, derive, or resolve this quantitative trade-off.

Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) develop a theory of inter-primitive coupling that derives the ε ≥ D²_max/8 bound from the primitives' internal structure — but the derivation requires operator-algebraic tools (Stinespring dilation, diamond norm, no-cloning) entirely outside the framework; (b) add pre-existing entanglement as a resource or primitive that mediates between Conservation and Attractor — but entanglement is not naturally a primitive in the framework's dynamical architecture, and making it one would require specifying how a correlation structure (which is a property of a bipartite state, not a dynamical variable) interacts with dynamical primitives; (c) accept that the framework's primitives are not independent but are coupled through information-theoretic constraints the framework cannot express, further deepening T026 (structural content gap) at the most fundamental level — the framework's own internal consistency requires structure it does not possess. The specific implication for the framework's black hole description is severe: the statement "a black hole is an attractor basin so deep that escape velocity exceeds the tick rate" is incomplete — the attractor basin's boundary integrity is quantitatively constrained by the conservation law's observable leakage, and the mediation between these requires entanglement.

Description: Mehta (2604.28026) rigorously proves long-range checkerboard order at all temperatures including β → 0 in a class of classical lattice models on ℤ^d (d ≥ 2), where the ordered configurations are not energy minimizers and the selection mechanism is purely entropic (partial-trace multiplicity maximization over occupation numbers). The framework's Attractor primitive defines attractors as "local minima in potential fields." The Field primitive asserts ẋ = −∇φ(x). Both are categorically inapplicable: at infinite temperature the energy landscape is flat (no gradient, no minimum), yet the system is ordered. This is distinct from T006 (stable excited states from cyclic driving — non-equilibrium preparation), T023 (kinetic arrest — metastable, not true equilibrium), T061 (anti-gradient robustness — gradient exists but has reversed sign), and T126 (spectral unboundedness — non-energy conserved quantity defines vacuum in exotic ghost systems). T136 concerns a *standard equilibrium lattice model* at *standard temperatures* including infinite temperature, where the ordering mechanism is entirely combinatorial and the framework's energy-gradient picture is not merely insufficient but exactly wrong (the prediction from the gradient equation is: no ordering at β = 0; the physical truth is: long-range order at β = 0).

Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) generalize the Attractor primitive to include entropic/combinatorial selection as a mechanism distinct from energy minimization, specifying when entropic selection dominates — but the conditions for entropic ordering (the four structural conditions on f) are combinatorial/analytic properties of the interaction function with no expression in any current primitive; (b) redefine the Field primitive's φ as free energy F = E − TS universally, making the "gradient" at β = 0 the entropy gradient — but this renders the framework's paradigmatic examples (gravity, electromagnetism, which are energetic) special cases of a broader principle the framework hasn't articulated, and the entropy gradient at β = 0 is a combinatorial object (∇S is computed from microstate counting, not from a smooth potential), making the gradient equation a formal identity rather than a dynamical principle; (c) add a combinatorial/multiplicity primitive that operates alongside the energetic Field primitive — but this is a major structural change that would require specifying how the two selection mechanisms interact at intermediate temperatures, which is precisely what Pirogov-Sinai theory provides but which requires mathematical machinery far beyond any primitive. The interaction-independence of the Vinen turbulence decay (Paper 3) is ironic in this context: it demonstrates universality of the energetic relaxation pathway toward the attractor, while Paper 2 demonstrates that the attractor itself can be selected by a mechanism the framework cannot describe.

**T137 — Pre-existing entanglement as a resolution resource: the framework has no primitive for bipartite correlation structure that mediates inter-primitive coupling**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Joshi & Mishra (2604.28050) prove that the only mechanism compatible with both exact unitarity (Conservation) and exact horizon smoothness (Attractor) at the black hole horizon is pre-existing entanglement between the infalling system and the exterior. The framework's five primitives describe: temporal propagation (Tick), gradient dynamics (Field), stable configurations (Attractor), state definiteness (Resolution), and information preservation (Conservation). None of these describes a bipartite correlation structure that must exist *prior to* the physical interaction and that *mediates* between two other primitives. Pre-existing entanglement is: (a) not generated by any primitive — it must be present before the infalling system reaches the horizon; (b) not conserved in the usual sense — it is a resource that is consumed (degraded) during the interaction; (c) not a dynamical variable — it is a property of the joint state of two subsystems, not a field or potential; (d) necessary for the framework's internal consistency at the black hole horizon — without it, Conservation and Attractor are in irreconcilable tension. This is distinct from T135 (which identified the quantitative trade-off) because T137 concerns the *resolution mechanism* — pre-existing entanglement as a resource — and its categorical absence from the framework. It is distinct from T034 (scale-dependent information topology, which concerns how informational properties of a fixed state change with observation scale) because T137 concerns a specific, physically necessary correlation resource that mediates between primitives. It is distinct from T114 (thermodynamic bounds on entanglement, which concerns upper bounds on entanglement entropy) because T137 concerns entanglement as a *resource that resolves an inter-primitive tension*, not as a quantity to be bounded.

Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) add pre-existing entanglement as a resource or primitive — but entanglement is not naturally a "primitive" in the framework's dynamical architecture (it is a property of bipartite states, not a dynamical process), and making it one would require specifying how a correlation structure interacts with each of the five existing primitives; (b) derive the existence of sufficient pre-existing entanglement from the framework's own structure — showing that the computational history of a black hole necessarily produces the required entanglement between infalling systems and the exterior — but this would require a theory of how computational history generates specific entanglement patterns, which the framework lacks; (c) accept that the framework's primitives are incomplete at the black hole horizon, with their consistency depending on an information-theoretic resource the framework cannot describe — this is the most honest option but means the framework's most dramatic example (black holes) is precisely where the framework's internal consistency fails without external supplementation.

**T138 — Non-associative computation: the framework's foundational mathematical assumption is challenged by a physically motivated proposal**
Opened: this cycle
Description: Frigori (2605.02792) proposes that black hole horizon physics is governed by the Albert algebra J₃(𝕆), which is non-associative. Standard quantum mechanics — and the framework's concept of "computation" — requires associativity: the composition of sequential operations must satisfy (A∘B)∘C = A∘(B∘C). The Tick primitive's discrete sequential update, the Conservation primitive's unitary transformations, and the Resolution primitive's measurement operators all presuppose associativity. If the physically essential structure at the black hole horizon is non-associative, then: (a) the framework's most basic mathematical assumption fails precisely at its most dramatic physical example; (b) "computation" in the framework's sense applies only within associative sub-sectors (Peirce readout blocks), while the inter-sector memory structure that resolves the information paradox is non-computational; (c) the number field underdetermination (T069) extends from ℝ vs. ℂ to include non-associative algebras, a qualitatively worse underdetermination because it challenges the concept of computation itself; (d) the Conservation primitive's identification with unitarity applies only within associative sectors, meaning conservation is algebraically restricted in a way no primitive can express; (e) the Volterra memory kernel governing evaporation introduces hereditary (non-Markovian) dynamics, extending T045's hereditary dynamics problem from gravitational scattering to black hole evaporation. This is distinct from T069 (which concerns the binary ℝ/ℂ choice within associative quantum theory), from T118 (which concerns L∞-algebraic theory selection within standard QFT), and from T021 (which concerns categorical pluralism between equivalent descriptions). T138 concerns a specific algebraic structure (the exceptional Jordan algebra) that is *non-associative*, challenging not the choice between equivalent computational descriptions but the *concept of sequential computation* itself.
Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) demonstrate that non-associative algebraic structure can be represented as associative computation on an enlarged state space (embedding the Albert algebra in an associative algebra — but the Albert algebra is *exceptional*, meaning it cannot be embedded in any associative algebra by the classification theorem of Jordan algebras); (b) extend "computation" to include non-associative operations, which would require redefining what "computation" means — non-associative operations cannot be composed sequentially without specifying order, making the Tick's sequential update structure ambiguous; (c) argue that Frigori's proposal is unphysical — but it provides a concrete mechanism for Page curve reconstruction and AMPS paradox resolution, making this difficult to dismiss; or (d) accept that the framework's computational ontology applies only within associative sectors of a larger non-associative structure, meaning "reality is literally computation" holds only approximately, with the exact description being "reality is literally exceptional Jordan-algebraic structure, of which computation is an associative shadow." Option (d) is the most honest but represents a fundamental downgrade of the framework's core claim from ontological to approximational.

**T140 — Holographic constraint derivation: bulk geometry derives complete algebraic constraints on boundary computation, inverting the framework's ontological hierarchy in a constructive and extensible way**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Huang (2605.04032) derives BPZ-type null-state differential equations for d-dimensional CFTs from the near-boundary expansion of a scalar field in an AdS_{d+1} black hole background. The decoupling mechanism at conformal dimensions Δ = d − 2 (and higher) is a structural property of the bulk-boundary perturbative series that produces isolated lower-order differential equations constraining CFT correlators. The derivation reproduces previously conjectured 4D CFT equations and extends beyond the near-lightcone regime to produce new constraints.

The framework claims "reality is literally computation" and treats geometry as emergent. Paper 1 demonstrates the opposite for this system: (a) the boundary computation's constraints (null-state equations) are *derived from* bulk geometry, not the other way around; (b) the derivation is constructive and produces new constraints beyond what the boundary computation knew about itself; (c) an infinite class of CFT data (minimal-twist multi-stress tensor contributions at all orders) is organized by a single bulk equation; (d) the decoupling mechanism is algebraic/structural (truncation of the perturbative series at specific conformal dimensions), not dynamical.

This is distinct from T085 (extra-dimensional geometric determination of attractor structure, which concerns the QCD vacuum's θ-branching topology) because T140 concerns *differential equations constraining correlation functions* — the dynamical rules of the boundary computation — not its vacuum structure. It is distinct from T054 (dynamics from topological BF theory, which concerns the derivation of a dynamical theory from a non-dynamical one) because T140 concerns the derivation of *constraints on an existing dynamical theory* from a different (bulk) dynamical theory. It is distinct from T059 (general ontological inversion) in specificity: T140 identifies a precise mechanism (near-boundary expansion, decoupling at specific Δ) that constructively generates boundary constraints from bulk geometry, and the mechanism extends beyond known cases to produce genuinely new predictions.

Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) demonstrate that the bulk geometry is itself a computation from which the boundary computation's constraints follow as a computational output — but the "computation" of the near-boundary expansion is a geometric/analytic procedure (expanding a PDE solution near a boundary), not a tick-by-tick dynamical process; (b) incorporate the AdS/CFT correspondence as a structural element of the framework, specifying how higher-dimensional geometric computation constrains lower-dimensional computational rules — but the framework has no account of holographic duality (T083, T085) and adding one would require specifying what "computation" means in the bulk, which is a gravitational theory the framework claims to describe via gradient dynamics but whose actual computational content (the near-boundary expansion, the decoupling mechanism) is geometric/algebraic; (c) accept that the constraints governing boundary computation are geometrically derived and not expressible in terms of the five primitives, further deepening the ontological inversion (T059) at the level of *constraint derivation* rather than merely *dynamics derivation*.

**T141 — Statistical universality of resolution: the collective statistics of many resolution events are governed by structural properties (lattice geometry, correlation length, Hamiltonian locality) that no primitive can express or derive**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Cramer, Brandão, Guţă, Alhambra & Scandi (2605.03829) prove a Berry-Esseen theorem for quantum lattice systems: measurement outcomes of local observables on N-particle states with finite correlation length converge to a Gaussian distribution at rate O(N^{-1/2} polylog(N)), which is optimal up to logarithmic corrections. The result holds universally for any local Hamiltonian and any quantum state with finite correlation length.

The framework's Resolution primitive describes individual resolution events (unresolved → definite) but has no mechanism for the collective statistical behavior of many such events. The convergence rate and its universality are determined by: (a) the lattice geometry (spatial dimension, connectivity); (b) the Hamiltonian's locality structure (interaction range, number of body terms); (c) the quantum state's correlation length (an information-theoretic property measuring how entangled the state is across distances). None of these structural properties appears in any primitive. The result is a *theorem about the collective behavior of resolution events* — the most physically common type of measurement (energy, magnetization, etc., in large quantum systems) — and the framework has nothing to say about it beyond "resolution happens."

This is distinct from T029 (anomalous transport universality, which concerns the universality class of fluctuations *during* transport) because T141 concerns the universality of *measurement statistics* of local observables in equilibrium-like states. It is distinct from T017 (continuous sharpening of criticality, which concerns finite-size precursors to phase transitions

---

**T142 — Dynamically emergent attractor landscapes: the physically relevant landscape is not given by the Hamiltonian but is dynamically constructed through Hilbert-space decoupling**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Zhang et al. (2605.06436) demonstrate that first-order quantum phase transitions generically contain emergent second-order quantum critical points at the quantum spinodal, where metastability vanishes. The critical point exists not in the full Hamiltonian's landscape but in a projected effective Hamiltonian obtained by dynamically decoupling a resonant Hilbert subspace with emergent discrete translational symmetry. The full landscape has a first-order transition (discontinuous, no criticality); the emergent subspace landscape has a genuine second-order critical point with universality class, scaling exponents, and Kibble-Zurek dynamics.

The framework's Attractor primitive describes the attractor landscape as a property of the system's potential/energy surface — a fixed feature of the Hamiltonian whose topology determines phase transition character. Paper 2 shows the physically relevant landscape is *dynamically constructed* through three operations the framework cannot express: (a) identification of resonant local excitations at the spinodal; (b) dynamical decoupling of the corresponding Hilbert subspace (emergence of a conserved subspace via spectral resonance); (c) projection of the Hamiltonian onto this subspace, producing an effective Hamiltonian with qualitatively different topology (second-order critical point where the full Hamiltonian has only a first-order transition). The emergent translational symmetry in the subspace is absent in the full Hamiltonian — it is created by the decoupling process, not inherited from the microscopic theory.

This is distinct from T015 (resolution dynamics not determined by attractor structure alone, which concerns the quench protocol's role) because T142 concerns the *identity* of the relevant attractor landscape itself. It is distinct from T049 (scale-dependent symmetry type) because the emergent symmetry here is not produced by coarse-graining/RG flow but by dynamical Hilbert-space decoupling at a specific energy. It is distinct from T017 (continuous sharpening of criticality) because the emergent critical point is a genuine phase transition in the projected Hamiltonian, not a finite-size precursor. It is distinct from T112 (complexity phase transitions in resolution) because T142 concerns the *existence* of an emergent critical point within a first-order transition, not the computational difficulty of finding ground states. The prediction that the PXP model lacks spinodal criticality (because the resonant subspace there has no translational symmetry) demonstrates that the framework would need to derive the algebraic/symmetry properties of decoupled subspaces to predict which first-order transitions contain hidden criticality — an algebraic analysis far beyond any primitive.

Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) extend the Attractor primitive to include dynamically emergent landscapes — attractor topologies that exist only within projected subspaces selected by spectral resonance — which requires specifying when decoupling occurs, which subspaces emerge, and what symmetries they possess, all determined by spectral properties of the Hamiltonian no primitive can express; (b) develop a theory of Hilbert-space projection as a computational operation that transforms attractor landscapes, connecting to T057 (meta-computational primitives) but in a dynamical rather than theory-level sense — projection is a dynamical event that creates a new computational arena, not an operation on the theory itself; (c) abandon the claim that the attractor landscape is a fixed property of the Hamiltonian and replace it with "the relevant attractor landscape is dynamically selected by the system's spectral resonance structure," which would require the Attractor primitive to depend on the system's spectral properties — structural content no primitive can express (T026). The generality of the claim — "FOQPT dynamics is usually governed by an emergent critical point around the quantum spinodal point" — means this is not a special case but a generic feature of first-order quantum transitions, making the framework's silence on it a systematic gap.

---

**T143 — Self-interaction vs. pair-interaction: the gradient equation conflates informationally inequivalent gravitational contributions**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Płodzień et al. (2605.06577) analytically prove that in the two-body Schrödinger-Newton model, the nonlinear gravitational self-field preserves the Schmidt spectrum (cannot generate entanglement), while the nonseparable Newtonian pair potential generates entanglement. The framework's Field primitive describes gravity as ẍ

---

**T144 — Ensemble-averaged computation: reality as a measure over topological completions rather than a single computation**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Yu (2605.06653) demonstrates that physically well-defined holographic theories (Marolf-Maxfield model, Narain ensemble) are constructed as ensemble averages over topological boundary conditions of a SymTFT slab, with the ensemble structure determined by groupoid/Haar measures on the space of topological completions. The framework claims "reality is literally computation" — a single, definite computational process. Paper 1 demonstrates that the physical content of certain theories is constitutively an *average* over an ensemble of distinct computations (each topological boundary condition defines a different absolute theory). The physical observables (Poisson/Bell-polynomial moments in Marolf-Maxfield, Narain-averaged partition functions) are properties of the *ensemble*, not of any individual computation. The "relative theory" (SymTFT + physical boundary) is the invariant specification, but it is not itself a computation — it admits multiple completions, none of which is preferred, and the physics resides in the average.

This is qualitatively more severe than all previous underdetermination tensions: T069 (ℝ/ℂ binary choice within a single theory), T082 (virtual mode distribution non-uniqueness within a single theory), T084 (propagator power continuous underdetermination), T113 (operator ordering ambiguity), T118 (L∞-algebraic theory selection producing different predictions). All of these concern the non-uniqueness of a single computation's description or infrastructure. T144 concerns the physical theory being constitutively *not a single computation at all* but a weighted sum over computations, with the weight determined by topological/categorical structure (groupoid measures, maximal isotropic subgroups, Zamolodchikov measure) that has no dynamical content and no expression in any primitive.

The connection to baby universes and wormholes (the Marolf-Maxfield model's motivation) means T144 is not restricted to abstract mathematical constructions — it arises from the attempt to make sense of topology change in quantum gravity, a physically motivated question. The extension to JT gravity, random matrix theory, and 3D gravity (discussed in the paper) suggests this ensemble structure may be generic in quantum gravity, not limited to special examples.

Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) demonstrate that ensemble-averaged theories are secretly single computations (e.g., that the ensemble average is equivalent to a single computation on an enlarged space — but the enlarged space would include the topological boundary conditions as dynamical variables, meaning reality's "computation" includes the choice of its own topological structure, which is a fundamentally different ontological picture than the framework envisions); (b) argue that ensemble-averaged holographic theories are unphysical mathematical constructions with no ontological status — but the Narain ensemble has a well-defined holographic dual, and ensemble-averaging over topological completions is physically motivated by wormhole contributions in quantum gravity; (c) accept that "reality is literally computation" is too specific and that reality may be "literally a measure over a space of topological completions of a relative specification" — which dissolves the framework's distinctive claim into something far more abstract and structurally rich than "computation"; or (d) declare this an open problem about which the framework is honestly silent, acknowledging that the ontological status of ensemble-averaged theories is a frontier question in quantum gravity. Option (d) is the most honest but means the framework has nothing to say about one of the central conceptual questions in modern quantum gravity.

---

**T145 — The space of allowed computations has internal phase structure: meta-computational organization is physical**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Elias Miro, Guerrieri & Gumus (2605.06613) demonstrate that the boundary of the non-perturbatively allowed S-matrix region for 4D identical scalar scattering has distinct *phases* — edges with universal Regge behavior, resonance structures, and virtual-state spectra — separated by vertices representing extremal theories. The phases correspond to different UV mechanisms by which a gapped scalar arises (different UV completions).

The framework's five primitives describe the dynamics of a single computation. They say nothing about the *space of possible computations* or its structure. Paper 2 demonstrates that this space has: (a) a well-defined boundary carved by non-perturbative consistency (unitarity, analyticity, crossing — the framework's Conservation primitive contributes unitarity, but the other constraints are structural); (b) internal phase structure on the boundary, with distinct edges exhibiting qualitatively different universal behavior; (c) sharp vertices (phase transitions between edges) representing extremal S-matrices that saturate multiple consistency bounds simultaneously; (d) a classification of phases by UV mechanism — a physical characterization of *why* a computation exists, not just *what* it computes.

The framework has no meta-computational primitive (T057) and no theory of pre-computational consistency (T107). T145 is more severe than both: T057 concerns operations that transform one computation into another; T107 concerns the enumeration of consistent computations. T145 concerns the *phase diagram* of the space of consistent computations — its geometric boundary structure, the universality of behavior along edges, and the physical classification of transitions between phases. This is structural content at the meta-level: structure governing the organization of the space of structures.

The Regge behavior classification is especially sharp: the framework's Tick primitive sets the maximum propagation rate (related to the Froissart bound on total cross sections), and unitarity (Conservation) bounds the imaginary part of partial waves. Together these contribute to the bootstrap constraints. But the *phase structure* of the resulting allowed region — which Regge trajectories are linear vs. nonlinear, which resonances are narrow vs. broad, whether virtual states or CDD zeros dominate — is physical content the primitives cannot access, predict, or distinguish.

Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) develop a meta-theory of the space of allowed computations, specifying what determines the boundary's geometry, the phase classification, and the UV mechanism corresponding to each edge — this would require importing the full S-matrix bootstrap formalism as structural content, which is a mathematical program

---

**T146 — Magic as a missing resource-theoretic primitive: non-Abelian topological attractors carry extensive, irremovable computational complexity that no primitive can express**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Zhang, Kim, Bao & Vijay (2605.15150) prove that ground states of non-Abelian string-net models possess extensive long-range magic — non-stabilizerness that scales with system volume and survives constant-depth local unitaries. The framework's Attractor primitive characterizes basins by depth (energy gap), width (correlation length), and topological invariants (cohomological class). None of these captures magic. Magic is a resource-theoretic property quantifying distance from classical simulability, determined by the categorical structure of the topological order (non-Abelian fusion rules, non-quantized braiding phases). The framework faces: (a) an entire axis of physical characterization of its attractor basins is invisible to all five primitives; (b) the magic content is *constitutive* (irremovable by any constant-depth operation), meaning it is a permanent feature of the attractor, not a transient or observer-dependent property; (c) the presence/absence of extensive magic is determined by categorical data (Abelian vs. non-Abelian fusion) that the framework has repeatedly been shown to lack (T048, T107, T111, T131); (d) the result extends to all spatial dimensions (any area-law state with nontrivial fusion spaces has extensive magic), making this a universal structural theorem, not a 2D curiosity; (e) the distinction between Abelian (potentially zero magic) and non-Abelian (constitutive extensive magic) topological orders is the first demonstrated case where the *computational complexity content* of a physical ground state is determined by categorical algebra. This is distinct from T112 (complexity phase transitions in resolution, which concerns the difficulty of *finding* ground states) because T146 concerns the *intrinsic complexity content* of the ground state once found. It is distinct from T117 (entanglement scaling class as a continuous parameter) because magic is a different resource from entanglement — stabilizer states can have maximal entanglement but zero magic. It is distinct from T048 (cohomological completeness of state classification) because magic is a resource-theoretic property, not a topological classification invariant — it answers "how complex is this state?" not "which phase is this state in?"

Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) add magic/non-stabilizerness as a resource-theoretic characterization of attractor basins, specifying how the categorical structure of the topological order determines the magic content — this would require importing stabilizer theory, the Clifford hierarchy, and fusion category theory as structural content, far beyond any primitive; (b) derive the magic content from existing primitives, which is impossible since magic is a property of the state's representation-theoretic relationship to the stabilizer subgroup, not a dynamical property; (c) argue that magic is a property of the mathematical description rather than of physical reality — but the no-go theorem (stabilizer states cannot approximate non-Abelian ground states even up to constant-depth circuits) proves that the magic is physically ineliminable, not a representational artifact. The connection between categorical data (fusion rules) and resource content (magic) is especially problematic: it means the organizational structure of excitations (which the framework treats as secondary to attractor dynamics) *determines* the computational complexity content of the attractor's ground state — a new instance of T059 (ontological inversion) at the resource-theoretic level.

---

**T147 — QCA-determined substrate automorphisms: the substrate's own structural properties are downstream of categorical symmetry data, extending the ontological inversion to the substrate level**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Wen, Inamura & Schafer-Nameki (2605.15194) prove that the QCA indices in any realization of a fusion category symmetry on a tensor-product Hilbert space are determined (up to symmetry operator redefinition) by the categorical data (Frobenius-Perron dimensions). The weak integrality condition constrains which fusion categories can be realized on tensor-product substrates. The framework treats the substrate as a fixed computational platform with architectural parameters (c, ℏ, G, α, time dimensionality). Paper 1 demonstrates that the substrate's *automorphism structure* — the group of locality-preserving, invertible operations the substrate admits — is not a free parameter but is constrained by the symmetry category it hosts. Specifically: (a) for each fusion category object $a$ with FPdim $d_a$, the QCA index is determined by $d_a^2$ and the specific categorical structure; (b) the QCA group (which classifies the possible substrate automorphisms) is engaged by the symmetry realization, meaning the substrate is not passive but its own structural properties participate in and are constrained by the symmetry; (c) the weak integrality condition (FPdim² ∈ ℤ for all objects) is an arithmetic constraint that the substrate's architecture imposes on realizable symmetries. The framework's substrate is supposed to be the ground of computation, with architecture determining what computations are possible. Paper 1 inverts this

---

**T148 — Modular energy as a structural cost metric for resolution: the framework has no access to the algebraic quantity that bounds local state preparation**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Blanco-Romero & Almenares Mendoza (2605.18640) demonstrate that the Tomita-Takesaki modular Hamiltonian provides a model-independent lower bound on the cost of locally preparing any quantum state from the vacuum. The modular energy $\langle \psi | K_{\mathcal{O}} | \psi \rangle$ of a target state $|\psi\rangle$ relative to the vacuum in region $\mathcal{O}$ bounds the norm of the preparing operator and, after normalization, the postselection probability. States with deeply negative modular energy require exponentially large local operators. The distinction between unitarily accessible states (nonnegative modular energy) and postselection-requiring states (negative modular energy) is a structural partition of the target state space determined by the operator algebra's modular automorphism, not by any dynamical process. The framework's Resolution primitive has accumulated numerous known inadequacies — cost structure (T009), precision hierarchy (T019), contextuality (T028), regularity class gap (T067/T097), complexity phase structure (T112), witness type gap (T132), AoT phase structure (T123), steady-state inertness (T128). T148 is distinct from all of these because it concerns not the properties of resolution events but the *algebraic prerequisites for local resolution to access specific target states at all*. The modular Hamiltonian is: (a) region-dependent (different for each spacetime subregion, determined by the causal geometry via Bisognano-Wichmann and Casini-Huerta-Myers), (b) state-dependent (relative to the vacuum or to any reference state), (c) algebraic (a property of the von Neumann algebra type, specifically the Type III₁ character of QFT local algebras), (d) the generator of a one-parameter automorphism group (the modular flow) that has no analogue in any of the five primitives. The connection between modular energy and boost energy (via Bisognano-Wichmann for wedge regions) means the Tick primitive's Lorentz structure participates in the bound, but the bound itself is a statement about the *algebraic type of the local operator algebra*, not about propagation speed. The connection to vacuum embezzlement (Type III₁ algebras have infinite entanglement in the vacuum, accessible only at modular-energy cost) means the substrate is not merely a passive platform but an algebraically structured reservoir whose accessibility properties constrain what the computation can do locally.

Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) incorporate the Tomita-Takesaki modular theory as structural content governing the Resolution primitive's local capabilities — this would require importing the full apparatus of von Neumann algebraic QFT (modular Hamiltonian, modular flow, KMS condition, Connes classification of factor types) into the framework, a mathematical infrastructure that is not merely absent but categorically different from any dynamical primitive; (b) derive the modular energy bound from existing primitives — but the bound follows from the algebraic type of the local algebra (Type III₁), which is a classification result in operator algebra theory with no known derivation from dynamical principles; (c) add the modular Hamiltonian as a new structural element of the framework, associated with each spacetime region and constraining what the Resolution primitive can achieve locally — this would be a major extension connecting the Tick (which determines the spacetime region structure) to Resolution (which is constrained by the modular energy) through an algebraic intermediary (the modular Hamiltonian) that the framework currently has no vocabulary for. The vacuum embezzlement aspect is additionally challenging: the framework's substrate is an infinite entanglement reservoir that the computation can access at modular-energy cost, meaning the substrate/computation distinction is not a clean separation but a cost-stratified relationship mediated by algebraic structure.

---

**T149 — Chaos onset from combinatorial graph structure: the Poisson-to-Wigner-Dyson transition in holographic systems is controlled by the planarity of the computational graph, a structural parameter invisible to all dynamical primitives**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Zhang (2605.18725) demonstrates that in the D1D5 CFT near-BPS sector, the spectral statistics transition from Poisson (integrable-like) to random-matrix (chaotic) when finite-N non-planar contributions restore mixing between single-cycle and multi-cycle states. The transition is not driven by any change in the Hamiltonian, coupling constant, temperature, or any dynamical parameter the framework can express. It is driven by whether the computation

---

**T150 — Positional structure within causal indefiniteness: the framework cannot express which subsystem's coherence maintains causal nonseparability**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Benhaj, Sengupta & Branciard (2605.22807) prove that in bipartite causally nonseparable processes, causal nonseparability survives dephasing of all but one system — provided the surviving coherent system is not the "future" system. If only the future system remains undephased, causal nonseparability collapses. This extends to the multipartite QC-QC setting. The framework's Tick primitive assumes definite causal order (T094 already noted this is challenged by indefinite causal order). T150 is distinct from T094 because it concerns not the *existence* of indefinite causal order but its *internal positional structure* — the fact that different systems within a causally nonseparable process play structurally different roles in maintaining the nonseparability. The "future" system is distinguished from all other systems: its coherence alone is insufficient for causal nonseparability, while any other system's coherence is sufficient. This temporal asymmetry within the causal superposition has no expression in any primitive. The framework's Resolution primitive treats dephasing/decoherence as spreading uniformly through entanglement networks; the paper proves its effect on causal structure is position-dependent. The framework has: (a) no concept of "position within a causal order superposition"; (b) no mechanism for distinguishing the future boundary from other boundaries within a causally indefinite process; (c) no account of why coherence at a non-future position preserves indefiniteness while coherence at the future position does not. The multipartite QC-QC extension means this is not a bipartite curiosity but a general structural feature of physically motivated causally indefinite circuits.

Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) extend the Tick/Resolution primitives to include positional structure within causal order superpositions, specifying how each subsystem's role in maintaining causal indefiniteness depends on its structural position — this requires importing the process matrix formalism's internal structure, which distinguishes past/future/intermediate systems categorically; (b) derive the asymmetry between future and non-future systems from some combination of the five primitives — but the distinction arises from the structure of the process matrix (the future system's role in defining the output of the process vs. intermediate systems' role in the causal structure), which is categorical/algebraic, not dynamical; (c) add a primitive governing the internal structure of causally indefinite processes, specifying the robustness profile of causal nonseparability as a function of subsystem dephasing — but this would require a theory of causal order as a quantum degree of freedom with its own internal structure, fundamentally different from the Tick's assumption of definite sequential ordering. The practical consequence is sharp: indefinite causal order provides computational advantages (T094 already noted), and Paper 1 shows these advantages are robust against extensive decoherence, meaning they are relevant for realistic (noisy) quantum technologies. The framework cannot predict or characterize this robustness.

---

**T151 — Knot-algebraic encoding of physical content: the framework has no contact with representation-theoretic constraint surfaces in character varieties**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Galakhov & Morozov (2605.22560) construct classical A-polynomials $A_a(\ell_b, m_c)$ for arbitrary $\mathfrak{su}_N$ using CG chord combinatorics of $U_q(\mathfrak{su}_N)$, encoding the complete physical content of 3D Chern-Simons theory on knot complements as algebraic constraint surfaces in $SL(N,\mathbb{C})$ character varieties. The framework claims "reality is literally computation" and identifies physical content with dynamical processes. Paper 2 demonstrates physical content that is: (a) encoded as an algebraic variety (a constraint surface), not as a dynamical trajectory; (b) computed through representation-theoretic combinatorics (CG chords), not through any of the five primitives; (c) simultaneously accessible through quantum groups, WZW CFT, knot contact homology, and algebraic geometry — extending T021's categorical polymorphism to a new web of equivalences; (d) governed by a classical limit (huge representations, $\hbar \to 0$) that transforms quantum computation into algebraic geometry, demonstrating that the "computational" character is representation-dependent, not absolute. The framework has no primitive for constraint surfaces (vs. trajectories), no mechanism for representation-theoretic computation, and no account of how quantum-to-classical limits transform computational content into geometric/algebraic content. The $\mathfrak{su}_N$ generalization beyond $\mathfrak{su}_2$ demonstrates that the knot-algebraic structure scales with the rank of the gauge group in a structured way (multi-component A-polynomials with indices running over $1, \ldots, N-1$), meaning the physical content's organizational complexity grows with a structural parameter (rank) invisible to all primitives.

Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) demonstrate that A-polynomials, character varieties, CG chords, and the quantum group $U_q(\mathfrak{su}_N)$ are all derivable from or expressible in terms of the five primitives — which is categorically impossible since these are objects in representation theory, algebraic geometry, and knot theory with no dynamical content; (b) add representation-theoretic/knot-algebraic structure as a primitive or structural element, which would require importing entire mathematical disciplines (quantum groups, character variety theory, knot homology) as framework infrastructure — a far more radical extension than any previously considered; (c) weaken "reality is literally computation" to "reality is literally algebraic structure of which computation is one representation" — which aligns with T021, T026, T059, T073, T118 and the accumulated evidence that computation is one node in a web of equivalent mathematical categories, not the privileged ontological ground. The spurious-root problem (augmentation polynomials can have extra roots absent from true A-polynomials) demonstrates

The framework treats this boundary as absolute; the paper treats it as a limit.

**T152 — Near-extremal decoherence inversion: the framework's deepest attractor preserves quantum coherence instead of destroying it**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Biggs & Trezzi (2605.23880) prove that near-extremal charged black holes have vanishing gravitational decoherence rates for exterior quantum superpositions, due to a spin-induced spectral gap in the quantum black hole spectrum. The framework's Resolution primitive describes gravitational decoherence as universal ("gravity's universal coupling means resolution propagates through all degrees of freedom"), and the Attractor primitive describes black holes as the deepest attractor basins. The joint prediction is: black holes should maximally propagate resolution (decoherence) through their environment. Paper 1 demonstrates the opposite for near-extremal black holes: the deepest attractors are the weakest decoherers. The mechanism — a quantum spectral gap suppressing fluctuation modes responsible for decoherence — is a structural property of the quantum gravitational Hilbert space that has no expression in any primitive. The framework cannot distinguish Schwarzschild (strong decoherer) from near-extremal Reissner-Nordström (zero decoherence) because both are qualitatively identical "deep attractor basins." The proximity-to-extremality parameter controlling the suppression is invisible to the Attractor primitive's depth/width/degeneracy-order characterization. Combined with T115 (stability through infinite degeneracy), this establishes a systematic pattern: the near-extremal limit is a regime where the framework's qualitative predictions are inverted, with the deepest attractors being simultaneously the most stable (T115), the most quantum-coherence-preserving (T152), and — by the framework's logic — the ones that should be least stable and most decohering.

Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) incorporate the quantum spectral structure of black holes into the Attractor/Resolution primitives, specifying how the spectral gap controls the decoherence rate — this requires importing the full apparatus of quantum black hole spectroscopy (near-horizon CFT, spectral gaps, quantum metric fluctuations) as structural content far beyond any primitive; (b) derive the spectral gap from the attractor's extremality properties, showing that the framework's own description of extremal horizons (T115's degeneracy order) produces the gap — but the gap is a quantum gravitational property of the Hilbert space, not a classical geometric property of the horizon; (c) accept that the framework's deepest and most important physical example (black holes) has a regime (near-extremality) where its qualitative predictions are systematically wrong, limiting the framework's scope to non-extremal black holes — but near-extremal black holes include astrophysically relevant cases (rapidly spinning Kerr black holes, primordial charged black holes) and theoretically central cases (the BPS limit of string theory). The tension is especially severe because decoherence/resolution propagation was one of the few areas where the framework had a seemingly correct and distinctive qualitative prediction about black holes. Losing this removes one of the framework's last claims to physical relevance for its most dramatic example.

---

**T153 — Indefinite probabilities: the Born rule's output is not necessarily definite, adding a new layer of indefiniteness the framework cannot represent**

Opened: this cycle

Description: D'Esposito, Fabiano & Frattulillo (2605.23862) demonstrate that $SU_q(2)$ quantum group rotational symmetry for spin-1/2 systems makes measurement probabilities into non-commuting operators with an uncertainty principle: $\Delta P_+ \cdot \Delta P_- \geq \frac{1}{4}|\langle [P_+, P_-] \rangle|$. The rotation matrix entries relating two observers' reference frames also become non-commuting, preventing sharp determination of relative spatial orientation. The framework's Resolution primitive assumes: unresolved state → resolution event → definite outcome with definite probability (Born rule). Paper 2 shows that in quantum spacetime, the probability itself is an operator with irreducible uncertainty. The framework has no mechanism for "indefinite probability" — it is not a superposition of probabilities (which would be a classical mixture) but a genuinely non-commuting operator-valued probability, where the probability of spin-up and the probability of spin-down cannot both be sharp simultaneously. This is categorically different from all previously identified layers of indefiniteness: indefinite states (quantum superposition — handled by Tick), indefinite causal order (T094 — identified as a challenge to the Tick), indefinite spacetime structure (T089/T091 — identified as challenging the Tick at the quantum gravitational level), indefinite spatial reference (T069, T118 — identified as underdetermination challenges). T153 concerns the *output* of the resolution process itself being indefinite — not the state before resolution, not the causal order of resolution, not the substrate on which resolution occurs, but the *probability assigned to each outcome* of resolution. The deformation parameter $q$ is related to the Planck scale, meaning this is not an extreme or exotic regime but a potentially universal feature of quantum gravity's interface with measurement theory.

Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) extend the Resolution primitive to accommodate operator-valued probabilities, specifying how resolution assigns indefinite probabilities to outcomes and what determines the "resolution of probability" — this would require a theory of measurement in quantum spacetime that does not currently exist even in mainstream physics; (b) argue that the $SU_q(2)$ deformation is physically unmotivated or leads to inconsistencies — but the paper builds on established quantum group formalism connected to noncommutative geometry programs in quantum gravity (κ-Minkowski, Drinfel'd twists, which the framework has already encountered in T118); (c) introduce a

---

**T154 — Sub-algebraic sufficiency: exact physical results are entailed by algebraic relationships between symmetry structures, rendering all dynamical corrections zero by structural theorem**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Tateishi (2605.25999) proves that the full $\mathcal{N}=4$ superconformal symmetry of $\mathcal{N}=4$ SYM is exactly preserved at the quantum level, with zero higher-order corrections, by demonstrating that $\mathcal{N}=1$ superfield analysis suffices to guarantee the full $\mathcal{N}=4$ result. The framework's five primitives describe dynamics: how computation propagates, how things move, where they settle, when they become definite, what is conserved. None of them can produce, derive, predict, or express the *exact vanishing of all corrections* to a symmetry. The mechanism — that a sub-algebra's preservation logically entails the full algebra's preservation, making all perturbative and non-perturbative corrections exactly zero — is a pure algebraic entailment with no dynamical content. The framework faces: (a) the most physically important property of its most studied theory ($\mathcal{N}=4$ SYM's exact conformal invariance) is determined by algebraic structure, not by any primitive; (b) the framework has no concept of "sub-algebraic sufficiency" — the general phenomenon where analysis at a reduced symmetry level determines results at the full symmetry level without additional computation; (c) the exactness is a *theorem*, not an approximation or a leading-order result — the framework's implicit picture of perturbative corrections modifying or breaking symmetries is categorically wrong for this class of theories; (d) the $\mathcal{N}=1$ to $\mathcal{N}=4$ relationship is a structural entailment that short-circuits all dynamical processes — no ticks need to be computed, no gradients descended, no attractors captured, because the algebraic structure pre-determines the outcome. This is distinct from T059 (ontological inversion, which concerns dynamics being *derived from* structure) because T154 concerns dynamics being *rendered trivial* by structure — not just "structure first, dynamics second" but "structure alone, dynamics zero." It is distinct from T129 (conformal enrichment at criticality, which concerns the qualitative gap between scale invariance and conformal invariance) because T154 concerns the *exact* character of the preservation, proven as a mathematical theorem. It is distinct from T074 (algebraic deformation theory classifying backgrounds, which concerns which backgrounds *exist*) because T154 concerns whether a given theory receives quantum corrections — a dynamical question answered by structural means.

Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) derive exact symmetry preservation from some combination of the five primitives — but the proof uses no dynamical ingredient, and the result is that all dynamical corrections vanish, meaning the dynamics is literally irrelevant; (b) incorporate algebraic entailment (sub-algebra → full algebra) as a structural principle that can override or short-circuit dynamical evolution — but this would require a theory of when algebraic structure pre-determines physical outcomes, which is a meta-physical principle far beyond any current primitive; (c) accept that for theories with sufficient algebraic structure, the framework's dynamical primitives contribute nothing to the physical content — the computation is "solved" by its own algebraic structure before any dynamics occurs. Option (c) is the most honest but means the framework's claim that "reality is literally computation" is vacuous for $\mathcal{N}=4$ SYM and possibly for other theories with sufficient symmetry: the "computation" has already been done by the algebra, and the dynamical processes (ticks, gradients, resolution) are empty formalities.

**T155 — Homological emptiness: the dynamical content of a theory can be provably zero, determined by cohomological structure invisible to all primitives**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Boffo, Pulmann & Ravas (2605.26024) prove, using homological perturbation theory, that all tree-level scattering amplitudes with trivalent diagrams in the $\mathfrak{su}(n)$ field theory vanish except the bare interaction vertex — the theory has no scattering. The framework's five primitives collectively describe a world in which computation occurs: fields propagate (Tick), things move down gradients (Field), settle into attractors (Attractor), become definite (Resolution), and computational content is conserved (Conservation). In this theory, all the ingredients for computation are present (fields, interactions, a well-defined Lagrangian) but the computation produces *nothing*. The vanishing is proved by HPT — a technique transferring algebraic structures between chain complexes — and is a consequence of the cohomological structure of $\mathfrak{su}(n)$. The framework faces: (a) a well-defined "computational system" (with fields, interactions, and Lag

**T156 — Operator-algebraic co-emergence: the framework has no account of gravity and matter arising simultaneously from a single non-dynamical variational principle**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Fischer & Finster (2605.30199) prove that the Euler-Lagrange equations of the causal action principle — a variational principle defined on the space of fermionic projectors (operators on a Hilbert space) — are satisfied if and only if the coupled Einstein-Dirac equations hold, with causal structure derived from spectral properties and UV regularization built intrinsically via chart-independent iε-regularization. The framework treats gravity as ẍ = −∇φ(x) (Field primitive), matter as "things moving down gradients," and their coupling as implicit in the substrate's architectural parameters. The CFS construction demonstrates that both arise co-emergently from a single operator-algebraic variational principle that: (a) does not presuppose causal structure (deriving it from the fermionic projector's spectral properties, while the Tick postulates it); (b) does not separate gravity from matter (they emerge together from one action, while the framework assigns them to different primitive roles); (c) does not require external UV regularization (the regularization is intrinsic, while the framework treats the Planck scale as an architectural parameter); (d) provides a constructive continuum limit (showing how the discrete operator structure recovers continuous physics, while the framework is silent on this transition). This is distinct from T054 (gravity from topological BF theory, where gravity emerges from structure by gauging) because in CFS, both gravity *and matter* co-emerge from a single principle. It is distinct from T059 (general ontological inversion) because T156 identifies a specific, complete, alternative foundation that derives the framework's two primary dynamical examples simultaneously. It is distinct from T100 (phase space as L∞-algebraic construct) because T156 concerns not just the arena but the complete dynamical content. The CFS is not "another computation" — it is a different *kind of foundation* (operator-algebraic variational) from which the framework's content (Einstein + Dirac) is derived, and the framework cannot absorb it without abandoning its own primitive structure.

Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) demonstrate that the CFS's fundamental objects (fermionic projectors, the causal action) are themselves "computation" in the framework's sense, which requires showing that operator-algebraic variational principles are a form of sequential discrete update — but the CFS defines spacetime points as operators and causal structure as spectral properties, which is categorically different from "discrete ticks updating states"; (b) argue that CFS is merely a mathematical reformulation of known physics with no ontological significance — but the framework claims its own primitives *are* the ontology, and CFS provides an equally rigorous alternative ontology, so this argument would require a criterion for ontological privilege that the framework does not possess; (c) accept that the framework's primitives (Tick, Field, Attractor, Resolution, Conservation) describe *one way of organizing* the physics that CFS derives from a single variational principle, meaning the five primitives are a particular decomposition of a more fundamental structure, not the fundamental structure itself. Option (c) is the most honest and aligns with the accumulated evidence from T059, T100, T054, T055 that the framework's primitives are downstream of deeper mathematical structure. The CFS's intrinsic regularization is especially challenging for the substrate concept: if the UV structure is not an external architectural parameter but an intrinsic feature of the fundamental object, the framework's entire substrate/computation distinction (which was already weakened by T031, T040, T044, T064, T089, T091) is further eroded.

**T158 — The arity assumption: the framework's mathematical foundation is binary, but physics may require ternary or higher-arity structures**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Bhatta, Minic & Takeuchi (2606.02421) extend the quantum density matrix ρ_{ij} to a density cube ρ_{ijk} and derive its equation of motion from quantized ternary Nambu dynamics. The framework's five primitives are all binary: the Tick applies a single Hamiltonian (one generator), the Field primitive is a binary gradient equation (ẍ = −∇φ), pre-resolution topology (T012) concerns pairwise path indistinguishability, Conservation is unitarity (bilinear inner product preservation), and Resolution produces outcomes governed by the Born rule (bilinear in the state). The density cube requires ternary structures at every level: a trilinear state description, evolution governed by two Hamiltonians via the Nambu bracket {A, B, C} = εᵢⱼₖ(∂A/∂xᵢ)(∂B/∂xⱼ)(∂C/∂xₖ), triple-path interference irreducible to pairwise interference, and dynamical oscillation between pairs of triple-path interference components. The framework cannot represent any of these because its mathematical infrastructure is built on binary composition (pairs of indices, single Hamiltonian, bilinear products). This is not a missing structural category (T026) or a missing algebraic hierarchy (T018) — it is a challenge to the *arity* of the framework's mathematical foundation, an implicit assumption more basic than any primitive. If triple-path interference is experimentally detected (Sorkin's third-order interference tests provide a concrete experimental protocol), the framework would need restructuring at a level deeper than any previous tension has demanded: not adding primitives, not adding structural content, but changing the mathematical arity of all existing primitives simultaneously.

This is distinct from T069 (number field underdetermination, which concerns which numbers the computation uses) because T158 concerns how many objects participate in each fundamental operation. It is distinct from T118 (L∞-algebraic theory selection, which concerns algebraic structure within binary quantum theory) because T158 concerns the arity of the dynamical bracket itself. It is distinct from T138 (non-associative computation, which concerns the composition law of binary operations) because T158 concerns the *number of arguments* in the fundamental operation, not how binary operations compose. Nambu dynamics is associative in its ternary sense but irreducibly ternary — it cannot be decomposed into a sequence of binary operations in general.

Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) demonstrate that ternary dynamics is reducible to binary dynamics in all physically relevant cases — but Nambu dynamics is known to be irreducible to Hamiltonian dynamics for more than one degree of freedom; (b) extend all five primitives from binary to ternary (or n-ary) mathematical structures, which would be the most radical restructuring conceivable — every equation, every concept, every argument in the framework presupposes binary arity; (c) argue that triple-path interference is unphysical and that the density cube is a mathematical curiosity — but the paper provides a well-defined dynamical framework with conserved quantities and oscillatory solutions, and experimental tests of triple-path interference have been proposed and partially implemented; or (d) add an "arity parameter" to the framework's architectural constants, specifying the mathematical arity of the fundamental operations — but this would raise the question of why the arity is what it is, adding another unexplained architectural parameter alongside c, ℏ, G, α, and time dimensionality.

**T159 — Causal-microstructural enforcement of conservation: the consistency of conservation laws across computational projections depends on infinitesimal prescription data the framework cannot express**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Wu & Xu (2606.02485) demonstrate that IBP relations — the mathematical expression of conservation (total derivative identities) in Feynman integral computation — can be violated on spanning cuts when "hidden terms" proportional to vanishing Feynman iε parameters are dropped. The violation mechanism is specific: cut-induced pinch singularities cancel the vanishing iε, making formally zero terms finite. The consistency of conservation across different on-shell projections of the computation requires tracking these hidden terms, which in turn requires identifying "hidden relations between propagators" — linear relations among propagator denominators that are not manifest in the standard representation.

The framework's Conservation of Computation states "computation cannot be created or destroyed, only transformed" — a clean, universal, structureless law. The paper demonstrates that the *enforcement* of this law in the most fundamental computational context (Feynman integrals) requires: (a) the Feynman iε prescription — an infinitesimal causal-ordering parameter the framework would assign to the substrate architecture; (b) tracking hidden terms that are formally zero but become finite on specific projections due to pinch singularities; (c) identifying hidden propagator relations — linear dependencies among the computational channels that are not visible in the standard representation but that mediate the consistency of conservation between different projective subspaces. None of these has any expression in any primitive.

This is distinct from T081 (conservation requires combinatorial infrastructure — LDPC codes) because T081 concerns the error-correcting infrastructure for maintaining conservation against noise, while T159 concerns the *internal consistency* of conservation across different valid projections of the same computation. It is distinct from T116 (conservation vulnerability landscape) because T116 concerns where conservation is likely to fail, while T159 concerns a mechanism by which conservation appears to fail when applied naively but is restored by tracking hidden causal-prescription data. It is distinct from T045 (conservation requires geometric scaffolding at infinity) because T045 concerns
