**T160 — Resource-type non-fungibility: the Conservation primitive's transformation clause is subject to categorical constraints it cannot express**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Ahmed & Smith (2606.05097) prove that Gaussian quantum operations, homodyne measurements, and arbitrary classical communication cannot enhance the quantum capacity of a pure-loss channel beyond direct transmission. The framework's Conservation of Computation states "computation cannot be created or destroyed, only transformed." The no-go theorem proves that certain transformations are categorically impossible: no composition of Gaussian resources can produce non-Gaussian computational capacity. The Conservation primitive's transformation clause is unconstrained — it implies any computational resource can be transformed into any other — but the physics has strict resource-type boundaries that partition computational resources into non-fungible categories. The fractional extendibility framework — a continuous relaxation of the integer k-extendibility hierarchy, with monotonicity under Gaussian channels and closed-form for Gaussian states — is the structural tool proving the impossibility, and it is a resource-theoretic ordering of quantum states parameterized by a continuous real number that no primitive can express.

This is distinct from T127 (discrete replica thresholds, which concerns integer-valued thresholds for nonlinear moment estimation) because T160 concerns a *categorical* impossibility (no amount of Gaussian resources suffices, not just "fewer than k copies are insufficient"). It is distinct from T081 (conservation requires combinatorial infrastructure for enforcement) because T081 concerns the practical infrastructure for maintaining conservation, while T160 concerns a fundamental impossibility in the transformation between resource types. It is distinct from T111 (generalized symmetries producing non-conservation-law constraints) because T160 concerns constraints on the *transformation clause* of conservation, not on the *sector structure* of the state space.

Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) extend the Conservation primitive to include a resource-type hierarchy specifying which computational resources can be transformed into which — this would require importing the full apparatus of quantum resource theory (monotones, free operations, resource conversion rates) as structural content far beyond any primitive; (b) derive the Gaussian/non-Gaussian boundary from the five primitives, which is impossible since the distinction depends on the phase-space representation theory of the Heisenberg-Weyl group — algebraic structure the framework cannot express; or (c) accept that the transformation clause is an idealization: in practice, computational resources come in types that are categorically non-interconvertible, and the Conservation primitive is correct about preservation but wrong (by omission) about transformation. The continuous character of fractional extendibility adds a specific mathematical challenge: the non-fungibility is parameterized by a *continuous* resource-theoretic ordering, not a discrete classification, and the framework has no mechanism for continuous resource parameters.

**T161 — Thermal positivity as a derivation gap: the framework's premises entail quantitative thermodynamic constraints that the framework's formalism cannot derive**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Cheung & Rosen (2606.05136) prove that Lorentz invariance and unitarity jointly impose strict positivity on all perturbative interaction corrections to the pressure of the form $T^{2D-4+4k}$ ($k>0$) for weakly coupled relativistic massless boson EFTs. The framework's Tick primitive produces Lorentz invariance (speed of light as maximum propagation rate) and the Conservation primitive produces unitarity (information conservation). Together, these are the paper's two premises. The conclusion — an infinite family of sign constraints on thermodynamic quantities — is therefore *entailed by the framework's own architectural principles*. But the derivation requires: (a) the KMS condition relating thermal and vacuum physics (a structural relationship between equilibrium statistical mechanics and quantum field theory); (b) the optical theorem converting unitarity into positivity of spectral functions (an algebraic identity); (c) Lorentz-covariant dispersion relations (analytic structure of scattering amplitudes); (d) dimensional analysis and loop-counting in perturbation theory (the renormalization group hierarchy). None of these intermediary steps has any expression in the five primitives. The framework *has* the axioms but *lacks* the deductive machinery. This is the sharpest instance yet of a general pattern: the framework's premises are physically correct but its formalism is insufficient to derive the consequences of its own premises.

This is distinct from T026 (structural content gap, which concerns structural content the framework lacks) because T161 concerns structural content the framework *needs to derive consequences of its own principles* — it is not missing content from external physics but missing the internal deductive apparatus to exploit its own axioms. It is distinct from T129 (conformal enrichment of criticality, which concerns the qualitative gap between scale invariance and conformal invariance at critical points) because T161 concerns a *quantitative* constraint (sign of specific thermodynamic coefficients) derivable from the framework's premises but not by the framework's methods. It is distinct from T130 (gravitational energy floor geometrically guaranteed, which concerns a geometric theorem enforcing conservation) because T161 concerns a *thermodynamic* constraint enforced by the joint action of two primitives through analytic/algebraic intermediaries.

**T162 — Topology-changing dynamics: the framework has no primitive for dynamical modification of the computational topology by the Hamiltonian itself**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Chandrasekaran (2606.06404) demonstrates that the gravitational Hamiltonian in JT gravity acts through pair-of-pants topology-changing interactions, mapping a single-interior Hilbert space sector to a connected two-interior sector. The framework's five primitives all assume a fixed computational topology: the Tick propagates within a fixed causal structure, the Field describes gradients on a fixed space, the Attractor describes basins in a fixed landscape, Resolution accumulates on a fixed ledger, and Conservation operates within a fixed Hilbert space. Paper 1 demonstrates that the *fundamental dynamical evolution* — the gravitational Hamiltonian generating time evolution — changes the topology of the computational arena. This is not an exotic or optional feature: topology change is the mechanism that resolves the firewall paradox and produces smooth horizons with unitary Page-curve evolution. Without topology change, the framework's two most important primitives (Conservation and Attractor) are in irreconcilable tension at the black hole horizon (T135).

This is distinct from T087 (topology-induced causal shortcuts, which concerns wormhole topology connecting otherwise disconnected regions in a *fixed* topology) because T162 concerns *dynamical* topology change generated by the Hamiltonian. It is distinct from T057 (meta-computational primitives, which concerns operations that transform theories) because the topology change here is not a transformation between theories but a dynamical process *within* a single theory — the Hamiltonian of JT gravity includes topology-changing terms as part of its standard action. It is distinct from T054 (dynamics from topological BF theory, which concerns deriving a dynamical theory from a non-dynamical starting point) because T162 concerns topology change as an ongoing dynamical process, not as a one-time derivational step. It is distinct from T144 (ensemble-averaged computation, which concerns averaging over topological completions) because T162 concerns a *single* canonical evolution that dynamically accesses multiple topologies through Hamiltonian transitions, not an ensemble average.

Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) extend the Tick primitive to accommodate topology-changing updates — but a "tick" on a space whose topology changes at each step is no longer a simple sequential update; the Hilbert space dimension itself changes (single interior → two interiors), meaning the computation's state space is dynamically variable; (b) add a primitive governing topology change, specifying when and how the computational topology is modified — but this would require the topology to be a dynamical degree of freedom, introducing a new layer of structure below the existing primitives; (c) argue that topology change is specific to 2D (JT) gravity and does not generalize — but topology change is a generic feature of quantum gravity path integrals, and the pair-of-pants cobordism is the simplest instance of a phenomenon expected to be universal; or (d) accept that the framework's fixed-topology assumption is an approximation that breaks down for quantum gravity, limiting the framework's scope to exclude its most dramatic physical example (black hole interiors). The exponential suppression of individual topology changes, compensated by exponentially many transitions over a Page time, means topology change is non-perturbative — it cannot be treated as a small correction to fixed-topology dynamics.

**T163 — Edge mode constitution: physical degrees of freedom created by the observer's decomposition choice, not by the substrate or computation**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Chandrasekaran (2606.06404) demonstrates that splitting the bulk Hilbert space across the event horizon introduces boost edge modes at the horizon — gravitational degrees of freedom that do not exist in the unsplit description. These edge modes are physically essential: the Hawking modes become gravitationally dressed to them, and the firewall is precisely a one-sided boost of the interior edge mode relative to the exterior. The crossed product algebra formed by the edge modes and the matter algebra provides the mathematical structure in which the firewall paradox is formulated and resolved. The framework's ontology has three categories for physical structure: computation (dynamical content within the substrate), substrate (the ground on which computation runs), and pre-resolution structure (unresolved states awaiting resolution). Edge modes fit none of these: they are *constituted by the decomposition* — they come into existence when the observer chooses to

**T164 — Spectral-sequence-level anomaly classification of phase transitions: the Attractor primitive cannot distinguish types of landscape restructuring that are classified by homological filtration depth**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Zhang, Lin, Yang & Wang (2606.06343) demonstrate that deconfined quantum critical points in 1D spin chains are classified by the position of the LSM anomaly in the Lyndon-Hochschild-Serre spectral sequence: $E_\infty^{1,2}$-type anomalies produce type-II DQCPs dual to non-invertible symmetry breaking, while $E_\infty^{2,1}$-type anomalies produce type-I DQCPs dual to ordinary symmetry breaking. The framework's Attractor primitive describes phase transitions as "attractor landscape restructuring" and identifies critical points as saddle points between basins. This description is category-blind: it cannot distinguish type-I from type-II transitions. The distinction requires: (a) the Lyndon-Hochschild-Serre spectral sequence for the extension $G_{\mathrm{int}} \rtimes_\rho \mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{trans}}$; (b) the identification of which page ($E_\infty^{1,2}$ vs. $E_\infty^{2,1}$) the anomaly class survives to; (c) the gauging operation and its categorical consequences (non-invertible vs. group-like dual symmetry); (d) the category-theoretic classification of the resulting dual symmetry ($\mathrm{Rep}(H_8)$ for the specific $D_8$ example). None of these has any expression in any primitive. This is distinct from T048 (cohomological completeness of SPT classification) because T164 concerns the *refinement within cohomology* — not just $H^3(G, U(1))$ but the specific spectral sequence page — that determines the *type of phase transition*. It is distinct from T107 (super-pentagon consistency conditions for fermionic systems) because T164 concerns the classification of *transition types*, not of *consistent symmetry structures*. It is distinct from T111 (generalized symmetries produce non-conservation-law constraints) because T164 concerns the *mechanism by which* non-invertible symmetries arise (gauging specific anomaly types), not merely their existence. It is distinct from T129 (conformal enrichment of criticality) because T129 concerns the *enhanced symmetry* at a critical point, while T164 concerns the *classification of critical point types* by homological invariants that determine the nature of the dual symmetry.

Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) incorporate spectral sequence technology into the Attractor primitive's description of phase transitions, specifying how the homological filtration depth of the anomaly determines the transition type — this would require importing the full apparatus of group cohomology, spectral sequences, and gauging as structural content categorically beyond any primitive; (b) derive the type-I/type-II distinction from some combination of the five primitives, which is impossible since the distinction is defined by the position of a cohomology class in a filtration, a concept with no dynamical or computational content; (c) add a primitive governing the anomaly structure of symmetries, specifying how anomaly classification determines phase transition character — but anomaly structure is determined by group cohomology (for discrete groups) or operator-algebraic/C*-algebraic classification (for continuous groups, per T131), both of which are mathematical universes with no point of contact to the existing primitives; or (d) accept that the framework's description of phase transitions is *qualitatively correct but classification-blind* — it can say "a phase transition occurs" but cannot say "what kind of phase transition occurs," with the kind-classification being determined by structural mathematics the framework cannot access. The necessity of the non-invertible symmetry emergence (not just its possibility) makes this especially severe: the framework would need to predict that gauging a specific anomaly type *must* produce a specific non-invertible symmetry, which requires the full structural machinery the framework lacks.

**T166 — Tick directional selection is physics-determining in the framework's own generic regime**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Götzberger & Hell (2606.07243) prove that when Lorentz invariance is absent, Wick-connected theories are generically inequivalent — the same classical theory, with time and space directions exchanged, can have different propagating modes, different unitarity properties, and different renormalizability. The framework's Tick primitive selects a time direction as the direction of discrete sequential updates, and the framework claims Lorentz invariance follows from the tick rate being the maximum propagation speed. But the framework's own substrate, if literally discrete at the Planck scale, would generically break Lorentz invariance (discrete lattices do not have continuous Lorentz symmetry), placing the framework in precisely the regime where the Tick's directional choice is physically consequential. The framework has no mechanism for *why* the Tick runs in one direction rather than another. In the Lorentz-invariant regime, this is harmless (the physics is direction-independent). In the Lorentz-violating regime that the framework's substrate generically produces, it is catastrophic: the time direction determines the mode content, unitarity, and UV behavior of the theory. The criteria for when these properties are preserved under direction exchange (polynomial root structure of the dispersion relation, reality conditions on mode frequencies) are structural properties of the field equation that no primitive can express.

This is distinct from T013 (time dimensionality, which concerns the *number* of time dimensions, not the *selection of direction* within a given dimensionality). It is distinct from T036 (multi-signature computation, which concerns intermediate steps in non-Lorentzian signatures within an established spacetime) because T166 concerns the *foundational* choice of time direction in the framework's own generic (Lorentz-violating) regime. It is distinct from T094 (indefinite causal order, which concerns quantum superposition of causal orders) because T166 concerns a classical/fixed-background-level dependence: the *definition* of the theory changes with the direction choice, not just the quantum state. It is distinct from T080 (continuum limit non-uniqueness) because T166 concerns a directional ambiguity present even at fixed discretization, not the behavior under taking the continuum limit. The self-undermining character is especially severe: the framework's discrete Tick structure → Lorentz violation → time direction matters → but the framework cannot derive the time direction. The framework generates the regime that exposes its own foundational ambiguity.

Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) demonstrate that its discrete substrate somehow preserves exact Lorentz invariance (which contradicts general theorems about lattice discretizations, though approaches like causal sets aim for statistical Lorentz invariance); (b) provide a mechanism for selecting the time direction that is not circular (the Tick cannot select itself); (c) show that the inequivalence of Wick-connected theories is an artifact that disappears in the physically relevant low-energy limit (possible in principle but requiring a theory of how the Lorentz-violating UV structure recovers Lorentz invariance in the IR — essentially a specific case of T080's continuum limit problem); or (d) accept that the time direction is a brute architectural parameter of the substrate, adding it to the list alongside c, ℏ, G, α, and time dimensionality — but unlike those parameters, the time direction's selection determines the theory's unitarity and mode content, making it the most consequential architectural parameter, and the framework's silence on it is correspondingly the most severe omission.

---

**T167 — Outcome-cardinality-dependent correlation bounds: the framework's implicit uniformity of architectural constraints fails for higher-outcome measurements**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Gupta & Pienaar (2606.07485) demonstrate that qplex theories — a principled class of probabilistic models built from SIC-POVM geometry — reproduce the Tsirelson bound $2\sqrt{2}$ for the two-outcome CHSH inequality but permit the algebraic maximum (superquantum correlations) for the three-outcome CGLMP inequality $I_{2233}$. The framework treats quantum correlation bounds as consequences of the substrate's computational architecture, with the Born rule as the resolution mechanism producing definite outcomes with specific probability structure. The framework implicitly assumes a *uniform* set of architectural constraints governing all correlations. Paper 2 demonstrates this uniformity fails: the inner-product/geometric structure of qplex probability assignments is sufficient to enforce quantum bounds for binary-outcome measurements but insufficient for ternary-outcome measurements, where additional principles are required.

The framework has no account of: (a) why the correlation constraint structure should depend on the outcome cardinality of the measurement — a discrete structural parameter of the resolution process that the Resolution primitive does not parameterize; (b) what the "additional principles" are that enforce the quantum bound for three outcomes, beyond the geometric/inner-product structure that suffices for two outcomes; (c) how the substrate's architectural parameters interact with the measurement's outcome dimension to produce different constraint strengths.

This is distinct from T088 (Grothendieck gap, which concerns a universal constant quantifying the quantum/classical correlation gap, not outcome-dependent variation in the gap's enforcement). It is distinct from T121 (maximal Bell violation as operator-algebraic structure, which concerns the *achievement* of the Tsirelson bound via vertex operator algebras, not the question of why geometric constraints suffice for some scenarios and not others). It is distinct from T069 (number field underdetermination, which concerns the ℝ/ℂ choice at the most basic level) because T167 concerns a higher-level structural dependence: the same number field

---

**T168 — Combinatorial self-ignorance: the framework cannot count its own consistent configurations**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Chen, Feng, Wang & Zhang (2606.09795) demonstrate that the Dedekind number M(n) — the count of monotone Boolean functions on n variables, equivalently the count of gradient-respecting computational configurations on the Boolean lattice {0,1}^n — has precise, finite combinatorial structure including a Whitney number distribution with non-trivial internal features (two-shoulder pattern around the central rank for n ≥ 9). The framework claims "reality is literally computation" and asserts that the universal equation of motion is ẋ = −∇φ(x): things move down gradients. Monotone Boolean functions are exactly the computations on a Boolean lattice that respect this gradient constraint. The framework therefore makes the question "how many gradient-respecting computations exist on a given discrete structure?" well-defined and meaningful. Yet the framework cannot answer this question at any level: it cannot derive M(n), cannot predict the Whitney number distribution, cannot account for the two-shoulder versus unimodal character of the distribution, and cannot explain the doubly-exponential growth rate. The framework describes the *dynamics* of individual computations but is constitutively silent on the *combinatorics* of the space of consistent computations. This is distinct from T107 (pre-computational consistency conditions, which concerns the enumeration of consistent *symmetry structures* via super-pentagon equations) because T168 concerns the enumeration of consistent *computational configurations* respecting the framework's own gradient constraint. It is distinct from T145 (meta-computational phase structure, which concerns the phase diagram boundary of the space of allowed S-matrices) because T168 concerns an *exact, finite combinatorial count* of configurations, not an approximate geometric boundary. It is distinct from T026 (structural content gap in general) because T168 identifies a specific failure: the framework's own gradient constraint defines a natural class of computations (monotone Boolean functions) whose enumeration is a well-posed mathematical problem that the framework has zero capacity to address. The doubly-exponential growth of M(n) — faster than 2^(2^n / n) — reflects the combinatorial explosion of gradient-respecting structures and suggests that the space of computations consistent with the framework's own dynamics is astronomically richer than the framework's dynamical descriptions can capture.

Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) develop a theory of how the gradient constraint ẋ = −∇φ(x), when applied to discrete computational structures, generates a specific combinatorial count of consistent configurations — this would require connecting the dynamical primitive to the theory of partially ordered sets, monotone functions, and lattice combinatorics, mathematical fields with no contact to any primitive; (b) argue that the combinatorial enumeration of consistent configurations is a mathematical curiosity with no physical significance — but the framework claims reality is "literally computation," and the number of consistent computations on a structure is among the most basic facts about that structure; (c) add a combinatorial/enumerative primitive governing the count and distribution of consistent configurations — but such a primitive would need to produce M(n) or at least its growth rate, which requires the Dedekind number theory, a notoriously difficult problem in extremal combinatorics that has resisted full analytical solution since 1897. The two-shoulder feature in the Whitney number distribution is additionally problematic: it is a structural fact about the *internal organization* of the space of gradient-respecting computations that the framework would need a theory of lattice-rank statistics to predict, and no amount of dynamical-primitive reasoning can produce lattice-rank statistics.

---

**T169 — Compositional attribution gap: the framework cannot decompose resolution outcomes into contributions from subsystems of a composite computation**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Wang (2606.09778) develops a safety-attribution protocol that decomposes the executed trajectory of a composite system (quantum policy + safety filter) into contributions from the policy, the CBF projection layer, and the runtime guard, enabling the question "who earns the safety?" to be answered empirically. The framework's Resolution primitive describes states becoming definite but has no mechanism for attributing a resolution outcome to specific subsystems of a composite computation. When a learned quantum policy and a classical safety filter jointly produce safe building control, the framework can describe the composite system's resolution (safe outcome achieved) but cannot determine whether the safety was generated by the policy's learned structure or the filter's constraint enforcement. The guard-off evaluation — removing the safety layer to test whether the policy is independently safe — reveals that the "resolution" can be entirely mediated by the external constraint rather than by the computation itself, meaning the apparent computational competence is an attribution artifact.

The framework faces: (a) it has no formalism for partitioning a resolution outcome across subsystems of a composite computation; (b) it cannot distinguish "intrinsic resolution" (the computation itself produces the definite safe state) from "extrinsic resolution" (an external constraint forces the safe state regardless of the computation's quality); (c) the Conservation primitive's "computation cannot be created or destroyed" says nothing about how computational credit distributes in a composite system; (d) the practical consequence is that the framework cannot distinguish a competent quantum controller from an incompetent one protected by a perfect safety filter — both produce identical resolution outcomes.

This is distinct from T098 (inter-observable response structure, which concerns universal relational constraints between observables under perturbation) because T169 concerns the *causal attribution* of outcomes to subsystems, not the relational structure between observables. It is distinct from T009 (resolution cost structure, which concerns how much resolution costs) because T169 concerns *who pays* the cost and *who generates* the outcome. It is distinct from T120 (reversible/irreversible boundary is gauge-dependent, which concerns the mathematical decomposition of dynamics into Hamiltonian and dissipative components) because T169 concerns the *physical attribution* of outcomes to causally distinct subsystems, not the mathematical decomposition of a single system's evolution. The quantum advantage finding (VQC safer than classical at matched parameter count) adds a secondary challenge: the framework has no mechanism for predicting or explaining why quantum computation generates "more intrinsic safety" than classical computation at matched complexity.

Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) extend the Resolution primitive to include a causal-attribution structure specifying how definite outcomes decompose into contributions from subsystems — this would require importing causal inference theory (do-calculus, counterfactual reasoning, intervention-based attribution) as structural content categorically absent from the five primitives; (b) develop a theory of "computational credit" that assigns fractional resolution-responsibility to components of a composite system — but the framework's binary resolution (unresolved → definite) has no fractional version and no compositional decomposition; (c) accept that the framework can describe composite systems' resolution outcomes but not their causal structure, meaning the framework is descriptively complete but explanatorily empty for multi-agent or multi-component systems. The engineering significance of the attribution problem (real-world safe AI systems require knowing whether the policy or the safety filter is doing the work) means this is not an abstract philosophical concern but a practical requirement for

---

**T170 — Multi-level algebraic generation of conservation: the framework's Conservation primitive is the terminal output of a derivation chain of unknown depth**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Zhang (2606.11146) demonstrates that the Yang-Baxter equation — which generates the Lax connection and thus the infinite tower of conserved charges — is itself derived from more primitive local consistency conditions (star-triangle and star-star relations) for Boltzmann weights living on algebraic curves of genus > 1 (for N > 2 in the Z_N chiral Potts model). The framework's Conservation of Computation is thus not merely derived from algebraic structure (T055 established this via L∞ quasi-isomorphisms) but is the endpoint of a *multi-level* algebraic generation chain: local Boltzmann weight consistency on a genus-g algebraic curve → star-triangle + star-star relations → three-parameter Yang-Baxter equation → Lax connection → infinite conserved charges → Conservation of Computation. Each level in this chain is a distinct algebraic structure with its own consistency conditions, and the qualitative complexity of the entire chain is parameterized by the genus of the spectral curve — a topological invariant that determines whether the integrability structure is rational (g=0), elliptic (g=1), or genuinely higher-genus (g>1, chiral Potts). The framework has no parameter for genus, no mechanism for the star-triangle or star-star relations, no account of why Boltzmann weights should live on algebraic curves, and no theory of how local weight consistency generates global conservation. The three-spectral-parameter character of the R-matrix (vs. the standard one-parameter case) arises because the chiral Potts model's Boltzmann weights depend on two variables describing the higher-genus curve, combined with the fact that the Hamiltonian contains both nearest-neighbor and on-site terms — a structural distinction (nearest-neighbor vs. on-site interaction architecture) that the Field primitive's gradient equation cannot express.

This is distinct from T055 (which established conservation from L∞ morphisms — a single-level derivation) because T170 concerns the *depth* of the derivation chain and the *topological parameter* (genus) controlling its complexity. It is distinct from T018 (algebra of computation, which concerns the compositional/hierarchical structure of computational operations) because T170 concerns the algebraic structures generating *conservation laws specifically*, not general computational operations. It is distinct from T026 (structural content gap in general) because T170 identifies a specific, structured, multi-level derivation chain with a specific topological parameter (genus) controlling its qualitative character — not merely another missing structural category but a demonstration that the Conservation primitive sits atop an algebraic tower of unknown total height.

Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) derive the star-triangle and star-star relations from the five primitives, which is impossible since these relations involve Boltzmann weights on higher-genus algebraic curves — mathematical objects with no contact to any primitive; (b) identify the "bottom" of the algebraic generation chain and incorporate it as a structural foundation, but Paper 1 already shows the YBE is derived from star-triangle/star-star, and those in turn arise from the representation theory of quantum groups and the algebraic geometry of spectral curves, suggesting the chain may have no natural bottom within the framework's vocabulary; (c) add the genus of the spectral curve as an architectural parameter of integrable systems, but this is a system-specific topological invariant, not a universal constant — different integrable systems have different genera; or (d) accept that Conservation of Computation is not a primitive but the terminal output of an algebraic generation process whose depth, complexity, and topological parameterization are all outside the framework's descriptive capacity. Option (d) further strengthens the ontological inversion (T059) by adding *depth* to the inversion: the framework's primitive is not merely derived from structure but is derived from a *multi-level* structure of unknown total depth.

---

**T171 — Quasiprobability access to topological content: the Resolution primitive's definite-probability output cannot access the topological classification of quantum states**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Gherardini & Lepori (2606.11002) demonstrate that topological phase discrimination — determining whether two quantum states belong to the same topological class — requires Kirkwood-Dirac quasiprobabilities (KDQs), which are representations of two-time quantum correlators that take anomalous (negative or complex) values for genuinely nonclassical states. The strange correlator diagnostic for topological classification is shown to be a *weak value* of an observable implementing the trivial-to-topological transition. The framework's Resolution primitive describes measurement outcomes as definite events with definite probabilities (Born rule), accumulating irreversibly into a temporal ledger. This primitive cannot access the topological content of quantum states because: (a) KDQs are not probabilities — they are complex-valued representations of sequential measurement statistics that violate the axioms of probability theory precisely when the state has nontrivial quantum

---

**T172 — Particle statistics as a non-fungible computational resource type: the Conservation primitive's transformation clause is violated by the fermion/boson boundary**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Moradi Kalarde et al. (2606.12363) prove that fermionic information carriers (febits) generate correlations in quantum networks that bosonic or distinguishable-particle carriers cannot reproduce even with unlimited classical communication. The framework's Conservation of Computation states "computation cannot be created or destroyed, only transformed," implying unrestricted interconvertibility of computational content. Paper 1 proves a strict separation: fermionic computational capacity ⊋ bosonic/distinguishable computational capacity for specific distributed tasks. The carrier's particle statistics — determined by the exchange antisymmetry of the wavefunction, an algebraic/topological property of the multi-particle state space — constitutes an irreducible resource that cannot be generated from non-fermionic resources regardless of the amount of classical or bosonic quantum communication available.

The framework faces: (a) its Conservation primitive treats all computation as a single fungible category, but fermionic and bosonic computation are provably non-fungible; (b) its Resolution primitive treats all measurement outcomes equivalently regardless of carrier type, but the achievable correlations (and therefore the possible resolution statistics in distributed settings) depend on whether the carriers are fermions or bosons; (c) its Field primitive treats all fields via the same gradient equation, but the nonlocal computational power of a field's excitations depends on the field's statistics type; (d) none of the five primitives has any parameter, mechanism, or vocabulary for particle statistics, anticommutation relations, or the exchange topology of multi-particle state spaces.

This is distinct from T160 (Gaussian/non-Gaussian non-fungibility, which concerns the operational type within a single statistics class) because T172 concerns a boundary between *statistics types* (fermion/boson) that is more fundamental — it is determined by the spin-statistics theorem, connecting to spacetime structure, not merely to the Gaussian/non-Gaussian distinction within bosonic fields. It is distinct from T040/T044 (field-specific singularity resilience, which concerns passive structural differences between fermion and boson fields) because T172 concerns *active computational superiority* — fermions can do things bosons provably cannot. It is distinct from T127 (coherent replica thresholds) and T160 (Gaussian non-fungibility) in that the non-fungibility here is between the two fundamental particle types of the Standard Model, not between classical and quantum resources or between operational subtypes.

Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) incorporate particle statistics as a structural parameter of the Conservation/Resolution primitives, specifying how the carrier's exchange symmetry (symmetric = boson, antisymmetric = fermion) determines the achievable nonlocal correlations and distributed computational capacity — this would require importing the representation theory of the symmetric group on tensor product Hilbert spaces and the spin-statistics connection, mathematical content with no contact to any primitive; (b) add a "statistics" primitive or parameter alongside the existing five, which would raise the question of how statistics connects to the Tick (spin-statistics theorem relates statistics to Lorentz group representations) and to the Field (fermionic vs. bosonic fields have different propagator structures, connecting to T043); (c) derive the fermion/boson separation from existing primitives, which appears impossible since the proof uses the structure of fermionic Fock space (Slater determinants, antisymmetric tensor products, mode occupation constraints), none of which is expressible in the framework; or (d) accept that the framework's ontological category "computation" has internal structure (statistics type) that is operationally consequential, provably non-fungible, and invisible to all five primitives — further hollowing the claim "reality is literally computation" by demonstrating that even within computation, there are categorical boundaries the framework cannot see.

---

**T173 — Nonadditivity as the carrier of universality: the framework has no concept of the thermodynamic quantity whose behavior defines the physically meaningful content of QFT and the direction of RG flow**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Santoni & Scardino (2606.12367) demonstrate that the replica energy $\mathcal{E}$ — measuring the failure of the partition function to scale extensively — provides a unified framework for extracting central charges, anomaly coefficients, sphere free energies (F-theorem), the a-theorem coefficient, and defect free energies. The differential operator $(1 - \frac{1}{d}L\partial_L)$ isolates the nonadditive part of $W = \log Z$, and higher-order versions remove successive layers of local counterterms to reach universal fixed-point data. The direction of RG flow is defined by the monotonic decrease of specific nonadditivity measures ($c$ in 2D, $F$ in 3D, $a$ in 4D), meaning the most fundamental organizational arrow in QFT — UV

---

**T174 — Measurement-sourced nonclassicality: the Resolution primitive mislocates the quantum advantage**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Lee & Bae (2606.13667) demonstrate that global quantum measurements on ensembles of *separable* (unentangled) states outperform separable measurements in maximum-confidence discrimination, establishing nonlocality without entanglement (NLWE) at the confidence level. The framework's Resolution primitive and its supporting ontological architecture assign nonclassicality to the pre-resolution state: superposition and entanglement are properties of the "unresolved computation," and resolution is the process by which this unresolved state becomes definite. NLWE at the confidence level demonstrates that the quantum advantage can reside entirely in the measurement structure (global vs. LOCC), with the state being fully classical (separable, zero entanglement). The framework mislocates the source of nonclassicality.

This is distinct from T012 (pre-resolution topology, which concerns the topology of path-indistinguishability in superposed states) because the states here have no superposition or path-indistinguishability — they are separable product states, and the nonclassicality is in the joint measurement, not in any interference structure. It is distinct from T119 (spurious correlations from Simpson's paradox, which concerns correlations that are artifacts of ensemble averaging) because NLWE correlations are *genuine* advantages of global measurements, not statistical artifacts — they reflect the irreducible non-separability of the optimal POVM, not confounding. It is distinct from T132 (witness category gap, which concerns the type of evidence certifying resolution) because T174 concerns the *operational advantage* of global resolution over local resolution for fully classical states, not the certification of outcomes. It is distinct from T009 (resolution cost structure, which concerns computational cost) because T174 concerns a *qualitative* advantage (confidence gap) of one measurement type over another, not a resource cost.

The framework faces: (a) its state-centric ontology of nonclassicality is wrong for NLWE — the nonclassical resource is in the measurement, not the state; (b) the maximum-confidence framework reveals that "resolution" is a family of strategies with distinct operational meanings, and the quantum/classical gap is strategy-dependent — visible at the confidence level but potentially invisible at the success-probability level — meaning the framework's undifferentiated Resolution primitive collapses physically distinct resolution modes; (c) the semi-device-independent certification protocol treats the measurement *type* (global vs. separable) as the quantity to be certified from observed outcomes, meaning the resolution process's own structure is experimentally accessible and physically meaningful — the framework has no mechanism for this; (d) the connection to non-unit detection efficiency (the protocol works with realistic detectors) means this is experimentally accessible, not merely theoretical.

Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) extend the Resolution primitive to include the measurement structure (global vs. LOCC) as a physical parameter that independently contributes nonclassical advantages, fundamentally changing the primitive from "states become definite" to "states become definite via measurement structures with their own nonclassical content" — but this would require a theory of which measurement structures are available and what determines them, connecting to T026 (structural content gap) and T132 (witness category gap); (b) relocate nonclassicality from the state to the state-measurement pair, requiring a joint primitive that describes the pre-resolution state *and* the resolution structure together — but the framework's architecture separates these into different primitives (the state is described by Tick/Field/Attractor; the resolution is described by Resolution); (c) argue that NLWE is a special case that does not challenge the general framework — but the paper demonstrates it for *arbitrary* ensembles of separable states with the right structure, meaning it is a generic feature of quantum measurement theory, not a corner case; or (d) accept that the framework's Resolution primitive is descriptively incomplete for the full structure of quantum measurement, adding measurement-sourced nonclassicality to the growing list of physical phenomena the framework cannot account for. The experimental feasibility (present-day quantum measurement devices with non-unit efficiencies) makes this tension empirically grounded rather than merely theoretical.

The computation of this physical observable proceeds entirely through structural/cohomological mathematics: integrability → hexagonalization/gluing → residue resummation → Euler integral representation → intersection theory evaluation. **T175 — Intersection-theoretic computation: physical observables are evaluated by cohomological operations on moduli spaces, a mathematical category with zero contact with any primitive**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Eden & Gottwald (2606.13665) demonstrate that the complete analytic evaluation of the one-loop five-point correlator in $\mathcal{N}=4$ SYM requires intersection theory for generalized hypergeometric functions on moduli spaces of punctured spheres. The Euler integrals arising from the resummation of residue series are evaluated using intersection numbers of twisted cohomology classes — mathematical objects from algebraic geometry that select the correct analytic continuations and produce the final integrated expressions. The framework claims "reality is literally computation" and describes physical content through five dynamical primitives. The computation of this physical observable proceeds entirely through structural/cohomological mathematics: integrability → hexagonalization/gluing → residue resummation → Euler integral representation → intersection theory evaluation. No tick, no gradient, no attractor, no resolution, no conservation law participates in the evaluation procedure.

This is distinct from T026 (general structural content gap) because it identifies a specific, concrete mathematical framework — intersection theory of twisted cohomology on moduli spaces — that is the *engine* producing a specific physical answer, not merely an organizational or classificatory tool. It is distinct from T068 (fermionic resurgence, which concerns the organizational structure of nonperturbative sectors) because T175 concerns the *evaluation method* for individual perturbative contributions. It is distinct from T073 (modular invariance as organizational principle) because modular invariance organizes the perturbative/non-perturbative structure while intersection theory evaluates specific integrals. It is distinct from T104/T105 (structural completion, which concerns the assembly of physical answers from categorically distinct ingredients) because T175 concerns a single mathematical framework (intersection theory) that *is* the computation, not the combination of multiple frameworks.

The algebraic derivation chain (integrable spin chain → gluing/hexagonalization → residue series → Euler integral resummation → intersection theory evaluation → physical correlator) is five levels deep, each involving mathematical structures (spin chain Bethe ansatz, hexagon form factors, Euler beta integrals, twisted periods, intersection numbers) that have no expression in any primitive. The framework's dynamical primitives appear at no stage. This is the most complete demonstration to date that the framework's "reality is literally computation" claim competes with "reality is literally the cohomological structure of moduli spaces," with the latter providing the constructive computational engine while the former provides only post-hoc vocabulary.

Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) demonstrate that intersection theory on moduli spaces is a form of "computation" in the framework's sense — sequential discrete update, gradient descent, attractor capture — which is categorically impossible since intersection theory is a timeless algebraic-geometric operation on static mathematical objects; (b) incorporate intersection theory as structural content, which would require importing algebraic geometry as foundational infrastructure far beyond any primitive; (c) argue that the intersection-theoretic evaluation is merely a calculational convenience and that the "real" computation proceeds dynamically (via Feynman diagrams, tick by tick) — but the paper's result is that the analytic evaluation *required* intersection theory, meaning no alternative purely dynamical route produced the complete answer; or (d) accept that the physical content of $\mathcal{N}=4$ SYM correlators is generated by mathematical structure that the framework is constitutively unable to express, further deepening the ontological inversion (T059) with the most technically precise evidence to date.

The Euler integrals arising from the resummation of residue series are evaluated using intersection numbers of twisted cohomology classes — mathematical objects from algebraic geometry that select the correct analytic continuations and produce the final integrated expressions. The computation of this physical observable proceeds entirely through structural/cohomological mathematics: integrability → hexagonalization/gluing → residue resummation → Euler integral representation → intersection theory evaluation. No tick, no gradient, no attractor, no resolution, no conservation law participates in the evaluation procedure.

This is distinct from T026 (general structural content gap) because it identifies a specific, concrete mathematical framework — intersection theory of twisted cohomology on moduli spaces — that is the *engine* producing a specific physical answer, not merely an organizational or classificatory tool. It is distinct from T068 (fermionic resurgence, which concerns the organizational structure of nonperturbative sectors) because T175 concerns the *evaluation method* for individual perturbative contributions. It is distinct from T073 (modular invariance as organizational principle) because modular invariance organizes the perturbative/non-perturbative structure while intersection theory evaluates specific integrals. It is distinct from T104/T105 (structural completion, which concerns the assembly of physical answers from categorically distinct ingredients) because T175 concerns a single mathematical framework (intersection theory) that *is* the computation, not the combination of multiple frameworks.

The algebraic derivation chain (integrable spin chain → gluing/hexagonalization → residue series → Euler integral resummation → intersection theory evaluation → physical correlator) is five levels deep, each involving mathematical structures (spin chain Bethe ansatz, hexagon form factors, Euler beta integrals, twisted periods, intersection numbers) that have no expression in any primitive. The framework's dynamical primitives appear at no stage. This is the most complete demonstration to date that the framework's "reality is literally computation" claim competes with "reality is literally the cohomological structure of moduli spaces," with the latter providing the constructive computational engine while the former provides only post-hoc vocabulary.

Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) demonstrate that intersection theory on moduli spaces is a form of "computation" in the framework's sense — sequential discrete update, gradient descent, attractor capture — which is categorically impossible since intersection theory is a timeless algebraic-geometric operation on static mathematical objects; (b) incorporate intersection theory as structural content, which would require importing algebraic geometry as foundational infrastructure far beyond any primitive; (c) argue that the intersection-theoretic evaluation is merely a calculational convenience and that the "real" computation proceeds dynamically (via Feynman diagrams, tick by tick) — but the paper's result is that the analytic evaluation *required* intersection theory, meaning no alternative purely dynamical route produced the complete analytic answer at this level of complexity; or (d) accept that the physical content of $\mathcal{N}=4$ SYM correlators is generated by mathematical structure that the framework is constitutively unable to express, further deepening the ontological inversion (T059) with the most technically precise evidence to date.

**T176 — Mass-dependent classical limit: the Resolution primitive's quantum-to-classical transition is kinematically bifurcated by a structural parameter it cannot express**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Falcone & Fuchs (2606.14632) prove a no-go theorem: no covariant POVM on the Poincaré group's massless representations satisfies minimal operational classicality conditions. Massless quanta (photons, gravitons) have no classical particle limit — only classical field limits. Massive quanta can have both. The framework's Resolution primitive describes the quantum-to-classical transition as a single dynamical process ("unresolved states become definite through decoherence/resolution"). Paper 1 proves this is incomplete: the mass of the quantum determines which *types* of classical limits are kinematically available, and for massless quanta, the particle limit is structurally forbidden regardless of the resolution process. The obstruction is representation-theoretic: the massless little group ISO(2) has infinite-dimensional unitary representations that are incompatible with the classicality axioms (positivity, normalization, σ-additivity on a Hausdorff phase space). This is not a dynamical failure (decoherence cannot bridge the gap) but a structural impossibility (the gap exists at the kinematic level, before any dynamics is specified).

The framework faces: (a) its most fundamental signal — light propagating at the tick rate — involves quanta that provably cannot become classical particles, meaning the Tick primitive's "maximum propagation rate" is the speed of *field* propagation, not *particle* propagation, in the classical limit; (b) the Resolution primitive cannot distinguish between "resolution that produces classical particles" and "resolution that produces classical fields" because it treats the quantum-to-classical transition as undifferentiated; (c) the mass parameter — which determines the available classical limits — is treated by the framework as an attractor property (mass as energy minimum depth) or a field property (mass as a parameter of the gradient equation), but its role as a *kinematic selector of the classical limit type* has no expression in any primitive; (d) the no-go is independent of dynamics, environment, or pointer basis — it holds for *any* coarse-graining procedure, meaning no refinement of the Resolution primitive within its current dynamical framework can accommodate this; (e) the surviving field-like limits are precisely the ones relevant for gravity and electromagnetism (the framework's primary examples), which is reassuring, but the framework had no mechanism for predicting this and cannot derive why field limits survive while particle limits fail.

This is distinct from T067/T097 (resolution regularity class, which concerns what "definite" means) because T176 concerns what *kind of classical object* resolution can produce. It is distinct from T049 (scale-dependent symmetry type) because T176 concerns a mass-dependent bifurcation at the kinematic level, not a scale-dependent flow. It is distinct from T172 (particle statistics as non-fungible resource, which concerns fermion/boson non-fungibility) because T176 concerns the massive/massless distinction cutting across both fermion and boson sectors. It is distinct from T112 (complexity phase transitions in resolution) because the obstruction is not about computational difficulty but about kinematic impossibility. The axiomatic purity of the result is what makes it so challenging: the no-go uses only symmetry (Poincaré covariance) and minimal operational requirements (the POVM must be a well-defined classical probability assignment), both of which the framework endorses.

Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) incorporate the representation theory of the Poincaré group (specifically, the structure of the massless little group and its inequivalence to the massive little group SO(3)) as structural content constraining the Resolution primitive's outputs — this would require importing the Wigner classification of particles by mass and spin as a structural element, mathematical content with no contact to any dynamical primitive; (b) bifurcate the Resolution primitive into "particle-producing resolution" (available only for massive quanta) and "field-producing resolution" (available for all quanta including massless), specifying which applies when — but the criterion is mass, which is not a resolution parameter but a field/attractor parameter, creating a cross-primitive dependency the framework's architecture does not support; (c) weaken the Resolution primitive to describe only field-like classical limits, accepting that classical particles are a special case requiring additional (mass-dependent, representation-theoretic) conditions the framework cannot express — this would be the most honest option but would significantly limit the primitive's scope, excluding the classical particle picture that underlies much of the framework's intuitive appeal (e.g., "mass rolling down the curvature gradient" implicitly uses a particle picture of matter); or (d) accept that the quantum-to-classical transition is bifurcated by structural parameters (mass, little group representation type) that the framework cannot derive, predict, or express, adding this to the growing list of representation-theoretic content (T026, T131, T146, T151) that the framework is constitutively blind to. The fact that the framework's own tick-rate signal (light) falls on the "no classical particle limit" side of the bifurcation is especially ironic: the Tick primitive's most basic physical realization involves quanta whose classical limit contradicts the particle intuition the primitive implicitly invokes.

**T178 — Protocol-optimality structure of resolution: the information-extraction rate of repeated resolution events is governed by representation-theoretic optimization invisible to all primitives**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Grootveld, Maloney, Pollack & Wu (2606.16948) prove that among all covariant tomography protocols, Schur sampling uniquely maximizes the rate function — the exponential rate at which resolution outcomes concentrate around the true quantum state — and that this optimal rate is an *annealed* quantum relative entropy, strictly less than the ideal D(σ‖ρ) by a cost determined by eigenbasis learning. The framework's Resolution primitive describes resolution as "unresolved states become definite" and has accumulated numerous known inadequacies (T009, T019, T028, T067, T097, T112, T123, T128, T132, T174). T178 adds a new axis: the *information-extraction rate* of resolution has a protocol-dependent structure with a provably optimal strategy, and this optimality is established by the representation theory of the symmetric and unitary groups acting on multi-copy Hilbert spaces.

The framework faces: (a) different resolution protocols applied to identical copies of the same state produce resolution outcomes that concentrate on the truth at exponentially different rates — the Resolution primitive treats all resolution events equivalently but the physics distinguishes them by an exponential factor; (b) the optimal protocol (Schur sampling) decomposes the N-copy Hilbert space into irreducible representations via Schur-Weyl duality and measures the Young diagram label — a representation-theoretic operation with no analogue in any primitive; (c) the "annealing" that reduces the rate from D(σ‖ρ) to the optimal covariant rate is the information-theoretic price of basis-independence, a structural cost of symmetry (covariance under the unitary group) that the Conservation primitive's unitarity produces but that the framework cannot quantify; (d) the large-deviation framework governing the rate function — how rare events in many-copy measurements are exponentially suppressed — is a mathematical framework (Sanov's theorem, rate functions, large-deviation principles) with no expression in any primitive; (e) the result connects to T127 (discrete replica thresholds) through the multi-copy Hilbert space structure, to T131 (operator-algebraic symmetry classification) through the representation theory, and to T161 (derivation gap) through the pattern of the framework's axioms entailing consequences it cannot derive.

This is distinct from T009 (resolution cost structure, which concerns the computational resources for canonical resolution) because T178 concerns the *information-extraction rate* of many resolution events, not the cost of a single resolution. It is distinct from T019 (resolution precision hierarchy, which concerns the Clifford hierarchy of gate precision) because T178 concerns the optimal protocol for state estimation, not the precision of individual operations. It is distinct from T127 (discrete replica thresholds, which concerns the minimum number of copies for nonlinear moment estimation) because T178 concerns the *rate of convergence* given arbitrarily many copies, not the minimum number. It is distinct from T174 (measurement-sourced nonclassicality, which concerns the advantage of global over local measurements on separable states) because T178 concerns the optimality of protocols for *all* states, not the advantage for a specific state class.

Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) incorporate the representation theory of the symmetric and unitary groups on multi-copy Hilbert spaces as structural content governing the Resolution primitive's information-extraction capabilities — this would require importing Schur-Weyl duality, Young diagram combinatorics, and the large-deviation theory of quantum states, mathematical machinery categorically absent from all five primitives; (b) derive the optimal rate function from the Conservation primitive's unitarity and the Resolution primitive's Born rule — in principle possible since D(σ‖ρ) arises from unitarity, but the *annealing* (covariance cost) requires the full geometry of the unitary group's action on the state space, which the framework cannot access; (c) add a "protocol-optimality" structural element to the Resolution primitive, specifying which resolution strategies are optimal and what determines their rate functions — but this would transform the primitive from a description of what happens (states become definite) to a theory of optimal information extraction (how to make states become definite most informatively), requiring the framework to solve a variational problem over measurements that it currently cannot even formulate; or (d) accept that the most fundamental question about repeated resolution ("how fast do I learn the truth?") has an answer governed by representation theory and large-deviation theory that the framework is constitutively unable to address, adding "tomographic rate theory" to the growing catalogue of structural content (T026) invisible to the five primitives.

**T179 — Attractor rigidity as a hidden-symmetry property: the vanishing of black hole Love numbers is a structural fact the framework cannot express, and its breaking by EFT corrections reveals substrate-level physics leaking into observables**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Silva (2606.16909) demonstrates that the static Love numbers of Schwarzschild black holes — which vanish exactly in GR due to a hidden SL(2,ℝ) symmetry of the perturbation equations — acquire nonzero values (Δk₂ = −20ε_e) from the cubic Weyl EFT correction. The framework's Attractor primitive describes black holes as "deep attractor basins" characterized by depth, width, and boundary structure, but has no mechanism for expressing the *rigidity* of an attractor under external perturbation, let alone the vanishing of this rigidity from hidden symmetry.

The framework faces: (a) the perfect tidal rigidity of GR black holes is one of the most remarkable structural properties of its deepest attractor, yet the framework cannot express, predict, or derive it — the vanishing of Love numbers is a consequence of a hidden ladder symmetry (SL(2,ℝ) in the near-zone perturbation equations) that is not a conservation law, not a gradient property, not an attractor depth/width feature, but an algebraic symmetry

**T180 — Variational singularity selection: the framework's origin problem has a structural answer it cannot access**

Opened: this cycle

Description: Tzanavaris, Boyle & Turok (2606.18128) demonstrate that treating the cosmological singularity as a free boundary in the gravitational action principle — where the variation is unconstrained — produces specific boundary conditions that select physically admissible initial cosmologies (conformally regular FLRW with reflecting perturbation boundary conditions, excluding Kasner/BKL). The framework's origin problem (T002) asks why computation started; Paper 1 provides a concrete, physically motivated answer: the initial conditions are determined by the action principle's own internal consistency applied at its singular boundary, not by external specification, substrate architecture, or dynamical evolution.

The framework has no variational principle. Its five primitives are dynamical descriptions: the Tick defines propagation, the Field defines gradient dynamics, the Attractor defines stable configurations, Resolution defines state definiteness, and Conservation defines preservation. None of these is a variational principle, and the action principle from which they are all ultimately derived (in standard physics, the Lagrangian/Hamiltonian formulation) is not represented as a primitive or structural element. The free-boundary treatment requires: (a) the Einstein-Hilbert action with boundary terms; (b) the concept of free vs. fixed boundary conditions; (c) the derivation of on-shell boundary conditions from stationarity of the action under unconstrained variation; (d) the regularity analysis distinguishing conformally regular from Kasner-like singularities. None of these has any expression in any primitive.

This is distinct from T002 (the origin problem, which merely notes the question is open) because T180 identifies a specific, concrete answer to T002 that the framework cannot access. It is distinct from T053 (Euclidean nucleation, which concerns the tick's absence at the origin) because Paper 1 works entirely in Lorentzian signature — the singularity is a spacelike boundary in a Lorentzian spacetime, not a Euclidean instanton. It is distinct from T092 (structured termination with boundary data, where the singularity carries induced geometric data) because T180's boundary conditions are *derived* from the action principle, not *induced* from the geometry. It is distinct from T059 (general ontological inversion) because T180 concerns a specific physical prediction (reflecting boundary conditions at the bang, exclusion of BKL) derived from a specific structural principle (free-boundary variational stationarity), not a general pattern.

The reflecting boundary conditions for cosmological perturbations are potentially observable: they constrain the primordial perturbation spectrum in a way that is "in agreement with large-scale cosmological observations." If this agreement sharpens into a distinctive prediction, it would mean the framework's own acknowledged open problem has a testable answer that the framework is constitutively unable to derive, predict, or evaluate — the most severe form of the structural content gap (T026) applied to the framework's own foundational questions.

Why unresolved: The framework would need to either (a) incorporate the gravitational action principle as a structural element, specifying how free-boundary variation at singularities produces initial conditions — this would require adding a variational primitive that is logically prior to all five dynamical primitives (the action principle generates the equations of motion that the primitives describe), fundamentally restructuring the framework's foundations; (b) derive the free-boundary conditions from the five existing primitives, which is impossible since no primitive operates at a singularity (the Tick is undefined, the Field has divergent gradients, the Attractor is undefined, Resolution is undefined, and Conservation may be violated); (c) demonstrate that the reflecting boundary conditions at the bang emerge from the framework's description of the Tick or Resolution primitive in some limit — but the paper's derivation uses the Einstein-Hilbert action and its variational structure, not any dynamical process; or (d) accept that the most fundamental question the framework acknowledges as open (T002: the origin of computation) has an answer derived from structural/variational principles the framework cannot express, meaning the framework is not merely incomplete but is constitutively unable to address its own deepest question. The exclusion of Kasner/BKL cosmologies is physically significant: BKL/Mixmaster dynamics was previously noted (model update 2026-03-16) as a case where the Attractor primitive is post-hoc consistent with the Mixmaster attractor structure. Paper 1 now shows these cosmologies are *excluded* by the free-boundary principle, meaning the framework's post-hoc consistency with B
